# Read-Through: Test Suite

**Status:** active -- running record, appended as the read-through progresses.

**Scope:** the M3.1 task "Test-suite read-through and mechanism documentation (operator onboarding)". Phase 0 (complexity audit) and phase 1 (mechanism write-up) are done. This report covers phases 2-4: phase 2 the small `src/libs/*` units, phase 3 `src/build/` and `src/capability/`, phase 4 the `make`-workflow drivers.

**Purpose:** knowledge transfer. The operator reads; the agent supplies the source annotation and the BDD framing, runs the bite checks, and takes questions. The report is the single artifact; anything scheduled later comes only from findings recorded here.

## Format (per file, going forward)

Out of scope by operator direction: the compose YAML and the capability dockerfile (phase 3), and `scripts/macos_bootstrap.sh`, `scripts/manual/*.sh`, and the seven `check_*.sh` gates in phase 4. These are not part of the main tooling. Their file-level detail is not recorded here; where a finding touches one of them, it is recorded on the row that owns the behaviour.

1. **Chat - annotated source.** Present the subject source in labelled chunks, each chunk followed by what it does. Call out dead code, redundant guards, and behavioural consequences.
2. **Chat - units in BDD terms.** For every `test_*` unit: Given / When / Then, plus a one-line "what it is supposed to test".
3. **Chat - bite checks by mutation.** Mutate the subject (a targeted line at a time), run the file, and record which units turn red. A mutation that fails the intended unit proves the assertion bites; a mutation that leaves the suite green is a coverage finding. Restore the subject from a `/tmp` backup after each mutation. Present the results alongside the findings. One trap has cost a bite already: appending `&& false` to an `[[ ]]` condition is not a mutation, because inside `[[ ]]` the second operand is a non-empty string and the condition stays true. Disable a guard with `if false; then`, and confirm the mutant file differs from its backup before running the suite.
4. **Test file - write the BDD block back.** Above each `test_*` function, add the comment block:

```bash
# Given: <the precondition>
# When:  <the action>
# Then:  <the observable outcome>
# Asserts: <what the unit is supposed to test>
```

5. **This report - record the units, the bite results, and the findings.** Per file: a short subject note, the BDD unit table with a Bite column, a Bite checks list, and file-specific notes. Cross-file findings go to the Findings log below.
6. **Devlog - keep the feedback.** Every harness, bash, or methodology lesson this pass raises is recorded in `devlog/AGENT_FEEDBACK.md` in that file's own format, and the report says so. Grep the file for an existing entry on the topic first: a recurrence is recorded on that entry (re-open it and note the prior fix in `legacy:`) rather than as a new entry. A lesson that lives only in chat, or only in this report's Progress bullets, is not kept.

**Machine-readable companion.** The findings in this document pair with `20260924-design-active-test_suite_readthrough.jsonl`, one JSON object per line and one line per finding row: `id`, `title`, `sector`, `class`, `action`, `action_text`, `action_kind`, `files`, `status`, `refs`. The prose here is the record of reasoning; the data file is the record of labels, and a label lives in exactly one of them. Counts are read from the data file and never asserted in prose, because a stated count drifts from the thing it counts: this document's own close-out reported 46 test files against 49, 16 consolidation clusters against 31, and 49 note rows against 50. Query the data file with `jq` once that lands, or with the perl core module today. The format decision and the schema table are in [`20260925-design-draft-findings_register_format.md`](20260925-design-draft-findings_register_format.md).

Bite vocabulary: **proven** = a mutation was run and the named unit failed; **survived** = a mutation was run and no unit failed (a coverage finding); **read-assessed** = no mutation run; **probe-verified** = no unit exists for the behaviour, so it was observed directly in a throwaway harness - documented but unguarded, and every such observation is itself a coverage finding.

Numbering: a row is a stable identifier and is never renumbered once another document cites it. Consolidation happens by grouping (see the Consolidation map), not by relabelling, and a group's single disposition lives in the document that works the finding. Before a new row is numbered, grep the findings table for the finding rather than for its class: a defect an earlier file's pass already recorded keeps that row as the canonical entry, and the new pass either records its evidence on the existing row or states on the new row that it is the same finding - as row 242 does for row 38 - and joins the same group. Two rows for one finding cost a duplicate disposition and a second fix in the plan.

Assertion vocabulary: a behaviour is **pinned** when a unit fails if that behaviour is changed - the same observation as a `proven` mutation, stated from the assertion's side; **unpinned** is the state behind a `survived` mutation. Pinning is good when the behaviour is intended contract (a regression guard) and adverse when it freezes an accident that a later correct fix must break. Two intermediate states recur and are named at the unit: a **vacuous** unit exercises a path without pinning any behaviour inside it, and an **over-broad** assertion pins an outcome without its reason (a return code without the diagnostic, say). The repository's own rule for the second state is `docs/development/testing-conventions.md`, "Assert the meaning, not the string".

## Phase 2 -- `src/libs/*`

### `src/libs/env.sh` -- `tests/test_env.sh`

Subject: `env_load FILE`, the single loader for simple `KEY=VALUE` `.env` files. Its two consumers are the Makefile `-include` (make variables) and the runtime export. Behaviour to hold onto: no inline-comment rule (`FOO=bar # note` keeps the note in the value); a later duplicate key overwrites an earlier one; a missing file is a silent no-op; a non-identifier key warns and is skipped without aborting the file; the loop reads the file, so the caller's stdin is untouched.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_env_load_exports_valid_line` | Given a `.env` file containing `FOO=bar`, when `env_load` is called on it, then `FOO` holds `bar` | the happy path - one simple assignment reaches the environment (same-shell read; exportedness is not asserted) | proven (assignment no-op fails it) |
| `test_env_load_skips_comments_and_blanks` | Given `# c`, a blank line, a whitespace-only line, a tab-indented `# tab`, and `K=ok`, when loaded, then `K` is `ok` and no variable `c` exists | comment and blank lines are inert; comment text never becomes a variable (warning-freedom is not asserted) | proven only for the load path (assignment no-op fails it); comment-guard mutation survived (finding 5) |
| `test_env_load_trims_value_padding` | Given `V=` with a space-padded value, when loaded, then `V` is exactly `padded` | surrounding whitespace on the value is removed | proven (deleting the leading-trim line fails it) |
| `test_env_load_skips_invalid_key_with_warning` | Given `1BAD=odd` then `GOOD=1`, when loaded with stderr captured, then the output contains "invalid variable name" and `GOOD=1` still applied | a non-identifier key is skipped with a warning and the loader continues | proven (warning-text mutation fails it) |

Bite checks (each mutation run against `tests/test_env.sh`, subject restored after):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `export "$KEY=$VALUE"` to a no-op | 1, 2, 3, 4 | the assignment is the load path all four units depend on |
| delete the leading-trim line | 3 | unit 3 pins leading-space trim; the trailing-trim line alone does not satisfy it |
| warning text `invalid variable name` to `INVALID variable name` | 4 | unit 4 pins the warning text |
| comment guard `^#.*$` to `^@@.*$` | none | **survived** - no unit asserts comment lines are warning-free (finding 5) |
| `export` to `printf -v "$KEY" "%s" "$VALUE"` | none | **survived** - no unit checks that a loaded value reaches a child process (finding 6) |

### `src/libs/env_resolve.sh` -- `tests/test_env_resolve.sh`

Subject: the precedence resolver for the identity triple (`PROJECT_NAME`, `PROJECT_DIR`, `SANDBOX_DIR`). Precedence per identifier, highest wins: explicit flag, then the `AGENT_SANDBOX_<KEY>` host env var, then the `.env` file, else a hard error pointing at `agent-sandbox onboard`. `default_env_file` locates the `.env`: an absolute `--env` value as-is, a relative value as a name under the sandbox dir, an empty value as `<sandbox>/.env`, else the invocation CWD's `.env`. `SANDBOX` is resolved first, because a relative `--env` needs a sandbox that may itself live in the `.env`. The two-level guard reads only the `AGENT_SANDBOX_*` keys, so a leaked plain `PROJECT_NAME` export does not bypass `.env`.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_resolve_explicit_wins_over_env_and_file` | Given a fixture `.env`, an explicit name `flagName` and dir `/explicit`, and `AGENT_SANDBOX_PROJECT_NAME=envvar`, when resolved, then `PROJECT_NAME=flagName`, `PROJECT_DIR=/explicit`, `SANDBOX_DIR=<dir>` | the explicit level beats both the env var and `.env` | proven (identity's explicit-name branch off fails it) |
| `test_resolve_env_var_wins_over_file` | Given a fixture `.env` and `AGENT_SANDBOX_PROJECT_NAME=fromenv`, when resolved, then `PROJECT_NAME=fromenv` | the `AGENT_SANDBOX_*` env var beats `.env` | proven (un-guarding the env level fails it) |
| `test_resolve_file_provides_value` | Given a fixture `.env` only, when resolved, then `PROJECT_NAME=envname` and `PROJECT_DIR=<dir>/envproj` | `.env` supplies the identity with no flag and no env var | proven (disabling the file level fails it) |
| `test_resolve_missing_all_is_hard_error` | Given no value at any level and an absent `.env`, when resolved, then rc is non-zero and stderr names "not set" and "onboard" | a missing value at every level is a hard error with the onboard remedy | proven (making the error return 0 fails it) |
| `test_resolve_env_file_in_provided_sandbox_dir` | Given an explicit sandbox dir and the CWD moved elsewhere, when resolved, then `PROJECT_NAME=envname` from that dir's `.env` | the `.env` location derives from the provided sandbox dir, not the CWD | proven (disabling the sandbox branch of `default_env_file` fails it) |
| `test_resolve_env_file_cwd_fallback` | Given no sandbox dir and the CWD holding a `.env`, when resolved, then all three fields come from the CWD `.env` | with no sandbox known, the `.env` comes from the invocation CWD | proven (disabling the file level fails it) |
| `test_resolve_ignores_leaked_plain_export` | Given a plain exported `PROJECT_NAME=leaked`, a fixture `.env`, and an explicit sandbox dir, when resolved, then `PROJECT_NAME=envname` | only the `AGENT_SANDBOX_*` keys form the env level; a leaked plain name does not bypass `.env` | proven (reading the plain name fails it) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| rename `AGENT_SANDBOX_SANDBOX_DIR` to `AGENT_SANDBOX_NOPE` | none | **survived** - no unit exercises the env-var level for `SANDBOX` (finding 1) |
| `if [[ -n "$explicit" ]]` in `env_resolve_one` to `if false` | none | **survived** - the `explicit` parameter is dead; explicit values are handled in `env_resolve_identity` (finding 4) |
| `default_env_file` sandbox branch to `elif false` | 5 | unit 5 pins the sandbox-dir `.env` derivation |
| read plain `PROJECT_NAME` instead of `AGENT_SANDBOX_PROJECT_NAME` | 2, 7 | units 2 and 7 pin the env level and the two-level guard |
| identity's explicit-name branch to `if false` | 1 | unit 1 pins the explicit level |
| file level (`-n "$env_file" && -f "$env_file"`) to `if false` | 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 | the file level backs most units; disabling it also fails unit 2, because the resolver resolves the whole triple and an unresolvable field is a hard error |
| `return 1` on the hard error to `return 0` | 4 | unit 4 pins the hard error |

### `src/libs/dirs.sh` -- `tests/test_dirs.sh`

Subject: directory-leaf defaults and path derivation. It sets five env-overridable leaf names at source time (`SANDBOX_DIR_NAME`=`sandbox`, `WORKSPACE_DIR_NAME`=`.workspace`, `CHANGES_DIR_NAME`=`session-diffs`, `INPUT_DIR_NAME`=`input`, `OUTPUT_DIR_NAME`=`output`), offers `dirs_resolve` to derive `CHANGES_DIR`/`INPUT_DIR`/`OUTPUT_DIR` from a base directory, `lib_preflight` to check baked libraries, and `_source_lib` to source one with a stale-image diagnostic. `dirs_resolve` does not set `SANDBOX_DIR`: the caller supplies the base. A slash-bearing leaf is not rejected; the subpath doubles. `lib_preflight` treats any severity other than exactly `CRITICAL` as WARN.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_requires_base_dir` | Given an empty `BASE_DIR`, when `dirs_resolve` runs, then rc is non-zero | `BASE_DIR` is required | proven (empty-check off fails it) |
| `test_host_default_paths` | Given the leaf env vars unset, when `dirs_resolve /srv/sandbox` runs, then `CHANGES`/`INPUT`/`OUTPUT` sit under `.workspace` | the host default layout | proven (workspace and changes leaf mutations fail it) |
| `test_container_override` | Given `WORKSPACE_DIR_NAME=workspace`, when `dirs_resolve /home/agentuser` runs, then the three paths sit under `workspace` | the container override of the workspace leaf | proven (changes-leaf mutation fails it) |
| `test_changes_dir_name_is_leaf` | Given `CHANGES_DIR_NAME=workspace/session-diffs`, when `dirs_resolve` runs, then the path doubles | the current behaviour that a slash-bearing leaf doubles the subpath (documents, does not prevent) | proven in the negative: adding a slash rejection breaks it (finding 8) |
| `test_custom_leaf_overrides` | Given custom `CHANGES_DIR_NAME`/`INPUT_DIR_NAME`/`OUTPUT_DIR_NAME`, when `dirs_resolve /base` runs, then the custom leaves appear | custom leaf names override the defaults | read-assessed |
| `test_lib_preflight_passes_when_all_present` | Given all listed libraries present, when `lib_preflight` runs, then rc is 0 and nothing prints | a complete library set passes silently | read-assessed |
| `test_lib_preflight_critical_exits` | Given a missing CRITICAL library, when `lib_preflight` runs, then rc is 1 | a missing CRITICAL library refuses (stale image) | proven (critical branch off fails it) |
| `test_lib_preflight_warn_continues` | Given a missing WARN library, when `lib_preflight` runs, then rc is 0 and the output names the remedy | a missing WARN library warns and continues | proven (WARN-text mutation fails it) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `WORKSPACE_DIR_NAME` fallback in `dirs_resolve` to `.workspaceX` | 2 | unit 2 pins the workspace default; unit 5 keeps `.workspace` from the source-time assignment, so the in-function fallback is nearly dead |
| `CHANGES_DIR_NAME` fallback to `session-DIFFS` | 2, 3 | units 2 and 3 pin the changes leaf |
| empty-`BASE_DIR` check to `if false` | 1 | unit 1 pins the required argument |
| `if false` for the CRITICAL branch | 7 | unit 7 pins the critical refusal |
| WARN text `image may be stale` to `image MAY be stale` | 8 | unit 8 pins the WARN remedy text |
| add a slash rejection to `dirs_resolve` | 4 | **the test pins the un-enforced doubling**: the enforcement its name implies breaks it (finding 8) |

### `src/libs/common.sh` -- `tests/test_common_lib.sh`

Subject: pure flag parsing and validation, shared by the host scripts (`stop.sh`, `prune.sh`, `resume_agent.sh`). It parses `--name=`/`--project=`/`--sandbox=` into `PROJECT_NAME`/`PROJECT_DIR`/`SANDBOX_DIR`, validates that name and sandbox are present and that sandbox is not `/`, dispatches `--help`/`-h` to the caller's `usage`, and sets `INTERACTIVE_MAX_ENTRIES` to 10. It deliberately does not set caller-owned path variables. It also owns `sandbox_dir_canon`, the canonicaliser behind the `agent-sandbox.sandbox-dir` label and the stop/prune discovery filters. The test file also covers five `tests/libs/test_common.sh` harness helpers (finding 3); those units are listed here because they live in the file.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_help_flag_detected` | Given `--help`, when `parse_help_flag` runs, then `usage` is called | long help dispatch | proven (help-flags-off fails it) |
| `test_help_flag_short` | Given `-h`, when `parse_help_flag` runs, then `usage` is called | short help dispatch | proven (help-flags-off fails it) |
| `test_help_flag_not_triggered` | Given ordinary flags, when `parse_help_flag` runs, then it produces no output | no false help trigger (the returned rc is not asserted) | proven (always-trigger fails it) |
| `test_parse_base_flags_sets_vars` | Given `--name`/`--project`/`--sandbox` and an unknown flag, when parsed, then the three vars are set and the unknown flag is ignored | the three base flags parse; unknown args are tolerated | proven (name-flag typo fails it) |
| `test_parse_base_flags_defaults_empty` | Given pre-set vars and no base flags, when parsed, then all three reset to empty | parsing clears prior values | proven (reset made a no-op fails it) |
| `test_check_base_flags_valid` | Given name and sandbox set, when checked, then it passes | the required pair passes | read-assessed |
| `test_check_base_flags_missing_name` | Given an empty `PROJECT_NAME`, when checked, then it fails | `--name` is required | proven (required-check off fails it) |
| `test_check_base_flags_missing_sandbox` | Given an empty `SANDBOX_DIR`, when checked, then it fails | `--sandbox` is required | proven (required-check off fails it) |
| `test_check_base_flags_rejects_root_sandbox` | Given `SANDBOX_DIR=/`, when checked, then it fails | root is rejected as a sandbox | proven (root-check off fails it) |
| `test_check_base_flags_rejects_empty_sandbox` | Given an empty `SANDBOX_DIR`, when checked, then it fails | an empty sandbox is rejected | proven (required-check off fails it) |
| `test_interactive_max_entries_default` | Given `common.sh` sourced with no override, when read, then the cap is 10 | one canonical picker cap | proven (default changed to 20 fails it) |
| `test_subshell_rc_matches_expected` | Given a command exiting 0, when `assert_run 0` runs, then it passes | harness `assert_run` pass side (`test_common.sh`) | read-assessed |
| `test_subshell_rc_mismatch_fails` | Given a command exiting 3, when `assert_run 0` runs in a `bash -c` probe, then it fails with the expected-rc marker and increments the counter | harness `assert_run` fail side (`test_common.sh`) | read-assessed |
| `test_source_function_from_extracts_and_defines` | Given a source file with a named function, when extracted, then the function is invocable | function extraction (`test_common.sh`) | read-assessed |
| `test_source_function_from_fails_when_pattern_missing` | Given no such function, when extracting, then it fails | extraction fails loudly on a miss (`test_common.sh`) | read-assessed |
| `test_skip_counts_as_skipped_unit` | Given a fixture that calls `skip`, when run, then it exits 0, emits `SKIP:`, and counts one skipped | a skip is a warning, not a failure (`test_common.sh`) | read-assessed |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `--name=` to `--nome=` | 4 | unit 4 pins the name flag |
| `--help`/`-h` patterns renamed | 1, 2 | units 1 and 2 pin help dispatch |
| any argument made to call `usage` | 3 | unit 3 pins the no-trigger case |
| the three resets made no-ops (name) | 5 | unit 5 pins the reset |
| the required-flags check to `if false` | 7, 8, 10 | units 7, 8, 10 pin the required checks |
| the root check to `== "//"` | 9 | unit 9 pins the root rejection |
| `INTERACTIVE_MAX_ENTRIES` default to 20 | 11 | unit 11 pins the cap |
| `sandbox_dir_canon` output corrupted | none | **survived** - no unit asserts what `sandbox_dir_canon` returns (finding 2) |

### `src/libs/interface_contract.sh` -- `tests/test_interface_contract.sh`

Subject: the interface-contract version, the single declaration compared across host source, baked image labels, and session records. `interface_contract_version` prints the source-side version (a literal `"1"`). `image_contract_version IMAGE` reads the `agent-sandbox.interface-contract-version` label from an image via `docker image inspect --format`, empty when the image is missing or pre-label. Mismatch policy: drift or a missing label refuses preflight, and the agent entrypoint hard-stops on a container<->container mismatch; there is no runtime override. Bump rule: increment when a cross-boundary contract changes (wiring, mount shape, `SANDBOX_DIR` format, schema, labels), never for doc edits, tests, or internal refactors. The test file also covers `scripts/build.sh`'s `_check_interface_contract`, the authoritative preflight check (finding 3). The header's bump rule names `INTERFACE_CONTRACT_VERSION`, but no such constant exists (finding 12).

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_interface_contract_version_is_positive_integer` | Given the source, when read, then the version is a positive integer | the version is well-formed (the value itself is deliberately not pinned) | proven (version `1` to `0` fails it) |
| `test_image_contract_version_reads_baked_label` | Given a stubbed image label, when read, then the label value is returned | the baked label is the image-side version | proven (label-name mutation fails it) |
| `test_image_contract_version_per_image_map` | Given a per-image stub map, when reading image `b`, then `2` is returned | multiple images resolve independently | proven (label-name mutation fails it) |
| `test_image_contract_version_empty_for_unlabeled_image` | Given an unlabeled image, when read, then the result is empty | a pre-label image is distinguishable | proven (label-name mutation fails it) |
| `test_check_interface_contract_silent_on_aligned` | Given source and image versions equal, when preflighting, then rc 0 and no output | alignment is silent | proven (compare flipped fails it) |
| `test_check_interface_contract_refuses_drift` | Given image version `2`, when preflighting, then rc 1 and the drift is named | drift refuses, with the surface named | proven (compare flipped, drift-text mutation fail it) |
| `test_check_interface_contract_refuses_missing_label` | Given no label, when preflighting, then rc 1 and the rebuild remedy is named | a pre-label image refuses preflight with the remedy | proven (missing-label-text mutation fails it) |

Bite checks (subjects restored after each; `interface_contract.sh` and `build.sh`):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `interface_contract_version` literal `1` to `0` | 1 | unit 1 pins the well-formed version (a change to another positive integer survives - deliberate) |
| label name in `--format` changed | 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 | all label-driven units depend on the label read |
| `_check_interface_contract` compare `!=` to `==` | 5, 6 | units 5 and 6 pin alignment and drift |
| missing-label message changed | 7 | unit 7 pins the missing-label remedy text |
| drift message changed | 6 | unit 6 pins the drift text |
| `image_contract_version` `${1:?}` guard removed | none | **survived** - the required argument is untested (finding 13) |

Version-check limitations, all recorded below: the literal is not the ADR's named constant (finding 12); the value is not pinned by any test, so an accidental bump is invisible (finding 14); the bump timing is unverifiable by a test by design (finding 15); the required-argument guard is untested (finding 13); the Docker stub matches the label as a substring, so a label-name typo that preserves `interface-contract-version` passes (finding 16). `image_contract_version` also returns empty for both a missing image and a pre-label image, but the only caller (`build.sh`) guards image existence first, so the conflation is not reachable there.

### `src/libs/export_status.sh` -- no dedicated test file (units live in `tests/test_diff_export.sh` and `tests/test_session_save_guard.sh`)

Subject: the `.export-status` key=value file, the export pipeline's success/failure record. `_write_export_status OUTPUT_DIR STATUS TIMESTAMP [EXIT_CODE] [INIT_SHA] [HEAD]` always writes STATUS and TIMESTAMP, adds EXIT_CODE only when non-empty and not `0`, and adds INIT_SHA/HEAD only when non-empty; it then writes a temp file and renames it over `.export-status`. `export_status_read DIR KEY` scans for the key and prints everything after the first `=`, empty when the file or key is absent. `export_status_is_success DIR` is true only when STATUS=SUCCESS. Coverage is split: five content units in `test_diff_export.sh`, four read/HEAD units in `test_session_save_guard.sh`, plus two `_save_baseline` consumer units there.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_export_status_writes_success` | diff_export | Given a SUCCESS export with a timestamp and exit code 0, when written, then the file has STATUS=SUCCESS and the timestamp | the success content shape | proven (STATUS key renamed fails it) |
| `test_export_status_writes_failure_with_exit_code` | diff_export | Given a FAIL export with exit code 1, when written, then the file has STATUS=FAIL and EXIT_CODE=1 | a failure records its exit code | proven (STATUS key renamed fails it) |
| `test_export_status_does_not_include_exit_code_on_success` | diff_export | Given a SUCCESS export with exit code 0, when written, then the file has no EXIT_CODE line | exit code 0 is omitted | proven (condition widened fails it) |
| `test_export_status_includes_init_sha` | diff_export | Given a non-empty INIT_SHA, when written, then the file contains INIT_SHA | INIT_SHA is stamped when provided | proven (INIT_SHA condition off fails it) |
| `test_export_status_omits_init_sha_when_empty` | diff_export | Given an empty INIT_SHA, when written, then the file has no INIT_SHA line | an empty INIT_SHA is omitted | proven (INIT_SHA condition always fails it) |
| `test_export_status_stamps_head` | session_save_guard | Given an export with INIT_SHA and HEAD, when written, then the file has HEAD and INIT_SHA | the next save's comparison point is stamped | proven (HEAD condition off fails it) |
| `test_export_status_no_head_when_empty` | session_save_guard | Given an export with no HEAD, when written, then the file has no HEAD line | an absent HEAD is omitted | proven (HEAD always written fails it) |
| `test_export_status_read_fields` | session_save_guard | Given a written file, when read per key, then STATUS/HEAD/TIMESTAMP return, and an absent key or file return empty | the single reader for the format | proven (reader key match off fails it) |
| `test_export_status_is_success` | session_save_guard | Given a SUCCESS record then a FAIL record then an absent file, when checked, then true, false, false | success is exactly STATUS=SUCCESS | proven (comparison inverted fails it) |
| `test_baseline_reads_last_export_head` | session_save_guard | Given a prior SUCCESS export, when `_save_baseline` runs, then it uses the recorded HEAD | consumer: the reader feeds the save baseline | proven |
| `test_baseline_ignores_failed_export` | session_save_guard | Given a prior FAIL export, when `_save_baseline` runs, then it falls back to init_sha | consumer: a failed export does not advance the baseline | proven |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| STATUS key renamed | writes_success, writes_failure, diff_export_failure_writes_export_status | the content key is pinned by three units |
| exit-code condition widened (`EXIT_CODE=0` written) | does_not_include_exit_code_on_success | the omit-on-success rule is pinned |
| INIT_SHA condition off / always | includes_init_sha / omits_init_sha_when_empty | both sides of the INIT_SHA rule are pinned |
| HEAD condition off / always | stamps_head, read_fields, baseline_reads_last_export_head / no_head_when_empty | both sides of the HEAD rule are pinned |
| reader key match off | read_fields, is_success, baseline_reads_last_export_head | the reader is pinned by all its consumers |
| is_success comparison inverted | is_success, baseline_reads_last_export_head, baseline_ignores_failed_export | the STATUS check is pinned |
| `mktemp` fallback path changed | none | **survived** - the fallback is unexercised (finding 22) |
| direct write, temp and rename removed | none | **survived** - the atomic write is unasserted, though `test_diff_export.sh`'s header claims atomicity (finding 22) |

### `src/libs/dry_run_record.sh` -- `tests/test_dry_run_record.sh`

Subject: the readiness model's diagnostics record. The dry-run probes (`scripts/dry_run_capability.sh`, `scripts/dry_run_reasoning.sh`) write a key=value record to the output mount; the orchestrator reads the record, not the probe's stdout. Format: `container=<identity>`, `layer.<name>=<PASS|FAIL>` for six names, and `status=<PASS|FAIL>`. `dry_run_record_value RECORD KEY` prints the value for a key. `dry_run_record_verify NAME EXPECTED_IDENTITY RECORD` checks identity, overall status, and each layer, and returns 0 only when all pass. `dry_run_image_verify IMAGE COMPOSE_FILE TYPE` compares the digest stamped in the compose file at generation time with the current image digest (the ADR `harness_versioning.md` roundtrip gate); it is undocumented in the file's function list and depends on `image_digest` from `src/build/image.sh`.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_record_value_key` | Given a record with container, status, and a layer key, when read per key, then each value returns and an absent key returns empty | the key=value reader | proven (awk key filter off fails it) |
| `test_verify_passes_on_healthy_record` | Given a matching identity, status PASS, and all six layers PASS, when verified, then 0 and the identity/status/per-layer PASS lines | a healthy record verifies | proven (layer, identity, status, and reader mutations fail it) |
| `test_verify_fails_on_identity_mismatch` | Given a container unequal to the expected identity, when verified, then non-zero and the expected/got pair | identity echo-back is checked | proven (identity compare inverted fails it) |
| `test_verify_fails_on_layer_fail` | Given a FAIL layer and status FAIL, when verified, then non-zero and the failed layer named | a failing layer fails the gate | proven (status compare inverted fails it) |
| `test_verify_fails_on_missing_record` | Given no record file, when verified, then non-zero and "record missing" | a missing record is a hard failure | proven (guard removed fails it) |
| `test_verify_passes_record_within_container` | Given an all-PASS record, when verified in a subshell, then 0 | (intended) a mixed record must not pass; as written it re-runs the healthy case (finding 8) | proven |
| `test_digest_gate_passes_when_digests_match` | Given a compose label digest equal to the image digest, when the gate runs, then 0 and the roundtrip match | the roundtrip gate | proven (compare inverted fails it) |
| `test_digest_gate_fails_when_digest_diverges` | Given a compose label digest differing from the image digest, when the gate runs, then non-zero and "digest changed" | a rebuild during the run fails the gate | proven |
| `test_digest_gate_fails_on_missing_record_label` | Given a compose file with no digest label, when the gate runs, then non-zero and the missing label named | no unknown-fallback for a missing label | proven (guard removed fails it) |
| `test_digest_gate_fails_on_dangling_image` | Given a stamped digest and an image the stub reports absent, when the gate runs, then non-zero, the dangling condition, and the rebuild remediation | a pruned image fails the gate | proven (guard removed fails it) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| layer check `!= FAIL` becomes `== PASS` | none | **survived** - no unit feeds a non-PASS, non-FAIL layer value (finding 17) |
| layer check forced to fail | healthy, within-container | the layer check is pinned for the PASS case |
| identity compare inverted | healthy, identity-mismatch, within-container | identity is pinned |
| status compare inverted | healthy, layer-fail, within-container | status is pinned |
| missing-file guard off | missing-record | the missing-record guard is pinned |
| awk key filter off | five units | the reader's key selection is pinned |
| digest compare inverted | digest-passes, digest-diverges | the digest compare is pinned |
| dangling-image guard off | dangling | the dangling guard is pinned |
| missing-label guard off | missing-label | the missing-label guard is pinned |
| `layer.*` line filter removed | none | **survived** - nothing asserts that only layer lines are scanned (finding 17) |

A record with no `layer.*` lines at all (identity and status only) verifies clean, rc=0. The header promises return 0 iff "every layer reported PASS"; the implementation checks only that each line it finds is not `FAIL`, and does not enforce the documented six-name set.

### `src/libs/dry_run_harness.sh` -- no dedicated test file (units live in `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh`)

Subject: the shared check framework for the two dry-run probes. Module state: `CRITICAL_FAILS`, `WARN_FAILS`, the per-layer associative arrays `LAYER_CRIT` and `LAYER_WARN`, and `CURRENT_LAYER`. `_pass`/`_fail`/`_warn` print and count; `critical NAME CMD...` runs a command and records a critical failure; `warn_check` records a warning; `section TITLE` sets `CURRENT_LAYER` to the first word of the title; `_is_writable`/`_is_readonly` probe a directory; `dry_run_write_record RECORD LAYERS [EXTRA...]` writes the diagnostics record; `dry_run_summary` prints the closing block and returns 1 when any critical check failed. The file intentionally omits `set -e`/`set -u` so every check runs. A stub at `tests/stubs/libs/dry_run_harness.sh` sources this file, so the probe test and the liveness scan see it without a drifting copy.

The harness is covered only indirectly, through the probes: `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh` runs each probe once per forced-red layer and asserts the record's `status`, `container`, and `layer.*` values, plus the exit code.

| Unit | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_cap_healthy_rc0_record_pass` | Given a healthy capability fixture, when the probe runs, then rc=0, status PASS, identity echoed, every layer PASS | the healthy path of the framework and record writer | proven |
| `test_cap_session_state_fail` | Given a well-formed but nonexistent init_sha, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.session_state FAIL, session_data still PASS | per-layer failure isolation | proven |
| `test_cap_session_data_fail` | Given a failing diff_export, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.session_data FAIL, session_state still PASS | a data-plane failure stays in its layer | proven |
| `test_cap_container_network_fail` | Given an unwritable CHANGES_DIR parent, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.container_network FAIL | a marker-write failure stays in its layer | proven |
| `test_reas_healthy_rc0_record_pass` | Given a healthy reasoning fixture, when the probe runs, then rc=0, status PASS, identity echoed, every layer PASS | the reasoning healthy path | proven |
| `test_reas_session_state_fail` | Given no SESSION_STATE file, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.session_state FAIL, workspace_mounts still PASS | identity failure isolation | proven |
| `test_reas_workspace_mounts_fail` | Given AGENT_HOME unset, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.workspace_mounts FAIL | `section` maps a check to its layer name | proven |
| `test_reas_agent_runtime_fail` | Given AGENT_CMD points to a missing command, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.agent_runtime FAIL | runtime failure isolation | proven |
| `test_reas_container_network_fail` | Given CHANGES_DIR != EXPECTED_MOUNT_TARGET, when the probe runs, then rc=1, layer.container_network FAIL | a mount-target mismatch is critical | proven |
| `test_no_docker_image_layer_assertion` | Given healthy fixtures for both probes, when each runs, then neither stdout has a docker_image section | docker_image is CP-owned and unasserted by the probes | proven (read) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| layer status forced to PASS | seven fail-path units | the per-layer verdict is pinned |
| overall status forced to PASS | seven fail-path units | the overall verdict is pinned |
| `_fail` stops counting | seven fail-path units | the critical counter drives the exit code |
| `section` keeps the full title | seven fail-path units | the first-word layer key is pinned |
| `_is_writable` always true | reas_healthy, reas_session_state_fail | the writability probe is pinned |
| `_warn` stops counting | none | **survived** - no test forces a warning (finding 21) |
| warn-branch summary text changed | none | **survived** - the warning branch is unexercised (finding 21) |
| record-write failure message changed | none | **survived** - the write-failure branch is unexercised (finding 3) |

`dry_run_write_record` writes `layer.<name>=PASS` for every name in the caller's layer list whose counter is unset, so a listed-but-unchecked layer is reported as PASS. Called with no checks run at all, it produces this:

```text
container=unknown
layer.docker_image=PASS
layer.sandbox_init=PASS
layer.session_data=PASS
status=PASS
```

Both probes list `docker_image` (deliberately unchecked, CP-owned); the capability probe lists `sandbox_init` (the section holds no checks); the reasoning probe lists `session_data` (no such section). `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh`'s `assert_all_layers_pass` then asserts those fabricated values as PASS.

### `src/libs/session_state.sh` -- no dedicated test file (units in `tests/test_session.sh`, `tests/test_reasoner_container_contract.sh`, `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh`)

Subject: the SESSION_STATE K/V store at `SANDBOX_DIR/.git/SESSION_STATE`, cross-context for host and container. `session_state_read DIR KEY` scans the file and prints the first value for the key, empty when the file or key is absent. `session_state_write DIR KEY VALUE` appends a line, and returns 1 when the `.git` directory is absent. `session_state_write_set DIR INIT_SHA` writes the identity block: `init_sha`, `session_ts`, `session_id`, `host_head_sha`, and `interface_contract_version` (the last read from this image's bake). `init_sha_is_valid DIR` returns 0 only when the recorded `init_sha` exists in the sandbox repo as a **commit** object (`git cat-file -e "$sha^{commit}"`, not `rev-parse --verify`, which would accept any well-formed hex). `container_contract_check DIR` compares this image's baked version against the recorded one: a definite mismatch prints a FATAL and returns 1; a missing record or key prints a WARN and returns 0, a documented exception to bash-coding-conventions 3.2 because both cases tell the caller to proceed.

No dedicated test file; the surface is split across three files.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_session_state_read_existing_key` | test_session | Given a record with init_sha and session_ts, when read for init_sha, then the value returns | the key lookup | proven (key match off fails it) |
| `test_session_state_read_missing_file` | test_session | Given no record file, when read for init_sha, then empty | a missing record is not an error | read-assessed (guard removal survived) |
| `test_session_state_read_missing_key` | test_session | Given a record without the key, when read for init_sha, then empty | an absent key yields no value | proven (key match forced true fails it) |
| `test_session_state_read_malformed` | test_session | Given a line that is not key=value, when read for init_sha, then empty | a malformed line is ignored | read-assessed (see finding 8) |
| `test_agent_baked_equals_sandbox_recorded_silent` | reasoner_container_contract | Given a record holding this image's version, when checked, then 0 and no output | an aligned pair passes silently | proven (compare flipped fails it) |
| `test_definite_mismatch_hard_stops` | reasoner_container_contract | Given a different recorded version, when checked, then non-zero and the FATAL plus the orchestration cause | a mismatch is a hard stop | proven (compare flipped fails it) |
| `test_missing_record_key_warns_not_aborts` | reasoner_container_contract | Given a record without the version key, when checked, then 0 and the pre-record-image warning | the upgrade path proceeds | proven (key guard off fails it) |
| `test_missing_record_file_warns_not_aborts` | reasoner_container_contract | Given no record at all, when checked, then 0 and the missing-record warning | the file guard warns instead of failing | proven (file guard off fails it) |
| `test_init_sha_is_valid_lib` | dry_run_probe | Given four fixtures (valid commit, bogus hex, missing key, blob object), when checked, then 0, 1, 1, 1 | object existence and commit type, not hex shape | proven (commit peel removed fails it) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| reader key match off | read_existing_key | the match is pinned |
| reader key match forced true | read_missing_key | the absent-key case is pinned |
| reader missing-file guard off | none | **survived** - result-redundant; the guard only silences stderr (finding 22) |
| `session_state_write` truncates instead of appending | none | **survived** - `session_state_write` has no unit (finding 22) |
| `session_state_write_set` drops the contract-version line | none | **survived** - `session_state_write_set` has no unit (finding 22) |
| `init_sha_is_valid` drops `^{commit}` | init_sha_is_valid_lib | the commit-type peel is pinned |
| `init_sha_is_valid` empty-value guard off | init_sha_is_valid_lib | the empty guard is pinned |
| contract compare flipped | both aligned and mismatch units | the comparison is pinned |
| contract missing-file guard off | missing_record_file | the file guard is pinned |
| contract missing-key guard off | missing_record_key | the key guard is pinned |
| contract `${1:?}` arg guard off | none | **survived** - the required-argument guard is untested (finding 13) |

### `src/libs/session_inventory.sh` -- `tests/test_session_inventory.sh` and `tests/test_session_log.sh` (plus `env_field` units in `tests/test_prune.sh`)

Subject: the registry-record and session-inventory helper set. Five concerns share the file. (1) Record parsers over `.compose/<sid>.yml`: `record_image FILE SERVICE` is a service-scoped awk scan that prints the first `image:` inside the named block; `record_provider FILE` wraps it and strips `-agent-*`; `record_label FILE LABEL` and `env_field FILE VAR` are `grep`/`sed` readers, each pipefail-safe through a trailing `|| true`. (2) Git helpers against the caller's `PROJECT_DIR`: `project_current_sha`, `project_current_branch` (branch name, `<short-sha> (detached)`, or `(absent)`), and `project_branch_age SHA` (`N commit[s] ago`, `0 commits ago`, `not in tree`, or `-` for an empty SHA). (3) Enumeration and staleness: `enumerate_records` prints `sid|provider|ts|branch` per record, optionally narrowed by `PROVIDER_FILTER`, and is deliberately UNFILTERED for `dryrun-` records because prune Rule 1 must reach them; `session_stale FILE [CURRENT_SHA]` prints `fresh`/`stale`/`unknown`. (4) The dry-run label: `DRYRUN_SID_PREFIX` plus `session_is_dry_run`, the canonical prefix definition, consumed by the resume listing filter and the run-time teardown. (5) The per-session activity log `.compose/<sid>.log` and two human-time formatters: `session_log_path`, `session_log_read` (`grep -m1` plus `sed`), `session_log_set` (sibling-temp upsert, the portability fix for the macOS teardown bug), `ts_to_epoch` (GNU `date -d`), `relative_time`, and `relative_time_compact`.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_record_image_extracts_service_image` | session_inventory | sandbox and agent blocks, each with an image; read per service | per-service extraction | proven (service match forced true fails it) |
| `test_record_image_stops_at_next_service` | session_inventory | an agent block with an image, followed by another service | the scan stops at the boundary | proven (service match forced true fails it); the block-without-image case is unasserted (finding 23) |
| `test_record_image_missing_service_empty_rc0` | session_inventory | no block for the requested service | empty output at rc 0 | read-assessed |
| `test_record_provider_recovers_prefix_and_rejects_noncanonical` | session_inventory | canonical and non-canonical agent images | the `-agent-` gate | proven (gate off fails it) |
| `test_record_label_pipefail_safe_on_no_match` | session_inventory | one label present, one absent, under pipefail | the absent label is empty at rc 0 | proven (the pipefail guard off, and the value strip off, both fail it) |
| `test_project_current_sha_branches` | session_inventory | PROJECT_DIR unset / non-git / git | empty / empty / HEAD | proven (`echo HEAD` fails it) |
| `test_session_stale_classification` | session_inventory | match / differ / absent against CURRENT_SHA | fresh / stale / unknown | proven (swap and value strip fail it) |
| `test_session_stale_derives_sha_from_project_dir` | session_inventory | no explicit SHA, PROJECT_DIR git | derives and reports fresh | proven (derive off and swap fail it) |
| `test_enumerate_records_filters_and_skips` | session_inventory | two recoverable, one unrecoverable, one dry-run record | the unfiltered core plus the filter | proven (filter off, skip off, gate off, and value strip off all fail it) |
| `test_enumerate_records_no_dir_or_empty_is_silent_rc0` | session_inventory | a missing registry and an empty one | silent success | read-assessed |
| `test_session_log_set_read` | session_log | set, overwrite, then add a second key | latest values, one line per key | proven (append off and upsert-to-append fail it) |
| `test_session_log_missing` | session_log | no log file | empty value, no file created | read-assessed |
| `test_session_log_path` | session_log | SANDBOX_DIR set | `<dir>/.compose/<id>.log` | proven (`.log` to `.txt` fails it) |
| `test_session_log_set_avoids_inplace_sed` | session_log | a shim that fails any `sed -i` | set and upsert with no in-place sed | proven (append off, upsert-to-append, path change all fail it) |
| `test_inplace_sed_shim_rejects_dash_i` | session_log | the shim itself, `sed -i` invoked directly | it fails | read-assessed (the guard on the shim) |
| `test_ts_to_epoch` | session_log | well-formed / malformed / empty | integer / empty / empty | **survived** - the guard accepts anything and the unit still passes (finding 26) |
| `test_relative_time_units` | session_log | now, 125s, 2h, 2d, empty, garbage | just now / 2 minutes / 2 hours / 2 days / --- / --- | proven (minutes divisor fails it) |
| `test_relative_time_compact_units` | session_log | the same timestamps | just now / 2m / 2h / 2D / --- / --- | proven (minutes divisor fails it) |
| `test_env_field_reads_value_from_environment_block` | test_prune | an environment block holding `SANDBOX_TYPE=mount` | the value | read-assessed |
| `test_env_field_no_substring_matches` | test_prune | NODE_PATH and PATH entries | PATH is not matched as a substring | read-assessed |
| `test_env_field_first_match_wins` | test_prune | two `A=` lines | the first value | proven (`head -1` off fails it) |
| `test_env_field_missing_key_is_empty_and_clean` | test_prune | no ABSENT entry | empty at rc 0 | read-assessed |
| `test_env_field_tolerates_dash_spacing_variants` | test_prune | `- A=plain` and `-   B=spaced` | both values | read-assessed |
| `test_record_image_service_scoped` | test_trace_build | a comment shadow, a labels block, and three services | each service's own image and the provider | read-assessed (the same mutation fails the session_inventory units) |

End-to-end units that reach this lib through a consumer: `test_interactive_shows_current_branch_hint`, `test_interactive_branch_hint_absent`, `test_list_shows_branch_point_age`, and `test_list_state_cell_from_log` (`test_resume.sh`), plus `test_select_session_state_and_age_columns` and `test_select_session_current_branch_hint` (`test_interactive_session_select.sh`). They pin `project_current_branch` and `project_branch_age` behaviour without naming either function. No test reaches `session_is_dry_run`.

### `src/libs/session_hints.sh` -- no dedicated test file (assertions live in `tests/test_trace_start.sh` and `tests/test_trace_stop.sh`)

Subject: one function, `session_end_hints SANDBOX_DIR SESSION_ID`, prints the operator-facing teardown hint pair. It prints the resume hint unconditionally, then resolves `CHANGES_DIR` through `dirs_resolve`, selects the newest `*-SESSION_ID` export directory under `CHANGES_DIR/session/` by name sort, and prints the draft hint only when that directory holds `patches/` or `uncommitted.diff` (the same rule `resolve_source_for_draft` validates, in inverted sense). Suppress-on-absent is the point: a hint naming a non-existent or non-draftable bundle would make `make draft` fail. Consumed by `scripts/run_agent.sh` (`make start` teardown) and `scripts/stop.sh`; both run `set -euo pipefail`.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_start_standard_shutdown_resume_hint` | trace_start | a session with no export dir, when the standard run tears down | the resume hint, and no draft hint | proven (B1) |
| `test_start_standard_shutdown_draft_hint` | trace_start | one draftable export dir in the `EXPORT_TIME-SESSION_ID` shape | the draft hint names that exact bundle | proven (B2) |
| `test_start_standard_shutdown_draft_hint_suppressed_for_empty_export` | trace_start | an export dir with no draftable content | the draft hint is suppressed | proven (B3) |
| `test_stop_shutdown_hints` | trace_stop | `make stop --session-id` with one draftable export dir | both hints | proven (B1, B2, B8) |
| `test_stop_draft_hint_suppressed_without_export` | trace_stop | `make stop --session-id` with no export dir | the resume hint only | proven (B1) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each):

| B2 draft hint text changed | 2 | the draft wording is pinned |
| B3 draftability gate removed | 1 (start) | suppress-on-empty is pinned on the start surface only |
| B4 newest selection turned into oldest (`tail` to `head`) | none | **survived** - every fixture holds one export dir (finding 34) |
| B5 `*-SESSION_ID` filter removed | none | **survived** - single-session fixtures (finding 34) |
| B6 `_session_base` early return removed | none | **survived** - redundant guard (finding 35) |
| B7 assignment `or return` removed | none | **survived** - redundant guard (finding 35) |
| B8 `dirs_resolve` call removed | 1 (stop) | the stop path depends on it; the start fixture sets `CHANGES_DIR` itself |

### `src/libs/draft_state.sh` -- `tests/test_draft_state.sh` (plus the commit-message units in `tests/test_draft_workflow.sh`)

Subject: draft-branch state. `draft_parse_folder_name BASENAME` sets `SESSION_TS`, `SANITIZED_HOST_BRANCH`, and `SESSION_ID` in the caller's scope by column slicing (`:0:15`, `:16`) plus a trailing `[a-f0-9]{6}` shape test. `draft_guard_no_collision DIR BRANCH` refuses an existing branch. `draft_write_state` renders the `.draft-state` record: colon-delimited, eight fields plus an optional `session_id`. `draft_read_state_from_branch DIR BRANCH` and `draft_validate_branch DIR` print `KEY="VALUE"` assignments for the caller to `eval`; `draft_validate_branch` additionally enforces the `draft/*` prefix and a non-empty `from_hash`, and locates the `.draft-state` commit by log message. `draft_resolve_commit_message DIFF_FILE` resolves a commit subject from a sibling `.msg`, then the filename, then a fallback.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_parse_folder_name_basic` | draft_state | `<ts>-<branch>`, no session-id | split at column 15, empty session id | proven (M1, M2) |
| `test_parse_folder_name_with_session_id` | draft_state | trailing 6-hex suffix | session id recovered and removed from the branch | proven (M1, M2, M4) |

### `src/libs/cli.sh` -- `tests/test_cli_lib.sh`

Subject: the declarative flag parser behind thirteen scripts. `parse_args USAGE_FN spec... -- args...` and `parse_args_collect SINK spec... -- args...` are thin policy wrappers over `_cli_parse MODE USAGE_FN SINK spec... -- args...`. The spec is compiled into a per-call associative array (`--flag=VAR` value, `--flag` boolean defaulting to `<UPPER_SNAKE>`, `--flag=` value into `<UPPER_SNAKE>`, `--flag:VAR` boolean into a named var, bare word literal), targets are written with `declare -g`, and the three modes are strict (unmatched prints usage and returns 1), drop (`_CLI_TOLERANT=1`, unmatched ignored), and collect (unmatched appended to a caller array). The unset-only rule means a caller-predeclared default survives a spec whose flag never fires.

| Unit | File | Given / When / Then | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `test_parse_args_value_flag` | cli_lib | `--delivery=DELIVERY` and `--delivery=copy` | the value route | proven (C7, C8) |
| `test_parse_args_boolean_flag` | cli_lib | a boolean spec and `--force` | the boolean route | proven (C7, C8) |
| `test_parse_args_unknown_strict_fails` | cli_lib | an unknown flag, strict | rc 1 plus usage | proven (C7) |
| `test_parse_args_tolerant_drops_unknown` | cli_lib | `_CLI_TOLERANT=1` | rc 0 | proven (C7) |
| `test_parse_args_help_exits_2` | cli_lib | `--help` among the args | usage and rc 2 | proven (C2, C3) |
| `test_collect_routes_specs_and_appends_rest` | cli_lib | mixed specs and args | routing plus ordered appends | proven (C7, C8) |
| `test_collect_never_errors_on_unknown` | cli_lib | unknown args only | rc 0 and all sinked | proven (C7) |
| `test_collect_empty_args` | cli_lib | no args after the separator | empty sink | proven (C7) |
| `test_collect_boolean_spec_not_sinked` | cli_lib | `--verbose v1` | boolean consumed, `v1` sinked | proven (C7, C8) |
| `test_collect_literal_spec_not_sinked` | cli_lib | a `--permissive` spec | sink contents only | proven (C7); the spec is a boolean, so the literal kind is not exercised (finding 42) |
| `test_collect_appends_to_predeclared_sink` | cli_lib | a pre-seeded sink | append after the seed | proven (C7) |
| `test_collect_help_not_special` | cli_lib | `--help -h --other` in collect | all three sinked | proven (C4) |
| `test_collect_bare_value_flag_consumed` | cli_lib | the arg `bar`, no flag | `ENV_REL` empty, `bar` sinked | proven (C7); no bare flag is present, so the missing-value path is not exercised (finding 41) |
| `test_collect_has_no_stale_registry` | cli_lib | two parses, different specs | no cross-call registry | proven (C8) |

|---|---|---|

Direct probes (original code): a bare value flag at the end of a strict parse returns 0 and sets the variable to the flag name (`--name` becomes the value of `PROJECT_NAME`); the same bare flag in collect mode sets `ENV_REL` to `--env`; a `--permissive` spec is registered as a boolean and the flag sets `PERMISSIVE=true` rather than being ignored; a bracketed value (`x[0]`) and a value containing `=` (`a=b=c`) are both preserved; a predeclared `DELIVERY=copy` survives a parse that never sees `--delivery`.

### Arg-parsing review (cross-cutting) -- surfaces, rules, and unification

Subject: five surfaces carry the same settings into the harness, and each owns a different part of the contract.

| Surface | Mechanism | Scope | Rules it owns |
|---|---|---|---|
| Leaf CLI | `cli.sh` (`parse_args`, 14 callers) | every leaf flag | spec grammar, unknown-argument policy, variable naming (findings 41-44) |
| Dispatcher identity | `parse_args_collect` plus `env_resolve_identity` (`agent-sandbox.sh:143`) | `--env`, `--name`, `--project`, `--sandbox` | precedence explicit, then `AGENT_SANDBOX_*`, then `.env`, then interactive; forwards `--name`/`--project`/`--sandbox` to the leaf, which re-parses them |
| Identity re-validation | `check_base_flags` (`common.sh`), called at `stop.sh:55` and `prune.sh:241` | `PROJECT_NAME`, `SANDBOX_DIR` | a third pass over values the dispatcher already resolved |
| Make assembly | 16 `<NAME>_FLAG` translators plus four inline `$(if)` forms (`Makefile.template`) | the operator's `make` surface | Make-variable-to-flag mapping and truthiness (findings 44, 45) |
| Env override | `AGENT_SANDBOX_<KEY>` read by `env_resolve_one` | identity only | the host-environment spelling of the same identity fields |

Hand-rolled parsers outside this surface: `scripts/macos_bootstrap.sh` (`--patch-shell`, `--switch-shell`), `scripts/manual/install_host_git_hooks.sh`, and `scripts/run_tests.sh` (`-v`, `-vv`). These are host and developer scripts, not the harness CLI; they are noted, not proposed for unification. `interactive.sh:246` is a positional `shift`, not flag parsing.

Probe evidence, all against the shipped code:

| Probe | Input | Observed | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | `parse_args --name=PROJECT_NAME -- --name` | rc 0, `PROJECT_NAME=--name` | error: value required |
| P2 | `parse_args_collect --env=ENV_REL -- --env` | `ENV_REL=--env`, sink empty | error, or an empty value |
| P4 | `--name=x[0]` | `x[0]` | same - correct |
| P5 | `--name=a=b=c` | `a=b=c` | same - correct |
| P6 | predeclared `DELIVERY=copy`, no `--delivery` | `copy` | same - correct, but unpinned (finding 43) |
| R1 | boolean `--refresh=` | true | error, or false |
| R2 | boolean `--refresh=0` | true | false |
| R3 | value `--name=` | empty, rc 0 | error, or documented as the default |
| R4 | `--name=a --name=b` | `b` | last-wins - correct, but unpinned |
| R6 | tolerant typo `--dry-runn` | rc 0, flag unset | error, or a warning (finding 46a) |
| R7 | `-- --help` | rc 2, usage printed | help is not a positional value |
| R8 | undeclared collect sink | rc 0, sink created | error, or a documented creation |
| E1 | `.env` line `export FOO=bar` | `exportFOO=bar`, `FOO` unset | `FOO=bar` (finding 47a) |
| E2 | `.env` line `NOEQ` | `NOEQ` set empty | skipped with a warning (finding 47b) |
| E3 | `.env` line `FOO="a b"` | value keeps the quotes | decide and document |
| E4 | `.env` line `FOO=a=b` | `a=b` | same - correct |
| E5 | `.env` line `1BAD=x` | warned and skipped | same - correct |
| E6 | `.env` line `FOO=v # c` | `v # c` | decide and document |
| E7 | `.env` line ` K = v ` with CRLF | `K=v` | same - correct |

Unification analysis. The leaf parser is already unified; what is not unified is the set of surfaces around it. One setting can arrive as a Make variable, a CLI flag, an `AGENT_SANDBOX_*` variable, or a `.env` line, and each surface decides kind, default, naming, and 0-handling for itself. The three fail-open defects found in this review are each the same shape: a setting reaches a layer whose rules disagree with the surface the operator used (a bare flag in `cli.sh`, `FLAG=0` in the Makefile, a typo in prune's tolerant mode). That safety motive, not elegance, is the reason to unify.

The unification target differs from the KV family. KV needs one reader and writer pair for one record shape (finding 22). Arg parsing needs one declaration per setting - canonical name, kind, default, and the CLI flag, Make variable, and env-override spellings - from which the leaf spec, the Make translator, and the env override are derived. The leaf parser stays as it is, and the third identity pass disappears rather than being formalised. This is a cross-boundary change touching every leaf, the Makefile, the dispatcher, the env resolver, the help text, and the docs, so it needs its own handover and probably its own ADR.

---

### `src/libs/routing.sh` -- `tests/test_routing.sh` (plus `tests/test_diff_dispatch.sh` and the knowledge diagnostics)

Subject: path-layout conventions. `_resolve_paths SANDBOX_DIR` reads `changes_dir`, `input_dir`, and `output_dir` from SESSION_STATE and falls back to `dirs_resolve` when any is empty. `resolve_channel_base_dir CHANNEL` maps `session`, `autosave`, and `bundles` onto the resolved bases and rejects anything else. `export_path PARENT_DIR SUBDIR SESSION_ID [LABEL]` builds `<PARENT>/<SUBDIR>/<EXPORT_TIME>[-<LABEL>]-<SESSION_ID>`, with autosave as the one exception that omits `EXPORT_TIME`. `resolve_latest_dir BASE` picks the lexicographically last subdirectory, and `resolve_latest_dir_by_mtime BASE` the newest by directory mtime, with the comment recording why each channel uses which. `resolve_source_for_draft SANDBOX_DIR CHANNEL [BUNDLE_ARG]` resolves a draft source, rejects an absolute `BUNDLE_ARG`, and validates that the directory holds `patches/` or `uncommitted.diff`.

The lib depends on GNU `find -printf` for its mtime selection (finding 51).

Units, grouped (the file holds 36 registrations; the autosave group is not routing coverage):

| Group | Units | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `export_path` | `test_export_path_session`, `_autosave`, `_bundles_with_label`, `_bundles_no_label`, `_diffs_with_label`, `_missing_args`, `_missing_session_id` | path shapes, the autosave exception, the required-argument guard | proven (R1, R2, R3) |
| channel base | `test_channel_base_dir_all_channels`, `_unknown_rejected`, `test_resolve_channel_base_dir_session`, `_autosave`, `_bundles`, `_invalid` | the three channels and rejection | proven (R13) |
| latest dir | `test_latest_dir_missing_base_fails`, `_empty_base_fails`, `_picks_lexicographically_last`, `test_resolve_latest_dir_missing_base_fails`, `_empty_base_fails`, `_picks_lexicographic_max_ignoring_files` | missing and empty failure, lexicographic selection, files ignored | proven (R4, R5); three pairs of near-duplicate units (finding 52) |
| latest dir by mtime | `test_resolve_latest_dir_by_mtime` | mtime ordering and the path cut | proven (R7); numeric versus lexicographic sort unpinned (finding 53) |
| draft resolution | `test_resolve_draft_default_channel`, `_explicit_channel_autosave`, `_autosave_newest_by_mtime`, `_named_session`, `_absolute_path_rejected`, `_missing_session`, `_bundles_channel`, `_invalid_channel`, `test_staging_dir_is_not_selectable` | channel and base resolution, named versus auto | proven (R10); the absolute-path rejection (R8) and the draftability guard (R9) are unasserted (finding 49) |
| `_resolve_paths` | `test_resolve_paths_state_overrides_win`, `_falls_back_to_dirs_resolve` | state values win, fallback works | proven (R11, R12); the partial-state case is unasserted (finding 50) |
| autosave integration | `test_autosave_swap_sequence`, `_tick_absorbs_status_under_real_set_e`, `_loop_survives_failing_ticks`, `_cycle_refuses_unreadable_repo_end_to_end`, `test_entrypoint_autosave_call_arguments` | the autosave cycle, not this lib | row 3 extension |

Probe for finding 50: a SESSION_STATE holding only `changes_dir=/custom/changes` yields `CHANGES_DIR=<sandbox>/.workspace/session-diffs` after `_resolve_paths`, so the recorded value is discarded because the `dirs_resolve` fallback overwrites all three keys; a complete record returns the three custom values unchanged. A second probe with two directories of identical mtime returns the same directory on repeated calls, but the winner is the last entry in `find` order, which the contract does not define.

### `src/libs/session_save_policy.sh` -- `tests/test_session_save_guard.sh` (plus the autosave units in `tests/test_routing.sh`)

Subject: the save lifecycle. `session_save_needed SANDBOX_DIR BASELINE` is pure policy over git state and returns a three-valued status: 0 save, 1 skip, 2 undeterminable, with the third value existing so a broken repository is never reported as nothing to save. `save_decision SANDBOX_DIR EXPORT_DIR LABEL` resolves the baseline and maps the three values onto save / skip-with-diagnostic / save-loudly. `session_export_needed SANDBOX_DIR` is the exit-time form whose baseline is the durable branch point (`init_sha`), never an autosave checkpoint. `_save_baseline SANDBOX_DIR EXPORT_DIR` reads the previous successful export's HEAD, falling back to `init_sha`. `autosave_cycle` builds the next checkpoint at a staging path beside the channel, runs the injected export command, and swaps it in only on success, rescuing the export's error log first. `autosave_tick` resolves the checkpoint via an injected path function and calls the cycle; `autosave_loop` repeats the tick on an interval and absorbs every status.

The export argv contract is the file's best-covered behaviour: the docstring records that a dropped argument once broke every autosave in production while the suite stayed green, and the argument order is now pinned (S10, S12).

Units (13 decision units in `test_session_save_guard.sh` plus 4 export-status units that live there for the record reader, and 5 autosave units in `test_routing.sh`):

| Group | Units | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `session_save_needed` | `test_dirty_tree_always_saves`, `_clean_tree_at_baseline_skips`, `_clean_tree_past_baseline_saves`, `_unreadable_repository_is_undeterminable`, `_clean_tree_guard_is_three_valued` | the three-valued decision | proven (S1, S2) |
| `save_decision` | `test_save_decision_skip_reports_and_returns_1`, `_save_arm_is_silent_and_returns_0`, `_undeterminable_saves_and_warns` | the three-way mapping and its diagnostics | proven (S1, S3) |
| `session_export_needed` | `test_session_export_runs_with_work_despite_committed_or_uncommitted`, `_skips_clean_tree_at_branch_point` | the durable-baseline rule | proven (S2, S5) |
| `_save_baseline` | `test_baseline_falls_back_to_init_sha`, `_reads_last_export_head`, `_ignores_failed_export` | the two levels and the success gate | proven (S6, S7) |
| autosave cycle | `test_autosave_swap_sequence`, `_cycle_refuses_unreadable_repo_end_to_end`, `test_entrypoint_autosave_call_arguments` | the swap sequence, the log rescue, the export argv | proven (S8, S9, S10, S14, S18) |
| autosave loop | `test_autosave_tick_absorbs_status_under_real_set_e`, `_loop_survives_failing_ticks` | status absorption under `set -e` | proven (S13) |

### `src/libs/diff.sh` -- `tests/test_diff_helpers.sh`, `tests/test_diff_workflow.sh`, `tests/test_binary_roundtrip.sh`, `tests/test_apply_count.sh`

Subject: the diff pipeline between a sandbox worktree and an exported patch. Three primitives write artifacts - `write_uncommitted_diff SANDBOX_DIR OUTPUT_FILE`, `write_all_changes_diff SANDBOX_DIR OUTPUT_FILE [SINCE_SHA]` (baseline from SESSION_STATE `init_sha` unless given), and `write_changed_files SANDBOX_DIR SINCE_SHA OUTPUT_DIR` (working-tree copies plus MANIFEST). Two primitives apply them - `_apply_patch_file PROJECT_DIR DIFF_FILE FORCE` (normal, then a `--recount` retry, or `--reject` in force mode) and `apply_and_commit PROJECT_DIR DIFF_FILE COMMIT_MSG AUTHOR [FORCE]`. Three helpers carry the byte-level contract: `_diff_stage_untracked` and `_diff_restore_untracked` bracket every write with `git add -N` on the untracked set, `_write_git_diff` is the single writer for both diff forms, and `strip_index_lines` drops index lines from text patches while keeping them for binary patches. `diff_is_empty` is the shared no-change test.

Units (41 direct across four suites; the remaining units in `test_diff_workflow.sh` and `test_apply_count.sh` drive `scripts/workflows/apply.sh` and belong to the phase 4 read-through):

| Group | Units | Asserts | Bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `write_uncommitted_diff` | 7 units, `test_uncommitted_*` | the write, the empty case, the untracked case, the strip, the guard, and two byte-fidelity round-trips plus the eight-class whitespace matrix | proven (D7, D13-adjacent), with the restore gap at D4 |
| `write_all_changes_diff` | 5 units, `test_all_changes_*` | both change classes, the empty case, both guards | proven (D15) |
| `write_changed_files` | 7 units, `test_changed_files_*` | copies, untracked, deleted, manifest, structure, dedup, guard | proven (D16), with the manifest gap at D17 |
| `strip_index_lines` | 4 units in `test_diff_helpers.sh` plus 7 in `test_binary_roundtrip.sh` | text strip, binary preservation, mixed, passthrough, and the round-trip matrix | proven (D7, 11 units failed) |
| `_apply_patch_file` | 5 units | normal, force, missing file, recount retry, failure hints | proven (D8, D10) |
| `apply_and_commit` | 5 units | apply-and-commit, force mode, empty-member commit, both guards | proven (D13, D14) |

Bite checks (subject restored after each; every mutation ran the six suites that reach this file):

| Mutation | Units failed | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| D1 stage: `--exclude-standard` dropped | none | **survived** - `git add -N` refuses ignored paths first (finding 62a) |
| D2 stage: `add -N` becomes `add` | none | **survived** - indistinguishable through `git diff HEAD` (finding 62b) |
| D3 restore: empty-array guard off | none | **survived** - a bash 4.0 floor guard, invisible on bash 5.2 (finding 62c) |
| D4 restore: `--staged` dropped | none | **survived** - the mutation truncates untracked files; the suite never looks at the worktree (finding 57) |
| D5 write_git_diff: empty fast path off | none | **survived** - pure optimisation (finding 62d) |
| D6 write_git_diff: trailing-newline append off | none | **survived** - the append only adds a blank line (finding 61) |
| D7 strip: binary index preservation off | 13 across 4 suites | the binary half is pinned hard |
| D8 apply: force mode returns 1 | 4 | force mode's never-fail contract is pinned |
| D9 apply: `--ignore-whitespace` dropped | none | **survived** - the permissive-apply policy is unpinned (finding 58) |
| D10 apply: recount retry off | 1 | the relaxed-context retry is pinned |
| D11 is_empty: header anchor off | none | **survived** - the anchor cannot be distinguished (finding 62e) |
| D12 apply_and_commit: AUTHOR requirement off | none | **survived** - only the all-empty invocation is tested (finding 13) |
| D13 apply_and_commit: empty-commit author dropped | 1 | the empty-member commit pins its own author |
| D14 apply_and_commit: `add -A` becomes `add -u` | 1 | the stage-everything step is pinned |
| D15 all_changes: `init_sha` becomes `host_head_sha` | 3 | the default baseline is pinned |
| D16 changed_files: deleted-file skip off | 1 | the copy-loop skip is pinned |
| D17 changed_files: manifest from the copied set | none | **survived** - the manifest/copy mismatch is unasserted (finding 59) |
| D18 changed_files: empty-dir cleanup off | none | **survived** - the deletion-only test accepts either outcome (finding 62f) |

Probes run alongside the bites: a gitignored untracked file is refused by `git add -N` (rc 1) and stays out of the diff (D1); `git restore -- <intent-to-add path>` empties the file at rc 0 with no diagnostic, and the mutated pipeline leaves the file at 0 bytes while `git status` still shows the staged entry (D4); a patch whose context differs by one internal space fails plain `git apply` and passes with `--ignore-whitespace` (D9); one deletion plus one modification yields a `changed-files/` holding only the modified file and a `MANIFEST.txt` naming both (D17); a corrupted index leaves `write_uncommitted_diff` at rc 0 with a 0-byte file and a `fatal:` line on stderr (finding 60); and the generated diff ends `+BETA\n\n` (87 bytes) against `+BETA\n` (86 bytes) with the append removed (finding 61).
