## Phase 3 -- src/build/*, src/capability/*

Scope note: shell files only, by operator direction. The compose YAML (`docker-compose*.yml`, 254 lines) and the capability `dockerfile` (74 lines) are excluded; they carry no shell and no mutation bite. A finding that would need a read of the exclusion to settle says so.

### `src/build/image.sh` -- `tests/test_image_names.sh` (5 units) and `tests/test_image.sh` (3)

60 lines, five functions. Four name the built images and one reads their identity: `agent_base_image_name` (`<provider>-base`), `agent_image_name` (`<provider>-agent-<project>`), `shared_base_image_name` (`agent-node-base`), `sandbox_image_name` (`sandbox-<project>`), and `image_digest` (`docker image inspect --format '{{.Id}}'`). The module is the naming contract's single implementation: `scripts/build.sh` (l.103, 107, 111, 171, 196-197), `src/build/compose.sh` (l.94-95), `src/libs/session_env.sh` (l.104-107), and `src/libs/dry_run_record.sh` (l.87) all call it. No consumer re-derives a tag by hand.

Units. `test_image_names.sh` pins the base lowercase, the agent project-only lowercase, the sandbox lowercase, the shared constant, and the three missing-argument guards. `test_image.sh`, through the docker stub, pins the digest pass-through, a per-image digest map, and the empty value for an image the stub does not know.

Bites (9 mutations, V1-V9). Proven, six: V1 removes the base lowercase; V2 removes the base argument guard; V4 removes the sandbox lowercase; V5 changes the shared constant; V6 changes the digest format string; V9 removes the agent project guard. Survived, three: V3 lowercases the provider inside `agent_image_name`; V7 drops the digest's `2>/dev/null`; V8 removes `image_digest`'s argument guard. Findings 86-93 record the survivors and the contract drifts around them.

### `src/build/compose.sh` -- `tests/test_trace_compose_gen.sh` (11 units) plus the phase 4 trace families

457 lines, seven functions, the largest file in phase 3. `compose_generate` merges the compose file set and bakes the harness values in; `compose_args` derives the compose project namespace; `compose_file_from_args` recovers the generated record path; `compose_dry_run` runs the two-pass fresh-plus-resume exercise; `session_teardown` and `session_destroy` end a session with and without its volume; `compose_sandbox_wait` polls the sandbox container to health. Its callers are `scripts/run_agent.sh` (l.296, 302) and `scripts/start_agent.sh`.

Units. `test_trace_compose_gen.sh` carries 11. Three of them are static greps over the YAML overlays rather than runs of the shell, and the YAML is excluded from this pass; the rest drive `compose_generate` through the docker stub and pin the generated record's shape. The shell functions' behaviour is otherwise covered by four trace families whose own read is the phase 4 pass: `test_trace_start.sh` (23 units), `test_trace_dry_run.sh` (6), `test_trace_resume.sh` (10), `test_run_agent.sh` (8), and `test_dry_run_record.sh` (10) for the record verifier `compose_dry_run` calls. `compose_args` is reached only through `run_agent.sh`.

Bites (22 mutations, C1-C22, each run against the full suite). Proven, four: C5 removes the `{{FLATTEN}}` stamp; C6 removes the `{{AGENT_IMAGE_DIGEST}}` stamp (nine units fail); C18 turns `session_teardown`'s `down` into `down -v` (six fail); C19 turns `session_destroy`'s `down -v` into `down` (three fail). Survived, eighteen: every `compose_generate` failure return (C1 no input, C2 docker absent, C3 empty digest, C9 input file not found), its staging cleanup (C8), the merge-status capture (C10), the `name:` strip (C4), `--no-interpolate` (C7), the staging filename order (C11), both `compose_file_from_args` edges (C12 first-versus-last `-f`, C13 the bounds guard), all four `compose_args` derivations (C14-C17), and three `compose_sandbox_wait` clauses (C20 the exited fast-fail, C21 the timeout boundary, C22 the poll target). Findings 94-100 record them; the teardown pair is the file's one solidly pinned area.

### `src/capability/git-hooks/pre-commit.sh` -- `tests/test_git_hook.sh` (9 units)

80 lines, no functions: a staged-file gate that runs two checkers and accumulates one verdict. The header states the contract precisely - copy delivery only, because the volume's `.git` is container-local and the hook can never run on the host; mount delivery installs none; the deliberate bypass is `git commit --no-verify`. Both header caveats are real and decided: the linters read the working-tree file rather than the index copy, and the staged list excludes deletions so a staged deletion is never linted.

Three items adjudicate as documented rather than as findings. The fail-open on a missing tool is the ADR's decision (`docs/adr/git_hooks.md`: the hook prints a note and allows the commit, while `scripts/manual/install_host_git_hooks.sh` fails closed for a host install and offers `--skip-tool-check`). The partial-staging caveat is stated in the ADR's consequences. The severity (`-S warning`), the `--no-globs` flag, and the staged-only filter all match the ADR's second decision entry, and the file sits inside `scripts/check_shell.sh`'s scan set (`src` is in `SCAN_DIRS`), so the repo gate lints it.

Units. Nine: the hook blocks a staged Markdown finding and names the bypass, passes a clean Markdown commit, blocks a staged ShellCheck finding and names both the gate and the bypass, passes a clean shell commit, runs both gates on a mixed commit, never invokes the Markdown linter for a non-Markdown commit, never invokes ShellCheck for a non-shell commit, and two entrypoint units assert that copy delivery installs an executable hook while mount delivery installs none. The two linter stubs record that they were called, never with which arguments.

Bites (11 mutations, H1-H11, each run against the full suite). Proven, five: H2 stops the Markdown finding setting the flag; H3 does the same for ShellCheck; H4 removes the final non-zero exit; H5 turns `git diff --cached` into the unstaged diff, after which a staged change is invisible; H11 removes the bypass hint from the failure text. Survived, six: H1 adds deletions to the filter; H6 and H7 drop the file list from each linter call; H8 drops `--no-globs`; H9 rewrites the directive-parse match; H10 makes the missing-tool branch fail closed. Findings 101-104 record them.

### `src/capability/entrypoint.sh` -- `tests/test_capability_entrypoint_mount.sh` (3 units) and two units in `tests/test_git_hook.sh`

375 lines, three local functions (`_preflight_crit`, `_preflight_warn`, `_session_export`). The body resolves paths, runs `lib_preflight`, sources the record and snapshot libraries, branches on the delivery type to validate or initialise the volume, installs the git hook for copy delivery, runs the preflight checks, sources the diff pipeline, registers the EXIT and TERM traps, starts the autosave loop, runs an optional start-up command, and then stays alive on `sleep infinity` so bash remains PID 1 and receives the stop signal.

The design reads well and several details are deliberate and correct: `exec 1>&2` keeps container output out of the agent's channel; `sleep infinity & wait $!` keeps the trap-bearing shell as PID 1, with a comment explaining why a foreground `sleep` would bypass the TERM trap; the `_preflight_crit` capture idiom (`2>&1 >/dev/null`) takes the command's stderr and discards its stdout; the mount branch writes the `SESSION_STATE` init marker only when it is absent, so an attach preserves identity; and `_session_export` guards with `session_export_needed` so a no-work session skips the durable export while an autosave cannot suppress it.

Units. Five cover this file, and they are behavioural rather than unit-shaped: three run the real entrypoint under mount delivery (fail-closed when the worktree has no `.git`; the first run writes the `SESSION_STATE` init marker with identity and workspace path fields, and its `init_sha` resolves to a real commit; an attach re-run preserves existing identity), and two in the hook suite run it under copy delivery, where they wait for the hook install line and then stop the process. Copy delivery's startup beyond the hook install is therefore reached by no unit, and the dry-run capability probe's start-up command is not reached either, because the dry-run trace tests stop at the docker stub.

Bites (15 mutations, E1-E14 plus a corrected E6a, each run against the full suite). Proven, six: E1 removes the mount `.git` fail-closed; E2 removes the init-marker write; E6a removes the mount branch's `changes_dir` write; E7 installs the hook without its execute bit; E8 removes the copy-only condition from the hook install; E10 breaks the hardcoded preflight path, which fails three units. Survived, nine: E3 (the `FLATTEN` init-sha rule), E4 (the empty-`init_sha` abort), E5 (the copy identity-upgrade block), E6 (the copy branch's `changes_dir` write), E9 (the copy unseeded-volume abort), E11 (the preflight verdict gate), E12 (the `AGENT_HOME` tilde), E13 (the export-needed skip), E14 (the start-up command block). Findings 105-112 record them.

### `src/capability/seed_volume.sh` -- `tests/test_seed_volume.sh` (11 units)

226 lines: `die`, `_nul_streams_equal`, `verify_parity`, `verify_baseline`, and `main`. The seeder runs inside the one-shot seeder service with the project mounted read-only at `/src`, transports it into the session volume, and self-verifies before any session container exists. The header states the contract in full and cites the ADR entries each rule derives from, including the reason the volume is mounted at the sandbox service's own target path (ownership is initialised from the image directory, so any other target leaves the volume root-owned and `cp` fails).

Two details are correct rather than notable. `_nul_streams_equal` compares sorted NUL-delimited streams held in files, because a bash variable cannot carry a NUL byte, and it removes both temp files on both paths. And the object-store prune runs only when the `fsck --unreachable` probe finds something, so a clean host repository pays one probe and no `gc`.

Units. Eleven, all behavioural, and the strongest test file in phase 3. Four guard units (submodule, unborn HEAD, linked worktree, tracked sentinel); one rich-fixture parity unit covering a staged edit that must stay staged, a staged new file, an unstaged edit, an unstaged deletion, untracked content, negation patterns, an ignored file, a symlink target, an exec bit, and a unicode filename; an induced-divergence unit that tampers with the volume after the seed; the empty-enumeration case; the stash clear with the host stack asserted untouched; the object prune with a dangling blob asserted absent from the volume and present on the host; the flatten baseline (exactly one commit, clean worktree, `init_sha` equal to that root); and a direct `verify_baseline` unit whose source loses a file after the seed.

Bites (15 mutations, S1-S15, each against the full suite). Proven, six: S2 removes the unborn-HEAD guard; S4 redirects the sentinel path; S5 removes the stash clear; S6 disables the prune block; S11 inverts the `FLATTEN` branch selection; S12 writes the root commit as the full-mode `init_sha` instead of `HEAD`. Survived, nine: S1 the linked-worktree guard and S3 the submodule guard (their units still pass, because the seed fails downstream anyway); S7 the post-prune tripwire; S8 and S9 the two self-verification call sites in `main`; S10 the case-collision call; S13 the no-repo guard; S14 `verify_baseline`'s worktree-clean assertion; S15 the post-clear stash tripwire. Findings 113-115.

### `src/capability/snapshot.sh` -- `tests/test_snapshot_host.sh` (15 units)

223 lines, seven functions, the shared delivery primitive set: `filesystem_tracks_exec_bits`, `snapshot_enumerate_worktree`, `snapshot_copy_git`, `snapshot_baseline_init`, `snapshot_copy_worktree`, `snapshot_deliver`, and `snapshot_check_case_mismatch`. `snapshot_deliver` is the single dispatcher, called by `seed_volume.sh` and by `start_agent.sh`, so the copy seed and the mount materialization share one primitive set rather than two pipelines.

`snapshot_enumerate_worktree` is the single owner of what crosses, and it is the best-pinned primitive in phase 3. Its comment states the two subtleties that matter: git resolves the ignore sources itself, negation patterns included, and the existence filter drops tracked paths that are absent from the disk so the list matches the operator's on-disk state. Breaking the existence filter fails 24 units (P1) and dropping `--exclude-standard` fails 7 (P2). The `if` rather than `&&` inside the loop keeps the loop's status 0 under pipefail when the last path is filtered out, which its comment also records.

The delivery order is mode-specific and correct: full copies `.git` then syncs the worktree, flatten syncs the worktree then inits a baseline. `snapshot_copy_worktree` respects the enumeration rather than rebuilding an exclude list, and the comment names the leak that decision closed (the R1 leak from hand-built rsync excludes, which ignored negation patterns in global excludes). The submodule pre-flight sits here as well as in the seeder; from the seeder the snapshot copy is the second guard, which is why that seeder unit passed with its own guard removed (finding 113, S3).

Bites (10 mutations, P1-P12 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, six: P1 the enumeration's existence filter; P2 `--exclude-standard`; P6 the submodule pre-flight; P8 the flatten baseline init; P10 the full arm's `mkdir -p`; P11 the copy primitive's `mkdir -p`. Survived, four: P3 `cp -a` reduced to `cp -r`; P4 the `core.fileMode` decision forced false; P5 `filesystem_tracks_exec_bits` forced to return 1; P12 `snapshot_deliver`'s documented default (`${3:-false}`) changed to true. Findings 116-119.

---

## Phase 4 -- the make-workflow drivers

Scope: the shell drivers and their test families. Order is leaf-first within the phase. `scripts/build.sh` comes first because it is a closed orchestrator and because it confirms the item left open at finding 91(b). The compose YAML and the capability dockerfile stay excluded by direction.

### `scripts/build.sh` -- `tests/test_trace_build.sh` (7 units), plus dispatch and start units elsewhere

350 lines, four functions plus `main`. Host scripts source it (`start_agent.sh`, `run_agent.sh`, `agent-sandbox.sh`) and `agent-sandbox build` executes it; the standalone guard at the foot enables `set -euo pipefail` with a comment explaining that a direct `bash build.sh` must exercise production's failure-abort semantics. `build_image` runs one docker build; `build_agent` runs the three-tier build (shared base, provider base, provider image); `build_sandbox` builds the capability image; `preflight` checks both images and builds what is missing unless the caller forbids it.

Several details are deliberate and worth stating. `build_image` captures the build status into a `_build_rc` variable initialised to 1, so a path that never runs a build reports failure rather than passing silently, and the `&& ... || ...` capture keeps the build's real status; the `--quiet` choice suppresses the cached-step staircase while leaving failures visible. `preflight` returns 1 rather than exiting, so `resume_agent.sh` can refuse without building. Tiers 1 and 2 carry no contract label while tier 3 does, and the comment gives the reason: the shared bases bake no harness content. Start's `--fast` path passes `build_missing=false` so a fast start cannot silently build behind the flag.

Units. Seven here: five build-command behaviours (images inspected, no compose invoked, a build issued, the descriptive failure message under standalone `set -e`, and the default-target count), the interface-contract refusal and pass unit, and one adjacent `record_image`/`record_provider` unit that belongs to `session_inventory.sh`. `test_dispatch.sh` covers the `build --targets` pass-through, and two `test_start_agent.sh` units pin the `--rebuild` and `--refresh` no-cache plumbing.

Bites (11 mutations, B1-B13 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, three: B5 removes `build_if_missing`'s no-cache bypass, which `test_start_agent.sh`'s rebuild unit catches; B9 redirects the provider glob that the default-target count is measured against; B10 disables the contract-mismatch branch. Survived, eight: B1 `main`'s `REBUILD_FLAG` construction; B2 tier 3's no-cache argument; B4 the explicit `exit 1` and B6 the standalone `set -e`, which each survive alone and together, because the unit named for them asserts only the diagnostic text; B7 `preflight`'s build-or-refuse branch; B8 `main`'s required-flag check; B12 `build_agent`'s Dockerfile validation. Findings 120-125.

### `scripts/install.sh` -- `tests/test_install.sh` (5 units)

148 lines: six probe functions (`check_bash_version`, `check_git`, `check_gnu_readlink`, `check_sha256sum`, `check_gnu_date`, `check_gnu_sed`) plus `install_os`, `install_dir`, `do_install`, `do_uninstall`, and `install_main`. It is the host requirement gate and the CLI symlink installer, run by the Makefile's `install` and `uninstall` targets and sourced by its tests.

The gate states its own governance: the comment names `docs/development/host_requirements.md` as the source of truth and `scripts/macos_bootstrap.sh` as the macOS enforcer, and requires a new requirement to be added to all three places; `macos_bootstrap.sh` l.25 states the same rule from the other side. Each probe targets one GNU/BSD difference and uses only tools present on both platforms (`grep`, or none at all), which is what lets the same file run the gate on either host. The `INSTALL_OS` seam exists so the tests can exercise the Darwin branch on Linux.

Bites (12 mutations, J1-J12, each against the full suite). Proven, six: J2 ignores the `INSTALL_OS` seam, so the Darwin units take the Linux path; J4 points `do_uninstall` at a filename that is not the CLI's; J5 makes the failure gate unreachable; J6 stops the git probe detecting absence; J8 ignores the `INSTALL_DIR` override; J11 compares the OS against `darwin` rather than `Darwin`, so the Darwin checks never run. Survived, six: J1 lowers the bash floor; J3 drops `-f` from the symlink; J7 disables the GNU-sed probe; J9 drops the tilde expansion; J10 rewrites the `.env` key; J12 disables the standalone entry guard. Findings 126-129.

### `scripts/stop.sh` -- `tests/test_trace_stop.sh` (10 stop units of its 14; the other four are prune units)

143 lines: one `usage` function and a linear body. It stops a session by label rather than by compose, and the header states why that matters: it "does not invoke docker compose and does not require resolved compose environment variables", so it still works when the compose environment is gone. The filter set (project-name plus sandbox-dir, plus session-id when given) is the same label-addressing scheme that prune and onboarding use, which is what makes a stop addressable without parsing a project name.

Two details are recorded lessons rather than restatements. The container capture uses command substitution, with the reason in the comment: `mapfile < <(cmd)` swallows the command's failure status under `set -euo pipefail`, so a docker probe failure would read as "no containers". The empty capture is normalised afterwards because a here-string yields one empty element. And `docker rm` tolerates failure with its reason stated: run_agent's EXIT-trap `compose down` may already be removing the same containers, so "already in progress" must not abort the stop, while `docker stop` stays fail-closed. The sandbox directory is canonicalised once before the filters are built, with a loud failure, so the filter matches the label baked at create time whatever the path spelling.

Units. Ten: no compose invoked; no containers means no teardown; containers stopped and removed; networks removed by label; a failing `docker ps` aborts; the removal race is tolerated; prune delegates without compose; prune reaches the registry-based path; the shutdown hints; and the draft hint suppressed without an export. The four remaining units in this file cover `prune.sh`, so their read and their BDD blocks belong to the next file's pass.

Bites (11 mutations, S1-S11, each against the full suite). Proven, four: S2 removes the `docker rm` tolerance; S4 removes the empty-capture normalisation, which the no-containers unit catches; S6 removes the hint call (two units); S10 redirects the prune delegation (two units). Survived, seven: S1 the help handler line, which is dead by finding 130; S3 adds tolerance to `docker stop`; S5 removes the network-removal tolerance; S7 the `--prune requires --project` guard; S8 the canonicalisation failure path; S9 the sandbox-dir filter value; S11 the session-id filter value. Findings 130-135.

### `scripts/prune.sh` -- `tests/test_prune.sh` (18 units) plus four prune units in `tests/test_trace_stop.sh`

370 lines: ten functions. `main` sources three libs, parses, canonicalises the sandbox path, validates, and runs the two-rule pass. The header states the invariant that governs the file: the prune outcome is a partition of sessions - every session ends fully pruned (record and resources gone) or fully kept (record and resources intact), never partial and never collateral. `rule1_selected_records` prints the selected stale records; `_sid_is_orphaned`, `_session_id_of`, and `collect_orphans` build Rule 2's orphan set; `rule2_orphan_resources` runs the three docker label queries; `show_rule1` and `show_rule2` render the preview.

Three details are deliberate and worth stating. The sequential design is stated in the header: Rule 1 deletes the records, then Rule 2 re-derives its orphan list against the updated registry, so the two rules share no in-memory contract - the registry is the only truth at each step, and a stale record's resources become orphans in the same pass. `unknown` is the third staleness value and it is a safety value: `session_stale` returns it when a record carries no `host-head-sha` or the current HEAD cannot be determined, and Rule 1 selects only the exact literal `stale`, so a `--project` that is not a git repository prunes nothing. `TREATED_REMOVED_SIDS` exists because a preview cannot delete-then-rescan, so the preview treats the Rule-1-selected sessions as already removed while the real pass clears the set and re-scans.

The tolerant parse is the file's one deliberate loosening: `_CLI_TOLERANT=1` puts `parse_args` in drop mode, where an unmatched argument is silently ignored. `prune.sh` is the only script in the tree that sets it.

Units. Eighteen in `tests/test_prune.sh`: thirteen drive the script end to end through a fixture with a real git project, and five pin `env_field`, the record-env parser from `session_inventory.sh`. Four more units sit in `tests/test_trace_stop.sh`, where two further units pin the `stop.sh` delegation. Inventory correction: the earlier phase-4 list recorded only the four units in the trace file and omitted `tests/test_prune.sh` altogether.

Bites (14 mutations, P1-P14, each against the full suite). Proven, six: P1 disables the age cutoff, which the recent-record unit catches; P2 makes the record removal a no-op; P9 removes the `--project` requirement; P10 makes the `--stale` validation accept everything; P11 drops the interactive abort gate; P12 removes the volume removal branch. Survived, eight: P3 the predictive preview's treated-removed set; P4 the empty session-id guard; P5 the `--age-days` integer validation; P6 the canonicalisation failure path; P7 an added bogus docker filter; P8 the `docker stop` before `rm`; P13 the staleness comparison widened to `!= fresh`; P14 the tolerant parse itself. Seven of the eight survivors were then probe-verified in a throwaway harness, and every observation is a finding. Findings 137-151.

### `scripts/onboard.sh` -- `tests/test_onboard.sh` (15 units)

470 lines, and the largest driver read so far: twelve functions across four blocks (helpers, refresh, provider provisioning, onboard), with `main` as the entry point. It is the only script that creates a sandbox in the first place, so it owns the output tree that every other driver later depends on: the Makefile, `.env`, `.workspace/{input,output,session-diffs}`, and one `.<provider>/` directory per provider.

Two design choices carry the file. The refresh mode splits the sandbox into versioned template files that it regenerates and `.env` values that it preserves, and the comment states the exception and its reason: `INSTALL_DIR` is operator configuration with no `--install-dir` input to sync it from, so clobbering it would silently undo an override. The other is the `_HAS_SIDE_EFFECTS` flag and the `ERR` trap: it is set only after the first `mkdir`, so the partial-state warning fires for a failure that leaves files behind and stays silent for a failure before that.

Three structural facts are worth stating. `_provision_providers` is a single loop over `src/reasoning/providers/*/` that appends `.env.example` stubs, seeds `config/` with `rsync`, renames `env.stub` to `.env`, and sources a provider's own `onboard.sh` hook in-process; the hook for `pi` is live and creates `.pi/agent/{sessions,prompts,skills,extensions}`. `resolve_and_validate` is the only path-style gate in the tree, and it tests the *resolved* value rather than the value the operator typed. And `--yes` and the tty check are two independent writers of `_INTERACTIVE`.

Units. Fifteen, and they are behavioural by intent: the header states that they "assert the contract (what files exist, what `.env` contains) not the internal implementation". Eleven cover the two modes end to end, two cover `template_version`, and two pin the Makefile template's thin-target contract, which is the template's business rather than onboard's. The file's own comment records a harness hazard: a `read` inside onboard can consume the test runner's stdin, so one unit redirects `/dev/null` to avoid flakiness. The middle `source "$REPO_ROOT/scripts/onboard.sh"` also changes the test shell's options (finding 163).

Bites (14 mutations, O1-O18 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, four: O1 drops `template_version`'s `|| true` absorb, which reddens all three version units; O2 disables the clobber guard; O5 disables the pre-P1 migration; O18 stops writing `MAKEFILE_VERSION`. Survived, ten: O3 the Windows-path check, which is unreachable rather than untested (finding 152); O4 the absolute-path check; O7 the non-tty branch; O8 the declined-answer branch; O10 the `--yes` branch; O11 the refresh `PROJECT_DIR` derivation; O12 the `.env.example` append, which is dead code; O13 the `env.stub` rename; O14 the provider hook; O15 the `rsync --chmod` mode contract, which is not delivered. Ten probes followed in a throwaway harness, using `script -q -c` for the interactive branches. Findings 152-164.

### `scripts/start_agent.sh` -- `tests/test_start_agent.sh` (28 units)

525 lines, four functions plus `main`. It is the host-side pre-flight for a session and then an `exec` into `run_agent.sh`: path validation, `.env` loading through `session_env_common_init`, fresh session identity, the workspace directories and mount materialization, the optional build, the preflight, and the dispatch. The header states the boundary plainly - "Compose generation and container lifecycle are owned by scripts/run_agent.sh" - and it is execute-only by design, because it exports variables for the child and its `exec` discards the parent shell.

Four things about the file are worth recording. `parse_help_flag` runs before any mode or flag validation and exits 0, which makes this the one leaf that gets the help path right (finding 173, and the pattern row 130 asks for). The `--delivery` and `--flatten` inputs are parsed once at ingestion and passed down as explicit arguments, with a comment that states the rule: they are command inputs, never ambient environment state, and resume recovers them from the persisted record. The wizard's `_start_providers` filter mirrors `build_agent`'s dockerfile check, so the menu offers only providers that can build. And the mount branch is the most defensive code in phase 4: it refuses an unborn HEAD, refuses a worktree with no recorded history mode, refuses a recorded mode that is not `true` or `false`, and refuses a flatten request against a full worktree.

Units. Twenty-eight, all in `tests/test_start_agent.sh`, and they are behavioural: the fixture builds a sandbox tree, a committed project, and the stub images' contract label, then asserts on the docker trace and the persisted compose file. One unit family covers the build policy (default, `--refresh`, `--rebuild`, the three illegal combinations), one the rendered compose contract, one the help and error surfaces, one the wizard's three paths (abort, no-re-prompt, accept), and seven the mount delivery. The trace family `tests/test_trace_start.sh` is not start's: both of its helpers run `run_agent.sh` (finding 172).

Bites (16 mutations, S1-S17 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, ten: S1 the WSL path guard; S3 the dry-run always-rebuild default; S4 the `--fast` plus `--rebuild` refusal; S5 the `--refresh` refusal for dry-run; S6 the `--fast` refusal outside dry-run; S7 the `--provider` requirement; S9 the dry-run session-id prefix; S12 the recorded-mode validation; S13 the flatten mismatch refusal; S17 `parse_help_flag`. Survived, six: S2 the `--delivery` case, which is duplicated downstream with identical text; S8 the derived sandbox-name suffix; S10 the canonicalisation failure path, which a later guard catches; S11 the mount unborn-HEAD check, which the earlier session-env gate already covers; S14 the wizard's standard-mode restriction; S15 the wizard's dockerfile filter, which no provider in the tree can exercise. Four probes followed. Findings 165-173.

### `scripts/run_agent.sh` -- `tests/test_run_agent.sh` (8 units) and `tests/test_trace_start.sh` (23 units)

447 lines, two functions (`_session_cleanup`, `seed_sandbox_volume`) and an otherwise linear body. It owns the container lifecycle for one session: flag parse, the provider setup hook, the provider config directory, host identity, the compose file set (base plus delivery overlay, optional provider overlay, mode overlay), the persisted compose record, the volume seed, the mode dispatch, the run, and the exit trap that tears the session down. Its header states the boundary with `start_agent.sh`: compose generation and container lifecycle are owned here.

Five things about the file are worth recording. `parse_args usage ...` names a `usage` function this script never defines; the only definition in its source set is `scripts/build.sh`'s, sourced for `build_sandbox` and `preflight`, so `run_agent.sh standard --help` prints build's usage text and exits 2 (finding 174, and the second measured site of row 130's dead help handler). The `""` arm of the delivery `case` is unreachable while the required-delivery guard at l.120 stands, and that guard is what turns a forgotten `--delivery` into an error: with the guard disabled the same command proceeds to start the agent (probe), so the arm documents an input the file refuses to accept (finding 175). The provider setup hook is sourced in-process, so the header's abort contract holds for a hook that returns non-zero but not for one that calls `exit` (finding 176), and the `mkdir` that pre-creates the provider config directory is redundant for the one provider that ships a hook and load-bearing for the two that do not (finding 177). `export FLATTEN` is read by no child process: the compose record gets the value from `compose.sh`'s generation-time substitution and the seeder from the yml's `SEED_FLATTEN`, so the export and its comment describe a mechanism the code does not use (finding 180). And the session's unattended-path guards (`-T` on the seeder, the two `< /dev/null`, `--progress quiet`) are cited to a live-observed hang and a compose prompt that the docker stub cannot reproduce (findings 178, 185).

Units. Eight in `tests/test_run_agent.sh`, all behavioural: the provider setup hook absent, present, and failing; `--flatten` acceptance; the provider overlay present and absent in the compose file set; and the SERVE_PORT warning in both modes. Twenty-three in `tests/test_trace_start.sh`, which the previous pass re-homed to this file (finding 172): the teardown verb per mode, teardown-is-last, three failure paths with the trap, the persisted compose file, and the three delivery-selection units. The two families divide cleanly, and they are thin in two specific ways: no unit observes the seeder path (bite R10 showed that its failure branch is pinned from `tests/test_resume.sh`, not from either family), and three trace units assert only that the final compose operation is `down`, an outcome every session in that file produces, so the sandbox health gate can be removed with the suite green (finding 181).

Bites (26 mutations, R1-R28 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, twelve: R3c the required-delivery guard; R5 the hook presence check; R6 the hook failure inversion; R10 the half-seeded volume discard, caught by `tests/test_resume.sh`; R12 arming teardown before the run; R14 `exit "$agent_rc"`; R18 `up -d sandbox`; R19 the compose record's identity-derived name; R20 the `last_started` activity log, caught by the resume trace family; R22 the mount overlay selection; R24 the serve-only SERVE_PORT warning gate; R25 the SERVE_PORT default. Survived, thirteen: R1 the dead help handler; R2b the dead `""` arm; R7 the provider config directory; R8 the seeder's `-T`; R9 the seeder timeout attribution; R13 the agent-exit capture, which is equivalent because `set -e` exits with the same status and the trap runs either way; R15 the `docker wait` swallow, invisible because the stub's wait always exits 0; R17 the sandbox health gate; R21 the compose template guard; R23 the `export FLATTEN`; R27 the dry-run overlay guard; R28 `realpath` on the dry-run script paths. Ten probes followed, one of them a bash-semantics probe on the hook construct. Findings 174-186.

One bite needs a note for the record: R3's first form was `if [[ -z "$DELIVERY" && false ]]`, which is a no-op inside `[[ ]]`, so the mutation left the guard working and the bite reported a survivor that was not one. The corrected mutation (R3c) proves the guard. The Format section now carries the rule.

### `scripts/resume_agent.sh` -- `tests/test_resume.sh` (19 units) and `tests/test_trace_resume.sh` (10 units)

258 lines, one function (`usage`) and a linear body. It continues an existing session: it parses the resume flags, canonicalises the sandbox dir, dispatches between `--list`, `--interactive`, and `--session-id`, recovers identity from the `.compose/<session-id>.yml` record (the provider from the agent image line, delivery and FLATTEN from the record's environment literals, `SESSION_TS` and `HOST_HEAD_SHA` from the session labels), runs the shared host-side prelude, cross-checks a mount worktree's recorded history mode, runs preflight with `build_missing=false`, and `exec`s `run_agent.sh standard`.

Five things about the file are worth recording. The record is the only source of identity, and the two fail-closed rules over its literals are pinned (bites A7 and A9). The provider recovery from the agent image line is not: swapping `$local_provider` for `$PROVIDER_NAME` - which resume never sets before that call, because `session_env_names` is what exports it - aborts the run at line 230 under `set -u` with "PROVIDER_NAME: unbound variable", while the pristine exec argv carries `--provider=pi` (finding 187). The `--env` flag and the `--flatten` forwarding are both live and both unpinned, because every fixture uses the default `.env` and the flatten unit asserts acceptance rather than forwarding (findings 189, 190; the `bash -x` exec argv differs in both cases). The two interactive abort legs - the picker's `|| exit 1` and the confirm's `if ! ...` - are unpinned, and the unit named for the abort cannot see them: its non-zero rc comes from the later preflight failure on that unit's PATH, and its "aborted" text is printed by the confirm helper on both paths, so inverting the gate lets a declined confirmation resume and the unit stays green (finding 188). And the file hard-codes `standard` as the mode it resumes into, which no document states (finding 198).

Units. Nineteen in `tests/test_resume.sh`, all display-and-verdict units over synthetic records: the enriched list, the provider filter and its no-match error, the bare and unknown-flag help paths, the interactive abort and the empty inventory, the provider-alone guidance, the missing record, the staleness label, the provider cell with and without an image-sig field, the page cap, picker pagination, the commit-distance AGE column, the current-branch hint in three shapes, the zero-padded index, and the STATE cell. Ten in `tests/test_trace_resume.sh`, the execution-path family: copy and mount resume keep their storage, the flatten mismatch warns, delivery is recovered with no ambient value, the two fail-closed rejections, the session-id round trip, the flatten acceptance, and the activity log. The display family is the strongest in phase 4; the execution family is thin exactly where identity is recovered.

Bites (31 mutations, A1-A28 selected, each against the full suite). Proven, twelve: A2b the `--provider` misuse guard; A5 the missing-record guard; A7 the delivery validation; A9 the FLATTEN validation; A10 the flatten default; A11 the mismatch warning; A16 the page size; A19 the recovered delivery; A20 the list remainder footer; A22 the `--list` branch; A23 the `--interactive` branch; A24 the empty-inventory refusal. Survived, fourteen: A1 the canonicalisation guard, which degrades to a misleading message when tolerated; A3b and A4b the no-target and no-sandbox guards; A6 the provider recovery; A8b the ambient-SANDBOX_TYPE warning; A12 the `build_missing=false` contract; A13 the workspace directories; A14 the provider argument; A15b the session-ts label; A17 the flatten forwarding; A18 the worktree-config existence test, which is defensive rather than load-bearing; A21 the `--env` value; A27 the picker abort; A28 the confirm gate. Ten probes followed. Findings 187-198.

Two methodology notes. Three bites (A2, A3, A4) used the ineffective `[[ ... && false ]]` form from the previous file and had to be redone, and one probe used `-n /dev/null`, which is likewise always true; the Format section's rule covers both. And this pass found an ambient leak in the harness: the shell that runs these probes keeps its exports, so `PROVIDER_NAME=pi` from an earlier fixture survived into a later probe and made a real mutant look equivalent. Probes must `unset` what they assume absent.

### `scripts/agent-sandbox.sh` -- `tests/test_dispatch.sh` (28 units)

279 lines, four file-scope helpers (`require_base_args`, `resolve_identity`, `print_subcommand_list`, `route_help`) and `main`. It is a self-locating route table: it resolves the repository root from its own path (the installed CLI is a symlink into the repo, per `docs/adr/harness_versioning.md`), sources `common.sh`, `cli.sh`, and `env_resolve.sh`, parses four flags of its own (`--env`, `--name`, `--project`, `--sandbox`) into `ENV_REL` and the identity triple, collects every other argument into `PASSTHROUGH`, scans the arguments for `--help`, and `exec`s the leaf for the subcommand.

Four things about the file are worth recording. The help scan sits before the per-case required-argument checks, which is why `agent-sandbox <sub> --help` works without identity flags - the one place the CLI surface gets help right - and `route_help`'s separate copy of the dispatch map is the duplication the CLI-to-workflow note records. The route table forwards identity per branch, and those subsets are unpinned in three directions at once: widening `resume` to `name dir sandbox`, narrowing `apply` to `sandbox`, and replacing `onboard`'s `require_base_args` with resolution all leave the suite green (bites B9, B10, B12), because every fixture supplies the flags or a complete `.env` (finding 206). The subcommand list's content is likewise unasserted: dropping `package-branch` from `print_subcommand_list` fails nothing (bite B6), because every help unit asserts the "Valid subcommands" header only - so the one list that must agree with the dispatch case can drift silently, which is the note's area 1 with evidence (finding 200). And `route_help`'s `resume` arm, the one shape whose file name differs from the subcommand, is absent from the help table and unpinned: pointing it at a non-existent `resume.sh` fails nothing (finding 199).

Units. Twenty-eight, all in `tests/test_dispatch.sh`, and the oracle is a captured `exec`: the harness sources the dispatcher with `AGENT_SANDBOX_REPO` preset, replaces `exec` with a function, and asserts on the argv text. Six units cover the build route and its passthroughs, five the start, dry-run, and serve routes, four the workflow routes, seven the help surface, three the error paths, and the rest identity resolution and the two tree-wide guards. `dispatch_and_capture` discards the exit status (`|| true`), so only one unit in the file asserts a verdict at all (`test_removed_serve_subcommand_is_unknown`); the empty-subcommand guard's `exit 1` is therefore unpinned (finding 202).

Bites (20 mutations, B1-B20, each against the full suite). Proven, ten: B4 the unknown-subcommand arm's `exit 1`; B7 start's `--env` forwarding; B11 `dry-run` in the help router; B13 `stop`'s `--project`; B14 the source guard, whose removal crashes the suite; B15 `help <sub>`'s argument; B16 the dispatcher's own `--env` parse; B17 `onboard` in the help router; B18 `readlink -f` for the self-location, which crashed the suite at source time rather than failing a unit; B20 start's mode. Survived, ten: B1 the `resume` help arm; B2 the help scan reading only the first argument; B3 the empty-subcommand verdict; B5 the unknown-subcommand message stream; B6 the subcommand list's content; B8 dry-run's `--env`; B9 the `resume` identity subset; B10 the `apply` identity subset; B12 `onboard`'s hard requirement; B19 the `-h` alias. Findings 199-208.

The pass also corrected the CLI-to-workflow note: its Context claimed `resume` was the only branch forwarding `--env` and its issue table said `--env` reached one workflow. Measured, `--env` is consumed by the interface and forwarded to exactly the two leaves whose parse specs accept it (`start_agent.sh`, serving start and dry-run, and `resume_agent.sh`), and no workflow accepts it - so the forward set follows the leaf contract rather than omission, and the note's option area 2 has one less issue and one more input.

### `scripts/workflows/apply.sh` -- `tests/test_diff_workflow.sh` + `tests/test_apply_count.sh` (22 + 8 units)

224 lines, four functions: `apply_run` (the work), `usage`, `apply_preview` (the interactive branch's summary), and `main` (the entry point). It sources `guards.sh`, `cli.sh`, `session_state.sh`, and `diff.sh`, so the patch mechanics themselves (`_apply_patch_file`, `diff_is_empty`, `strip_index_lines`) belong to `diff.sh` and were read in phase 2. This file owns the contract around them: the argument guards, the clean-tree gate, the branch checkout, the count report, and the confirm-then-apply interactive branch.

Four things about the file are worth recording. First, its guards overlap, so each one is individually unobservable: disabling the required-argument check leaves the not-found check to refuse the same input (bite A1), disabling the not-found check leaves `_apply_patch_file` to fail on the same input (A2), and removing `validate_project_dir` leaves the clean-tree read to refuse a missing project (A26) - and every unit that covers these paths asserts only a non-zero status, with stderr discarded, so none can show which guard refused. Second, the suite cannot see the `|| true` that protects the count report from `set -e`: every unit calls `apply_run` inside a command substitution whose failure is handled by `|| RC=$?`, which suppresses errexit inside the subshell, so removing the guard fails nothing (A23) - but the entry point aborts with rc 1 and a truncated report, shown by probe P6. Third, the whole `--interactive` branch is unpinned: disabling it, dropping the abort's status, and forcing its exit status to 0 all pass (A17-A19), and no unit anywhere passes `--interactive` to this script. Fourth, `--sandbox` is parsed, required by the guard, and never read (A13); `--permissive` is documented as "Retry with --recount on failure" while the retry is unconditional and the sibling `draft.sh` calls the flag a no-op, so the flag is accepted-and-ignored by design and the help text is the defect (finding 218).

Units. Twenty-two in `tests/test_diff_workflow.sh`, of which thirteen exercise this file (`apply_run`, `main`, `apply_preview`) and nine exercise `diff.sh` (`_apply_patch_file`, `apply_and_commit`); six more in `tests/test_apply_count.sh`. Six units assert an output contract, six a verdict, three a tree state, and the rest the preview's line format. The oracles are mostly "did the file appear" and "did it fail"; only `test_apply_count.sh` asserts a count value and its single emission, which is the one place the report's line format is pinned. Five of the thirteen call `_apply_patch_file` with a fourth argument that the three-argument signature ignores (finding 220).

Bites (26 mutations, A1-A26, each against the full suite). Proven, eight: A3 the unreadable-tree arm; A4 the dirty-tree arm; A5 the forceless refusal; A6 the force warning; A8 the empty-diff skip; A11 the preview's `a/` strip; A12 the preview's no-changes arm; A14 the `--diff` requirement. Survived, eighteen: A1, A2, A26 the overlapping guards; A7 the existing-branch checkout; A9 the count's double-emit guard; A10 the count's unreachable default; A13 the `--sandbox` clause; A15/A16 the dead help handler and the parse verdict; A17-A19 the interactive branch; A20 the branch forwarding; A21 the force tail text; A22 the usage stream; A23 the count's `|| true`; A25 the stale-lock clear. Probes P2 (the entry point's `--help` exits 2, confirming the row 130 family at this call site), P3 (the double-emit is real: `Done. Files changed: 0` then `0`), P5 (branch forwarding works through the invoked path, so finding 216 is a coverage gap only), P6 (the `|| true` is load-bearing under errexit), P7 (`git apply` to the working tree succeeds with a stale `index.lock` present, so the clearing is unobservable on this path). Findings 209-220.

Two probe lessons belong to `devlog/AGENT_FEEDBACK.md` and are recorded there: the first probe sweep's mutations silently matched nothing because perl interpolates shell variables inside `\Q...\E`, and the probe script lacked the backup comparison that the bite harness had.

### `scripts/workflows/draft.sh` -- `tests/test_draft_workflow.sh` (30 units) + one unit in `tests/test_apply_count.sh`

642 lines, eleven functions, and the largest workflow in the tree. It is layered: `main` (flag parse, source resolution, three entry paths) calls `_run_draft_workflow` (collect, savepoint, create, apply, roll back) which calls `draft_run` (guards, branch name, state) which calls `draft_create_and_init_branch` (guards, checkout, `.draft-state` commit); `draft_apply_patches`, `draft_apply_uncommitted`, `draft_collect_patches`, `_ingest_export_metadata`, `_draft_rollback`, and `_resolve_draft_source` are the leaves. Four things about the file are worth recording.

First, the suite's oracle for the orchestration is a **test-side replica**. `tests/libs/draft_fixtures.sh`'s `_test_draft_run` re-implements the old `draft_run` contract by hand - collect, count, error on zero, author, `draft_run`, apply loop, uncommitted - and diverges from production in three ways: it creates no savepoint tag, it never rolls back, and it errors when the patch count is zero where `_run_draft_workflow` accepts an uncommitted-only source. Twenty-eight of the thirty units go through the replica, so mutating the production guards behind it fails nothing (D6, D25), and the production uncommitted-only path has no unit at all (233). Only two units call `_run_draft_workflow` directly, and those are the two that pin the rollback (D35).

Second, **no unit drives `draft.sh` as a script.** Every draft unit sources the file and calls a function, so `main` is unexercised: its two argument guards, its dead help handler, its channel default, its three entry paths, and its whole interactive branch survive mutation (D37, D38, D39). `apply.sh` has an entry-point unit and `start_agent.sh` has many; this workflow has none.

Third, the usage advertises **`--strict`**, which the parse spec does not accept: probe P3 gets `Unknown argument: --strict` and rc 1. `docs/development/bash-coding-conventions.md` records that `STRICT` was removed in the 2026-08-21 loc-reduction campaign as dead API surface, so this is a leftover of a deliberately removed mechanism - the inverse of `apply.sh`'s `--permissive`, whose sibling `draft.sh` states correctly (finding 218).

Fourth, the **savepoint and rollback** carry a stated invariant that one failure mode breaks. `_draft_rollback` resets to `draft-savepoint` first, then restores the source branch; with the tag missing, probe P4 shows the reset failing (`fatal: ambiguous argument 'draft-savepoint'`) and the function aborting **before** the branch switch, leaving the operator on the partial `draft/*` branch - the exact state the docstring says it prevents. The two rollback units still pass in that condition, because their call sites use `|| true`, which suppresses errexit and lets the later checkout repair the branch. The reset itself is therefore asserted by nothing (234).

One more defect is measured rather than inferred: `_run_draft_workflow` reads its pre-collected patch list from position 9 while both production callers pass it at position 8, so the parameter is always empty and the source is re-enumerated. Probe P2 shows the asymmetry directly - a bogus list at position 8 is ignored and the real patches apply; the same bogus list at position 9 is honoured and the run fails.

Units. Thirty in `tests/test_draft_workflow.sh` and one draft unit in `tests/test_apply_count.sh`. Branch creation and naming take five, `.draft-state` two, the three branch guards five, the metadata ingestion four, the rollback two, the commit-message resolution five, the apply paths two, the tree-state guards two, the uncommitted skip one, and the bundle requirement one. The oracles are commit counts, branch names, `.draft-state` field values, and `assert_contains` on error text; nothing asserts a stdout summary, and no unit asserts an exit status of the script itself.

Bites (40 mutations, D1-D40, each against the full suite). Proven, fifteen: D1/D2 the range filter's two bounds; D5 the collection order; D7 the on-draft guard; D8 the collision guard; D10 the state commit's author; D12 the apply-failure verdict; D13 the commit-message resolution; D15 the empty-uncommitted skip; D21 the HEAD default; D22/D23 the two tree-state arms; D28 the rollback's branch restore; D35 the rollback on an apply failure; D40 the confirm hint's variable name, caught by the tree-wide guard that row 77 installed. Survived, twenty-five: D3/D4 the collect error messages; D6 the patches-dir early return; D9 the fork base; D11 the apply loop's force; D14 the blank-line skip; D16 the uncommitted presence check; D17/D18 the two metadata refusals, which mask each other; D19 the base-resolves check; D20 the divergence warning; D24/D25/D26 `draft_run`'s source, patches, and required-count guards; D27 the branch-name hash length; D29/D30/D33/D34 the savepoint tag's branch guard, creation, pre-deletion, and deletion; D31 the patch-list position; D32 the uncommitted-only path; D36 the uncommitted-failure rollback; D37-D39 `main`'s guard, channel default, and interactive branch. Probes: P1 (the patches-dir early return only suppresses `find`'s stderr; the verdict is unchanged), P2 (the patch-list position), P3 (`--strict` rejected), P4 (the rollback abandons the branch switch when the savepoint is missing), P5 (the uncommitted presence check is load-bearing off-suite, though every fixture supplies the file). Findings 221-241.

### `scripts/workflows/confirm.sh` -- `tests/test_confirm_workflow.sh` (12 units)

194 lines, four functions: `_confirm_into_new_branch` (the NEW-mode path), `confirm_run` (validate, drop `.draft-state`, rebase, fast-forward, delete), `usage`, and `main`. Three things about the file are worth recording, and one of them is the pass's most serious finding.

**The `.draft-state` record is loaded by `eval`.** This pass re-demonstrated the phase-2 finding 38 from the consumer side and gave it its own row, 242. `confirm_run` runs `DRAFT_VALIDATION=$(draft_validate_branch ...)` and then `eval "$DRAFT_VALIDATION"`; `draft_validate_branch` emits each field as a **double-quoted** shell assignment (`printf '%s="%s"\n'`), and double quotes do not neutralise a command substitution. Probe P1 put `drafted_at: $(touch /tmp/confirm_eval_marker)` into the `.draft-state` commit of a draft branch and ran `confirm_run`; the marker file appeared. The record is committed on the draft branch inside the sandbox, and the threat model treats the agent runtime as untrusted, so an edited record executes a command in the operator's host shell when they run `make confirm`. `reject.sh:32` evals the same string, and `tests/test_draft_state.sh:197` evals it too, so the fix is one item across three files plus the emitter (row 242). The phase-2 `draft_state.sh` pass already recorded this as finding 38, with an end-to-end chain probe, and both rows are grouped in the KV family item (row 22).

Second, **the savepoint comment records the lesson that `draft.sh` has not learned.** `confirm_run` holds its rollback target in a local variable, and the comment explains why the previous design's fixed-name tag was rejected: a missing tag "could ... be missing (no .draft-state commit -> hard abort under set -e) or stale (an interrupted prior run) and reset the branch to the wrong commit". That is exactly the pair of hazards row 234 records for `draft.sh`'s `draft-savepoint` tag, stated in the sibling workflow, and probe P4 shows the first hazard leaving the operator on the draft branch. Two confirm units still name a `confirm-savepoint` tag that no code creates: they assert that an absent tag stays absent and a stale one stays untouched, so they pin the removal of the old design rather than today's behaviour (finding 248).

Third, the guards around the work are unexercised in the same way the two apply/draft passes found: project validation, the stale-lock clear, and the validation verdict (C1-C3), the target-exists check (C7), and the whole NEW-mode failure arm (C13, C14) all survive mutation, because the units use valid repositories and never make `git switch -c` fail. The masked draft-branch deletion in NEW mode (C15, `|| true`) sits beside an unguarded one in the standard path (C21): a failed delete is silent in one path and fatal in the other. `--ff-only` (C20) is defensive, since the rebase immediately above guarantees a fast-forward. And `main` is unexercised: no unit drives `confirm.sh` as a script, so its guard and its dead help handler survive (C23, C24).

Units. Twelve, all in `tests/test_confirm_workflow.sh`, all calling `confirm_run` directly. Four cover the standard path (delete, merge, target override, non-draft refusal), two the moved-tip and conflict paths, three the savepoint conditions, and three the NEW mode. The oracles are branch existence, commit reachability, tip preservation, and `assert_contains` on two messages; the closing messages, the conflict hint text, and the NEW-mode failure arm are not asserted.

Bites (25 mutations, C1-C25, each against the full suite). Proven, twelve: C4 the eval (removing it aborts on an unbound variable, which is how it is pinned); C5 the NEW-mode empty-target guard; C6 the NEW-mode existence guard; C8 the merge-target default; C9 the savepoint capture; C10 the drop step; C11 the drop-failure rollback; C12 the NEW-mode dispatch; C16 the rebase step; C17 the conflict abort; C19 the switch to the target; C22 the `NEW_MODE` default. Survived, thirteen: C1-C3 the three pre-work guards, C7 the target-exists check, C13/C14 the NEW-mode failure arm, C15 the masked delete, C18 the conflict hint text, C20 `--ff-only`, C21 the final delete's verdict, C23/C24 `main`, C25 the closing message. Probe P2 counts the four `SANDBOX_DIR` references: the parameter is declared, parsed, guarded for emptiness, and passed in, and never read - the second instance of row 219's hollow requirement. Findings 242-250.

### `scripts/workflows/reject.sh` -- `tests/test_reject_workflow.sh` (4 units)

99 lines, four functions: `reject_run` (validate the project, clear a stale lock, read the draft record through `draft_validate_branch`, switch back to the source branch, force-delete the draft branch), `usage`, `main`, and the exec guard. It is the smallest leaf of the draft lifecycle, and it carries the KV family's second reader `eval` site (`reject.sh:32`, probe P4).

**The residue discard is narrower than its comment.** The fallback that runs `git clean -fd` sits inside the `if ! git checkout` branch, and an untracked file does not block a checkout. Probe P3 put `untracked_residue.txt` in the draft worktree and the file was still there after `reject_run` returned 0, while the comment above the fallback says "Reject discards the draft entirely, so discard the residue too -- the final working-tree changes carry no information once the draft commits are dropped." Unit 1 supplies tracked residue only (an edit to a tracked file), which the force-checkout leg discards, so bites R7 (`clean -fd` dropped) and R14 (the checkout's `2>/dev/null` dropped) both survive. Widening the discard to run unconditionally would delete untracked files the operator may want, so the two options - narrow the comment, or widen the discard - carry a safety trade-off (finding 253).

A second probe round bounds that exposure, and corrects one assumption. `clean -fd` takes no pathspec, so on the blocking path it removes **every** untracked, non-ignored file in the project, not only the one that blocked the switch - probe P7 planted an unrelated `notes_of_mine.txt` beside a tracked blocker and git printed `Removing notes_of_mine.txt` before deleting it. Two bounds follow. An ignored file survives, because `-fd` omits `-x` **and** the ignore rule lives on the source branch where the switch leaves it in place; the first attempt at this probe recorded the file as deleted, and the cause was the probe's own ordering - it committed the rule on the draft branch, so the forced switch removed the rule before `clean -fd` ran. That ordering is a real (if narrow) case: an ignore rule the session added on the draft side protects nothing at reject time. Second, the deletion is visible in the output, but no unit asserts the output and the happy-path callers discard it. Probe P8 confirms the other arm: with no tracked blocker the switch succeeds, the fallback never runs, and both files survive. So the policy today is not "discard the residue" or "keep the residue" - it is "discard every untracked non-ignored file if, and only if, some unrelated tracked path collided with the switch".

**The guards and the entry point are unexercised, as in the two sibling passes.** Bite R1 (the `validate_project_dir` verdict dropped) and R2 (the stale-lock verdict dropped) leave the suite green: every unit builds a valid project and none plants a stale lock, and the refusal messages arrive from the guards rather than from reject (finding 251). Bite R3 removes the `|| return 1` that makes `reject_run` honour `draft_validate_branch`'s verdict, and the suite still passes, because unit 4 asserts the message the sub-function prints - the reject-side instance of row 243, reported there. `main` is unexercised too: R12 (the `rc 2` arm exits 1) and R13 (the `--sandbox` requirement dropped) survive, and no unit drives the file as a script (finding 252). Probe P2 runs the file directly and gets rc 2 with the usage on stdout; that is row 130's dead help conversion, and an instrumented mutant confirms the arm is never reached because `set -e` aborts at the `parse_args` call (probe P5). Hardly a surprise that its `usage` text, correct as it is, is asserted nowhere.

**`--sandbox` is the third hollow requirement.** Probe P1 calls `reject_run "$P" "/nonexistent/sandbox/path"` and gets rc 0 with the draft branch deleted: the parameter is declared, parsed, checked for emptiness in `main`, forwarded, and never read (four references, two expanding) - the reject-side instance of row 219. The header comment names `draft_state.sh` and `guards.sh` and omits `cli.sh`, the third library the file sources (finding 254). The closure itself is complete: no `common.sh` symbol is used, so the suite's extra `source draft.sh` masks no missing dependency. That check is worth keeping in mind for the three leaves - the suite sources a sibling workflow before the subject, so a missing `common.sh` would pass the suite and fail in the dispatcher.

Units. Four, all in `tests/test_reject_workflow.sh`, all calling `reject_run` directly over a `make_draft_fixture` draft created by the draft fixture's `_test_draft_run` replica. Three are the happy path (return to source, delete the branch, discard blocking residue) and one is the refusal. The oracles are the current branch, branch existence, rc, and one `assert_contains`; the three operator messages are asserted nowhere.

| unit | Given / When / Then | what it is supposed to test | bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_reject_discards_uncommitted_draft_residue` | a draft branch with a tracked-file edit that blocks the checkout / `reject_run` / rc 0, source current, draft deleted, discard warning printed | the force-checkout fallback | proven (R6); the `clean -fd` leg and the suppressed stderr are unpinned (R7, R14) |
| `test_reject_returns_to_source` | a draft branch with its work committed / `reject_run` / the source branch is current | the branch switch, and the record it reads | proven (R4) |
| `test_reject_deletes_draft_branch` | the same / `reject_run` / the draft branch is gone | the existence guard and the force delete | proven (R8, R9) |
| `test_reject_rejects_non_draft` | a repository on a non-draft branch / `reject_run` / the output names the refusal | the refusal message, not the caller's honouring of it | no mutation pins it: the guard's verdict can be dropped (R3, row 243) |

Bites (14 mutations, R1-R14, each against the full suite). Proven, four: R4 the `eval` (removing it aborts on an unbound `source_branch`, which is how the phase-2 finding reaches this file); R6 the force-checkout leg; R8 the existence guard; R9 the force delete (`branch -d` refuses the unmerged draft). Survived, ten: R1/R2 the two pre-work guard verdicts, R3 the validation verdict, R5/R10/R11 the three operator messages (row 249), R7 `clean -fd`, R12/R13 `main`, R14 the checkout's stderr suppression. Probes: P1 the unread `--sandbox`; P2/P5 `--help` exits 2 and the arm meant to convert it is unreachable; P3/P7/P8 the three residue outcomes (blocking switch, non-blocking switch, ignored file); P4 the second `eval` site; P6 the same rc 2 at all four workflow leaves and through the dispatcher. Findings 251-254.

---

### `scripts/workflows/interactive.sh` -- `tests/test_interactive_session_select.sh` (32 units)

476 lines, four functions plus one private helper: `interactive_pick` (the numbered picker, with pagination and option-0 injection), `interactive_confirm_or_abort` (the y/N prompt), `interactive_select_channel` (the draft channel list), `interactive_select_bundle` (the bundle table), and `_resolve_channel_dir`. It is a library, not a leaf. It defines no `usage` and no `main`, `route_help`'s whitelist keeps `interactive` out of the subcommand table, and `bash scripts/workflows/interactive.sh --help` therefore exits 0 with no output (probe P5). Its five callers are `prune.sh`, `resume_agent.sh`, `start_agent.sh`, `apply.sh`, and `draft.sh`, three of them outside the workflow directory (row 148). It sources four libraries; `routing.sh` for `dirs_resolve` and `resolve_channel_base_dir`, `common.sh` for `INTERACTIVE_MAX_ENTRIES`, `session_inventory.sh` for `relative_time`, `relative_time_compact`, `project_branch_age`, and `project_current_branch`, and `export_status.sh` for `export_status_read`.

**The picker's stated EOF contract is not delivered.** `read -r -p "$PROMPT" REPLY || true` discards the read status, and the branch it guards treats an empty `REPLY` as a failed selection. Pressing Enter with no default prints "Invalid selection. Try again." and re-prompts, which is correct. Reaching end of input does the same thing forever: probe P1 fed `interactive_pick` a closed stdin with two entries and no default and it wrote 240,014 copies of the message (2.8 MB) in three seconds without returning. Probe P2 repeated it with a default and got the default, rc 0, so the loop needs the no-default case. The docstring promises "1 on abort (q/Q or EOF)"; q/Q returns 1 (unit 4), EOF does not. Both bites on this path survive - I1 drops the `|| true` guard and I2 turns the `continue` into `return 1` - because no unit reaches the no-default empty-entry branch (finding 256).

**The docstrings name two surfaces the code no longer has.** `interactive_select_channel`'s docstring lists `SUBCOMMAND -- "apply" or "draft"` and the file header describes the file as apply-and-draft helpers, but the `case` handles `draft` only; probe P3 gets rc 1 and `Error: unknown subcommand: apply`. Two channel lists name `diffs` (`interactive_select_channel`'s return-value examples and `interactive_select_bundle`'s `CHANNEL` arg), and `resolve_channel_base_dir` accepts `session`, `autosave`, and `bundles` only. Both surfaces were removed in the bundle refactor, so the code is correct and the prose is stale (finding 257). Bite I8 survives: the unknown-subcommand guard's `return 1` becomes `return 0` with the suite green.

**The file's self-description is a revision behind.** The header names `libs/interactive_session_select.sh` as its subject, three of the four functions (no `interactive_pick`), and one of the four sourced libraries (`libs/routing.sh`). `_resolve_channel_dir`'s comment claims a side effect the function does not have: it forwards to `resolve_channel_base_dir` and sets nothing, and the caller must have run `dirs_resolve` already. `tests/test_interactive_session_select.sh` line 3 repeats the old path. This is the third file in the read-through whose own header drifted - `reject.sh` (254) and `prune.sh`'s partition note are the others - and the pre-move instances join the post-move reference-drift item (finding 258).

**The picker's display and the autosave channel are the coverage holes.** The page cap, the entry numbering, the option-0 injection, the STATE cell, the patch count, and the column header are all pinned: I3 (a channel-list order change), I9 (the page step), I10 (the patch count), I11 (the STATE cell), and I12 (the header print) each fail a unit. The rendering around them is not. Three mutations survive: the BUNDLE column width 34 to 50 (I5), the uncommitted marker `[x]` to `[X]` (I4), and the AGE cell's `exported` prefix (I6). The unit named for the availability indicators asserts only that entry 1 is the newest, and the truncation unit asserts only that some `...` appears - its title and comment say "exceeds 50 chars" while the column is 34. The autosave channel is worse: every picker unit uses `session`, and its two ordering mechanisms survive, the bundle picker's mtime sort (I13) and the channel picker's newest-saved relative-time cell (I14). A third uncovered behaviour is the non-tty warning, which the docstring states and which every piped unit would print but none asserts (I7 survives). These are findings 260, 261, and 263.

**Two observations about the suite, one of them a defect in the runner.** The timeout bite I9 exposed `scripts/run_tests.sh`'s arithmetic: with the page step doubled, `test_interactive_session_select.sh` and `test_resume.sh` (the second consumer of the picker through `resume --interactive`) both exceeded the 5s deadline, the run printed both `TIMEOUT` lines, then reported `671 tests across 58 files, 671 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped`, and exited 1. A timed-out file contributes nothing to any column, so the summary loses its units and says zero failed; only the exit status and the stderr lines carry the timeout (finding 262). The second observation is not a finding: `interactive_confirm_or_abort`'s `for item in "$@"` leaves `item` global in the caller's shell, and no caller of the five reads `item`, so it is hygiene rather than a defect. `interactive_pick` is reached only through the two selectors; it has no direct unit, which is adequate while its contract is the bundle table it renders.

Units. Thirty-two, all in `tests/test_interactive_session_select.sh`, driven by piped stdin and two fixture helpers (`make_session_fixture`, `git_fixtures.sh`). Seven cover `interactive_confirm_or_abort`, five the channel picker, eight the bundle picker's list and columns, five option-0 injection, six pagination, and two the STATE/AGE and branch-hint columns. The oracles are the returned value, the rc, and `grep` over captured stderr; one unit (`test_select_session_state_and_age_columns`) reads the header and a cell.

| unit | Given / When / Then | what it is supposed to test | bite |
|---|---|---|---|

Bites I10 and I13 needed a second attempt: the first OLD strings quoted with double quotes did not match the file's single-quoted literals, so the `cmp` NO-OP guard reported them rather than counting them as survivals.

Probes (interactive.sh): P1 the picker's EOF loop without a default (240,014 stderr lines in 3s, killed by `timeout`); P2 the same input with a default (the default, rc 0); P3 `interactive_select_channel apply` (rc 1, `unknown subcommand`); P4 the picker with an unknown array name (`No items available.`, rc 1); P5 the file run as a script with `--help` (rc 0, no output); P6 the run behind bite I9 (two `TIMEOUT` lines, `671 tests ... 0 failed`, exit 1). Findings 256-263.
