### `scripts/templates/Makefile.template` -- `tests/test_onboard.sh` (5 of its 15 units read the template)

369 lines, one file, copied by `onboard.sh` into every onboarded project as `Makefile`. It declares 17 variables with `?=`, translates 16 of them into flags through named `$(if)` variables plus five more inline in the recipes, and defines 13 targets. It is the operator's primary interface, and it is the only file in the pass whose recipes no unit executes.

**The argument surface is undeclared against the convention that governs it.** `docs/development/interface-conventions.md` section 8 states two MUSTs: guard the known misused names, and declare every accepted variable with `?=` at the top "so they are visible in one place". Fourteen user variables are read by a recipe and missing from the block: `SERVE`, `FAST`, `LIST`, `PRUNE`, `PERMISSIVE`, `FORCE`, `SANDBOX_TYPE`, `FLATTEN`, `BRANCH`, `BRANCH_FROM`, `DIFFS`, `BRANCH_SUMMARY`, `NO_RENAMES`, and `WORKTREE_DIR`. `ALL` is declared and read by nothing (finding 267). The guard half is applied to three names - `CHANNEL`, `STALE_ONLY`, and `NEW_BRANCH` - and the target-scoped names that section 8's own rationale is about are unguarded: `make dry-run DRY_RUN=1` is silently ignored because the `dry-run:` target reads `FAST` only, and the same holds for `make start FAST=1` and `make build INTERACTIVE=1` (finding 268). Bites M3 (the `CHANNEL` guard deleted) and M4 (apply's `FORCE` translator deleted) both survive.

**The recipes are unpinned; the thin-CLI property is not.** `test_run_targets_are_thin` and `test_start_target_is_thin` extract the `stop` and `start` blocks and assert `--env=$(ENV_FILE)` and no identity flag. Those two units are the only ones that read a recipe, and their mutations are the only two of the eleven that fail (M1, M2). The nine survivors cover every other surface: apply's `FORCE` translator (M4), start's `INTERACTIVE_FLAG` (M11), the `.PHONY` list (M5), the `DRAFT_CHANNEL` default (M6), the version marker's value (M7), the duplicated refresh comment (M8), the `FORCE` help line (M9), and `ENV_FILE`'s path (M10). `refresh:` is the documented exception to the thin contract: it passes identity flags to `onboard --refresh`, and the test file's own comment states that exemption (finding 269).

**The help block carries three drifts.** `INTERACTIVE` is documented once, as start's config wizard, while the same variable drives resume, prune, draft, and apply (finding 271). `FORCE` is documented as "apply with --reject (draft/apply)", which is draft's meaning; apply's `--force` is the dirty-tree tolerance and reject's is a third meaning (finding 272). The "Harness maintenance / refresh" comment block appears twice, once above the package-exports section and once above the target it describes (finding 273).

**The version marker and the duplicated default.** Line 2 carries `# agent-sandbox template version: 5`; `onboard.sh`'s `template_version` parses it into `.env` as `MAKEFILE_VERSION`, and `docs/concepts/sandbox_host_interface.md` records that the marker has no consumer today. Only numeric-ness is asserted, so the value is unpinned (M7). The draft channel is the other duplicated default: `DRAFT_CHANNEL := $(if $(FROM),$(FROM),session)` always passes `--channel=`, so the Makefile's default overrides `draft.sh`'s, and a change to the leaf's default would be silently ignored through the Make interface (finding 274).

Units. Fifteen in `tests/test_onboard.sh`, five of which read the template: three `template_version` units and the two thin-CLI units. No unit runs `make`, so no recipe is expanded anywhere in the suite, and `make` is absent in this container, so recipe expansion could not be exercised by hand either.

| unit | Given / When / Then | what it is supposed to test | bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_template_version_reads_marker_line` | a fixture whose first line is the marker / `template_version` reads it / prints 3 | the marker parse | not mutated |
| `test_template_version_absent_marker_is_empty_and_clean` | a fixture with no marker / `template_version` reads it / empty output, rc 0 | an absent marker is not a failure | not mutated |
| `test_template_version_real_makefile_template_parses` | the shipped template / `template_version` reads it / matches `^[0-9]+$` | the shipped marker parses | survived (M7): the value is unasserted |
| `test_run_targets_are_thin` | the shipped template's `stop` block / extracted and compared / carries `--env`, no identity flags | the thin-CLI contract for `stop` | proven (M1) |
| `test_start_target_is_thin` | the shipped template's `start` block / extracted and compared / carries `--env`, no identity flags | the thin-CLI contract for `start` | proven (M2) |

Bites (11 mutations, M1-M11, each against the full suite). Proven, two: M1 adds `--project=$(PROJECT_DIR)` to `stop` and fails `test_run_targets_are_thin`; M2 adds `--sandbox=$(SANDBOX_DIR)` to `start` and fails `test_start_target_is_thin`. Survived, nine: M3 the `CHANNEL` guard block, M4 apply's `FORCE` translator, M5 the `.PHONY` list, M6 the `DRAFT_CHANNEL` default, M7 the version value, M8 the duplicate comment, M9 the `FORCE` help line, M10 `ENV_FILE`'s path, M11 start's `INTERACTIVE_FLAG`. M11's first run was recorded as PROVEN through the liveness gate; two re-runs gave 722/722 with the gate reporting zero findings, so the mutation survives and the first verdict was the false positive now recorded on row 135.

### `scripts/guards.sh` -- `tests/test_guards.sh` (4 units, plus 5 in other suites)

88 lines, five functions, no `main`. The header's consumer list is wrong in both directions: it names `agent-sandbox.sh`, which sources only `common.sh`, `cli.sh`, and `env_resolve.sh`, and it omits `confirm.sh` and `reject.sh` (finding 283). The four workflow leaves each source the file and call `draft_clear_stale_lock` as the second pre-work guard; `apply.sh` and `draft.sh` additionally call `require_clean_working_tree`.

**`require_clean_working_tree` is the best-pinned function in the file, and half its stated contract is unpinned.** The three verdicts are pinned hard: bite M1 (invert the final test) fails 33 units including `test_clean_tree_guard_is_three_valued`, M2 (`return 2` to `return 1`) fails three, and M14 (drop `-C "$dir"`) fails 32. The silence half, which the docstring and the 2026-09-19 ADR entry both require so the caller owns the message, has no unit: bite M3 adds an unconditional stderr trace and the suite stays 722/722 (finding 275). The hint functions are the mirror image: their pairing to the verdict is pinned (M6 replaces the unreadable hint with the dirty hint and fails the two caller units, and one line of `clean_tree_hint` is asserted by exact text), while the remaining wording is free (M4 and M5 survive; finding 276).

**`validate_project_dir` runs three refusals where one would do.** Bite M11 disables the existence check and M12 the `rev-parse --git-dir` check; both leave the suite green, and only the `rev-parse HEAD` check is pinned (M13 fails `test_validate_project_dir_no_commits`). A directory that is not a repository has no resolvable HEAD, so the middle check can never be the sole refusal, and the four units in `tests/test_session.sh` assert a non-zero return with stderr discarded, so none attributes the refusal to a check (finding 277, the shared-library face of the shadowed-refusal family at rows 209-214, 243-247, and 251).

**`draft_clear_stale_lock` fails open when its probe is missing.** The held-lock refusal is pinned (M8 inverts the test and fails all three lock units) and the removal is pinned (M7). The liveness probe is `lsof`, which no document lists as a host requirement; when `command -v lsof` fails, `LOCK_HELD` keeps its `false` default and the function deletes a lock a live git process may hold. Probe P1, with a PATH holding only `rm`, prints the removal warning, returns 0, and deletes the lock (finding 278). The outer existence guard is unpinned too: bite M9 changes it to `if true` and the no-lock path prints a spurious removal warning with the suite green, because `test_clear_stale_lock_no_lock_file` asserts the return code only (finding 280). The unit named for the absent-probe path never reaches it: part (b) points PATH at the stub directory that already holds the failing `lsof`, so `command -v lsof` still succeeds, and bite M10 (fail closed when the probe is absent) leaves that unit green while failing the sibling removal unit (finding 279).

**Coverage placement.** The four units in `tests/test_guards.sh` all drive `draft_clear_stale_lock`; `validate_project_dir`'s four units live in `tests/test_session.sh`, and `require_clean_working_tree`'s three-valued unit is `test_clean_tree_guard_is_three_valued` in `tests/test_session_save_guard.sh`, whose Covers list names only the save-policy functions and not the guard it sources (finding 281, joining the parser-in-a-consumer's-suite family at rows 73, 147, 164, and 172). That unit's BDD block names `session_save_needed` in its When line while the body calls `require_clean_working_tree` (finding 282).

| unit | Given / When / Then | what it is supposed to test | bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_clear_stale_lock_no_lock_file` | a committed repo with no lock / the function runs / rc 0 | the no-op path; the unit asserts the verdict only, so a spurious removal warning is unpinned | survived (M9) |
| `test_clear_stale_lock_removes_stale_lock` | a committed repo with an unheld lock / the function runs / rc 0 and the file is gone | stale-lock detection and deletion | proven (M7, M8, M10) |
| `test_clear_stale_lock_no_lsof_skips_check` | a failing `lsof` stub, and a PATH with only the stub dir plus an `rm` symlink / the function runs / rc 0 and the lock is gone | a failing probe is not a holder, and an absent probe falls through to removal | part (a) proven by M7 and M8; part (b) vacuous, because the stub dir still holds `lsof` (M10 survives) |
| `test_clear_stale_lock_held_lock_fails_and_keeps_file` | a stub `lsof` that reports a holder / the function runs / rc non-zero, the message names the holder, the file stays | the held-lock refusal | proven (M8) |

Bites (14 mutations, each against the full suite). Proven, 8: M1 the final clean/dirty test inverted, 33 failures; M2 rc 2 collapsed onto rc 1, three failures; M6 the unreadable hint replaced by the dirty hint, two failures; M7 the removal replaced by a no-op, two failures; M8 the held test inverted, three failures; M10 the absent-probe arm changed to hold, one failure (a second reported failure in `test_providers_pi_preflight.sh` passes standalone with the mutant applied and is a load flake); M13 the HEAD check disabled, one failure; M14 `-C "$dir"` dropped, 32 failures. Survived, 6: M3 the trace, M4 the first hint line deleted, M5 the unreadable hint reworded, M9 the outer existence guard removed, M11 the existence check disabled, M12 the git-dir check disabled.

### `scripts/lint.sh` and `scripts/check_markdown.sh` -- `tests/test_lint_umbrella.sh` (17 units)

49 and 64 lines: the umbrella static-check gate and one of its leaves. `lint.sh` declares its gate set as a hardcoded array of three, starts every gate before waiting on any, captures each gate's combined output to a temp file, prints the captures in array order, and returns a single verdict; `set -uo pipefail` without `-e` is what lets the wait loop observe the failures. The tree holds seven `check_*.sh` files: three run under the umbrella, three have their own Make targets, and `check_test_coverage.sh` documents itself as a non-gate helper.

**The gate set is a declaration surface restated by hand in three places, and one restatement is stale.** `GATES` names `check_shell.sh`, `check_lib_contract.sh`, and `check_markdown.sh`; the Makefile comment lists the same three; and `docs/adr/lint_gate_exit_codes.md` names two and calls them "the two gates", so the ADR predates `check_lib_contract.sh` joining the umbrella (finding 290). Membership is half-pinned: bite L1 deletes `check_lib_contract.sh` from the array and the suite stays green at 722/722, while deleting `check_markdown.sh` (L2) or `check_shell.sh` (L3) fails a named unit (finding 286).

**The umbrella's output contract has no unit.** Bite L5 replaces the per-gate `cat` with a no-op and the suite stays green, because every umbrella unit sends stdout and stderr to `/dev/null`; the same bite proves that the summary line - `Lint: clean in Ns across M gates`, the only place the umbrella states the gate count - is unread (finding 287). The verdict itself is pinned in both directions: L4 (`rc=1` to `rc=0`) and L6 (`exit "$rc"` to `: "$rc"`) each fail three units, and L7 (an unknown gate name) fails the clean-run unit, so an unresolvable gate fails closed.

**`check_markdown.sh` carries four unpinned halves.** Its verdict is `STATUS != 0 || COUNT > 0`; dropping the `COUNT` disjunct (M1) and corrupting the finding-count pattern (M6) both leave the suite green, so a tool that reported a finding and exited 0 would pass as Clean, and the printed count is unread (finding 288). Removing `cd "$REPO_ROOT"` (M5) leaves the suite green although the real gate's `**/*.md` glob would then resolve against the caller's directory (finding 289). The header says "all tracked Markdown files" while the glob lints the working tree: probe P1 put an untracked MD022-failing file at the repository root and the gate reported it at rc 1 (finding 291). The zero-lint guard and the linted-file parse are the well-pinned parts, in both directions (M2, M3, M7).

**The pass reported the liveness gate's mechanism, which I could not reproduce.** Its probe P2 captured 36 distinct false `DANGLING`/`UNREGISTERED` findings across 30 runs (13 runs with at least one) under the load of eight concurrent passes, and isolated the cause in the comparison at `check_test_liveness.sh` lines 47 and 55: under `set -o pipefail`, `printf '%s\n' "${REGISTERED[@]:-}" | grep -qxF "$fn"` yields a non-zero pipeline status when `grep -q` exits early on the match and `printf` then takes SIGPIPE, which the `if !` reads as not-found. On an idle host I measured 0 false negatives in 20,000 iterations of that construct with a three-element array, and 0 findings in 12 standalone gate runs. The mechanism is plausible and load-sensitive but unconfirmed, and the arrays the gate scans hold hundreds of entries rather than three, which multiplies the write and exit interleaving. Row 135 carries the evidence with that caveat.

| unit | Given / When / Then | what it is supposed to test | bite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_lint_passes_when_both_gates_pass` | both tool stubs pass / the umbrella runs / rc 0 | the clean verdict across all gates | proven (L7; L4 and L6 fail it) |
| `test_lint_reports_shell_failure` | the shellcheck stub prints 2 warnings, markdownlint passes / the umbrella runs / rc 1 | a failing gate maps to the verdict, not to the warning count | proven (L4, L3) |
| `test_lint_reports_markdown_failure` | shellcheck passes, markdownlint exits 1 / the umbrella runs / rc 1 | the leaf's non-zero exit reaches the umbrella | proven (L2, L4, L6) |
| `test_lint_reports_both_gate_failures` | both gates fail / the umbrella runs / rc 1 | the verdict stays boolean when every gate fails | proven (L4, L6) |
| `test_lint_runs_both_gates` | both gates fail / the umbrella runs / the markdown stub's log exists | a later gate still runs after an earlier one fails | proven (L2) |
| the 12 leaf-gate units (`check_shell.sh`, `check_markdown.sh`) | one refusal or one parse case each | the leaf gates' own fail-closed branches | proven for M2, M3, M4, M7; the eight `check_shell.sh` units are read-assessed in this pass, because the leaf belongs to the gates pass |

Bites (14 mutations, each against the full suite). Proven, 9: **L2** the Markdown gate dropped from `GATES` (two units); **L3** the shell gate dropped (one); **L4** `rc=1` to `rc=0` (three); **L6** `exit "$rc"` to `: "$rc"` (three); **L7** a bogus gate name (one); **M2** the zero-lint guard disabled (one); **M3** the singular `1 file` form rejected (one); **M4** the tool-absent exit turned into success (one); **M7** the linted-file parse anchored to `file$` (one). Survived, 5: **L1** the third gate dropped (`check_lib_contract.sh`), **L5** the forwarding removed, **M1** the `COUNT` disjunct dropped, **M5** the run directory removed, **M6** the count pattern corrupted.

### `scripts/dry_run_capability.sh` -- `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh` (11 units, 4 exercise this probe)

215 lines, the capability layer's bearer self-check. It runs as the sandbox container's `command:` override in the dry-run overlay, runs every check without `set -e` or `set -u`, and writes a per-container diagnostics record that orchestration validates. The header declares six readiness layers in order and claims the probe owns "the workspace_mounts ro/rw semantics the container preflight deliberately leaves out".

**Four checks cannot do the job they claim.** The `export_path` warn check (lines 97 to 99) calls a shell function inside `bash -c`; a child bash inherits no functions, production sets no `BASH_ENV` (the string appears nowhere in `src/` or `scripts/`), and `tests/stubs/libs/bash_env.sh` exists to re-source the libraries into those subshells, naming this check in its own comment - so the check can only pass under the test harness (finding 292, probe P1). The two `mktemp` guards at lines 74 and 110 call `_fail` and continue with an empty variable; probe P2 with an invalid `TMPDIR` produced two failures for one precondition and a raw `mkdir ... '/sandbox': Permission denied`, because the fixture path collapses to `/sandbox` (finding 293). `INPUT_DIR readable` runs `test -d`, so a mode-000 directory passes (finding 296, probe P4), and the header's read-only claim is unasserted. And `dry_run_write_record`'s failure path ends in a `WARN` print, so it returns 0: probe P3 with an unwritable `OUTPUT_DIR` exited 0 with no record, after which the orchestration waits out its record deadline and reports a timeout (finding 297).

**The cross-container marker is left behind and is pinned on neither side.** The probe writes `$CHANGES_DIR/.dryrun_capability_marker` and removes it only when the readback fails, so a clean run leaves it in the operator's session-diffs channel (finding 294, probe P5). Bites B8 (rename the marker) and B9 (rewrite both `CAPABILITY_LAYER_OK` literals) both survive at 722/722, because the probe test writes its own copy of the marker for the reasoning fixture, so neither half of the handshake is checked against the other side (finding 295).

**The record's layer list has a seventh name the header does not mention.** The record and the emitted section add `sandbox_init` after `agent_runtime`, and the header's six-layer list omits it (finding 298). Its three `sandbox_init.*` fields have no consumer, and bite B7 removes it from the record list with the suite green, which is row 17's finding in its third instance rather than a separate one; that evidence is on row 17.

What is well pinned: the healthy path, the per-layer failure isolation (B1 to B6 fail named units), the record filename and comparison literals, the shared `init_sha_is_valid` (which peels to a commit object rather than accepting hex), and the two session-export verdicts (B13).

Bites (14 mutations, each against the full suite). Proven, 8: **B1** the identity critical disabled, **B2** the `session_state` section renamed so its layer key is unmapped, **B3** the diff-export failure branch disabled, **B4** the marker write-failure branch disabled, **B5** the marker readback literal changed, **B6** the record filename renamed, **B12** the `sandbox_init` `.git` guard disabled, **B13** the session-export runs-assertion forced to failure. Survived, 6: **B7** `sandbox_init` removed from the layer list, **B8** the marker renamed, **B9** both marker literals rewritten, **B10** the input check pointed at a nonexistent path, **B11** `_is_writable` replaced by `true`, **B14** the `dry_run_summary` call replaced by `:`. Probes P1 to P5 as described above. The eleven probe units already carry their BDD blocks in the working tree, so this pass wrote none back.

### `scripts/dry_run_reasoning.sh` -- `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh` (5 reasoning units of the file's 11)

189 lines, the reasoning container's bearer self-check and the counterpart to the capability probe. Same shape: no `set -e`, no `set -u`, `set -o pipefail`, six layers named in the header, and a record plus summary at the end.

**One claim in the fan-out brief was wrong, and the correction is the finding.** The brief had this probe reading "a path the capability probe never writes". It derives the marker path as `$SANDBOX_DIR/../workspace/session-diffs/.dryrun_capability_marker`, while the capability probe writes `$CHANGES_DIR/.dryrun_capability_marker`; under the shipped layout those are the same directory, because the compose service sets `CHANGES_DIR=/home/agentuser/workspace/session-diffs` and `SANDBOX_DIR_NAME=sandbox`, so `SANDBOX_DIR` is `/home/agentuser/sandbox` and the derived path resolves to the changes directory. The check therefore passes by coincidence of two paths rather than by contract, and it breaks silently if either one moves. What survives is the fail-open half: both branches that do not find the expected content call `_warn` and never `_fail`, so a missing or wrong marker leaves `layer.container_network=PASS` and `status=PASS` (finding 299, probe P1 on a fixture whose two paths differ, bite B2 surviving). The healthy unit reproduces the production coincidence, which is why no unit catches it.

**The preamble is duplicated and one helper is a verbatim copy.** Both probes open with the same library defaults, source the same two libraries, resolve the same four paths, and run the same `dirs_resolve` fallback; this file then redefines `_is_readonly` over the identical function in `src/libs/dry_run_harness.sh`. Bite B1 deletes the local helper and B7 disables the fallback, and both leave the suite green, so neither is pinned (finding 300).

**The provider map is a second registry, and it fails open.** `_agent_binary_for_provider` hardcodes `pi`, `hermes`, and `opencode`, duplicating `src/reasoning/providers/`, and an unknown id becomes `_warn` rather than `_fail`, so a new provider silently downgrades the readiness check. `AGENT_CMD` is set nowhere in production, so the map is the only path to a binary name; bite B3 empties the map and survives because every fixture exports `AGENT_CMD` (finding 301).

**Two guards cannot fail.** `_running_as_bash_script` is a tautology: a script fed to the agent as input never executes, the entrypoint runs `"$@"`, and the overlay sets `command: ["bash", "/dry_run_reasoning.sh"]`, so the interface is enforced outside the probe; bite B4 stubs the function and the suite stays green (finding 302, observe). And the file-presence critical and `check_init_sha_readable` both refuse an absent file, so neither is individually observable (bites B8 and B10 each survive); that is the overlapping-guards shape of the workflow families, in the probe (finding 303).

**The record's identity verdict is weaker than its peer's.** This probe asserts only that `init_sha` is *readable*; the capability probe asserts that it is a valid commit object. Probe P2 with a forty-zero `init_sha` therefore yields rc 0 with `layer.session_state=PASS` and `status=PASS` here, while the capability probe exits 1 on the same input. The two records carry one layer name with two meanings, which is row 17's finding in its fourth instance, and that evidence is on row 17.

Bites (11 mutations, each against the full suite). Proven, 3: **B5** the mount-target check replaced by `return 0`, **B6** the `command -v` clause removed from the agent check, **B11** the `session_state` section key renamed so the layer verdict goes unmapped. Survived, 8: **B1** the local `_is_readonly` deleted, **B2** the marker path rewritten, **B3** the provider map emptied, **B4** the invocation guard stubbed, **B7** the `dirs_resolve` fallback disabled, **B8** the readable check stubbed, **B9** `check_session_ts` forced to fail, **B10** the file-presence critical disabled. Probes P1 (marker at `CHANGES_DIR`, probe warns, rc 0, `status=PASS`) and P2 (the forty-zero `init_sha`). All units already carry BDD blocks, so none were written back.

### `scripts/run_tests.sh` -- `tests/test_runner_selftest.sh` (18 `run_test` lines: 17 `test_*` units and one helper, `t_noop`)

271 lines, the runner that produced every number in this read-through. It discovers `tests/test_*.sh`, checks the docker stub once, runs the registration liveness gate before any test, dispatches every file through `xargs -P` into a `--worker` re-invocation of itself, aggregates the per-file `pass= fail= skip= rc=` records, and prints the summary line. `set -uo pipefail` with no `set -e`; failures are carried in variables.

**The runner's own accuracy is the finding of widest blast radius, and I reproduced it.** A file whose record says `fail=0` but whose exit status is non-zero is counted as a pass by the summary: probe P2, a fixture printing `UNIT: pass=5 fail=0 skip=0` and exiting 1, produced `5 tests across 1 files, 5 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped` at rc 1. And a file that exits 124 with a valid report has its report discarded, because the timeout branch is tested before the report is read: probe P4 gave `0 tests across 1 files, 0 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped` at rc 1. So a run can fail while the summary says nothing failed, which is what the earlier `532 tests ... 0 failed` and `592 tests ... 0 failed` runs at the default deadline were showing. Row 262 owns this defect.

**The deadline is not enforceable.** `run_with_deadline` polls, then sends `kill -TERM` and waits unconditionally, so a test that ignores SIGTERM blocks the run forever: probe P1 with `trap '' TERM; sleep 30` under `TEST_TIMEOUT=1` never returned (an external `timeout 8` had to kill it) and printed no summary. The only deadline unit uses a cooperative `sleep 30`. This is the mechanism behind row 285's leaked spinner.

**Untrusted input and undeclared trust.** The worker takes the last `UNIT:` line a file printed (probe P3: a file printing `99 units` is believed), and the zero-unit guard - the only thing failing a file that asserts nothing - is unpinned (B2). The output modes have no unit (B6, B7), the skip accounting is asserted against a substring the summary itself satisfies and the total omits skips (B10, B12), the malformed-record branch is unreachable (B13), and no unit checks which stream carries a diagnostic (B14). No subset selection exists, and the only selector also disables the gate (probe P5, finding 308).

| unit group | what it pins | bite |
|---|---|---|
| the nine runner verdict units (passing, failing, crash, missing report, no-assertion, two zombie shapes, multiple fails, fail-marker-trumps-rc0) | the failure classes and the exit aggregation | proven (B4 fails nine units; B11 two) |
| `test_runner_empty_discovery`, `test_runner_satisfied_prerequisite` | empty discovery warns and fails without a `set -u` crash | proven (B1) |
| `test_runner_broken_prerequisite` | the stub check refuses once, before discovery | proven (B5b) |
| `test_runner_deadline_expiry` | the cooperative timeout is reported by name | proven (B8, B3) |
| `test_runner_skip_is_warning`, `test_runner_aggregate_sums`, `test_runner_parallel_dispatch_integrity`, `test_runner_passing_file`, `test_runner_dead_registration` | the skip warning, the aggregate, parallel counting, and the gate's fixture | read-assessed or over-broad (B10 and B12 survive inside them) |

Bites (15 edits, 14 verdicts). Proven, 6: **B1** the discovery guard removed (two units); **B4** the final `exit 1` disabled (nine units); **B5b** the prerequisite `return 1` replaced by `:` (one); **B8** the rc-124 branch disabled (one); **B3** the aggregate's rc clause narrowed to `fail > 0` (one, through the timeout record only); **B11** the no-`UNIT` branch disabled (two). Survived, 8: **B2** the zero-unit guard, **B6** the `VERBOSE=1` line, **B7** the unknown-option refusal, **B9** `tail -1` to `head -1`, **B10** `+ TOTAL_SKIP`, **B12** the per-file skip warning, **B13** the malformed-record verdict, **B14** the diagnostic stream. One mutation (inverting the stub check) aborted the suite before any test ran and was replaced, so it carries no verdict. Probes P1 to P6 as described.

They are not per-file coverage gaps, and they need their own handover before any work starts. Rows 23-30 come from the `session_inventory.sh` read-through. Rows 86-93 come from the `src/build/image.sh` read-through, the first file of phase 3. Rows 94-100 come from the `src/build/compose.sh` read-through; row 96 is the second probe-confirmed partial-artifact case, and row 97 is the same vacuity shape as row 86. Rows 101-104 come from the `src/capability/git-hooks/pre-commit.sh` read-through, whose fail-open and partial-staging behaviours are ADR decisions rather than findings. Rows 105-112 come from the `src/capability/entrypoint.sh` read-through; row 105 is a hardcoded path that the suite passes only because this container carries the real file. Rows 113-115 come from the `src/capability/seed_volume.sh` read-through, whose fixtures are the strongest in phase 3 and whose guard units are the weakest assertions. Row 136 came from the follow-up measurement of row 130, and `scripts/agent-sandbox.sh`'s own pass later in phase 4 will cover the rest of that file. Rows 137-151 come from the `scripts/prune.sh` read-through, the fourth file of phase 4; row 137 is the tolerant-parse defect, row 145 names the docker stub rather than prune, and row 148 is helper-placement evidence for the CLI-to-workflow note. Rows 152-164 come from the `scripts/onboard.sh` read-through, the fifth file of phase 4; row 152 is the unreachable guard, row 162 is the second undeclared host requirement (with row 126), and row 163 is a property of the test file itself. Rows 165-173 come from the `scripts/start_agent.sh` read-through, the sixth file of phase 4; row 166 is the duplicated `--delivery` validation, row 172 is a test-family naming finding that also corrected this report's own inventory, and row 173 is the in-tree precedent for row 130's fix. Rows 174-186 come from the `scripts/run_agent.sh` read-through, the seventh file of phase 4, whose units are split across `tests/test_run_agent.sh` and the re-homed `tests/test_trace_start.sh`; row 174 is the second measured site of row 130 and joins the CLI-to-workflow cluster, rows 149 and 175 form the dead-condition pair, and rows 176 and 180 are the two cases where a comment's stated mechanism is not the one the code uses. Rows 187-198 come from the `scripts/resume_agent.sh` read-through, the eighth file of phase 4; row 187 is the unpinned record-to-identity provider recovery, row 188 is the second vacuous-abort unit in phase 4 and joins rows 171 and 181, and row 191 joins the fail-open cluster opened by row 137. Row 242 was raised by probe P1 rather than by a mutation: a command substitution placed in a draft's `.draft-state` ran in the operator's shell.

| # | Finding | File | Class | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The `AGENT_SANDBOX_SANDBOX_DIR` env-var level is unexercised; renaming it leaves the suite green (bite-proven). | `env_resolve.sh` | coverage gap | candidate follow-up: one unit that sets the env var for the sandbox path |
| 2 | `sandbox_dir_canon` has no unit; corrupting its output leaves the suite green (bite-proven). `test_checkpoint.sh` only asserts the function is declared, not what it returns. | `common.sh` | coverage gap | candidate follow-up: a `sandbox_dir_canon` unit covering absolute, relative, `~`, and symlink spellings |
| 3 | **Test-file organisation.** Three related gaps. (a) No dedicated test file: `dry_run_harness.sh` is covered only indirectly through the probe test (its counter arithmetic, summary branches, and record-write failure path have no direct unit, and a mutation to the writer's WARN text leaves the suite green), and `session_state.sh`'s surface is split across `test_session.sh`, `test_reasoner_container_contract.sh`, and `test_dry_run_probe.sh`, and `session_hints.sh` has no dedicated file at all (its five assertions live in the two trace suites). (b) Two files mix subjects: `test_common_lib.sh` also tests `tests/libs/test_common.sh` helpers, and `test_interface_contract.sh` also tests `scripts/build.sh`'s `_check_interface_contract`, and `tests/test_routing.sh` also holds five units that exercise `session_save_policy.sh`'s autosave cycle (`test_autosave_swap_sequence`, `_tick_absorbs_status_under_real_set_e`, `_loop_survives_failing_ticks`, `_cycle_refuses_unreadable_repo_end_to_end`, `test_entrypoint_autosave_call_arguments`) - a reader looking for autosave coverage would look in the file named for the save policy, not the one named for routing. A third naming drift sits in the diff suites: `tests/test_apply_count.sh` is named for a metric rather than a subject, and its eight units split between the apply driver (phase 4) and `apply_and_commit` (one unit, finding 13's neighbourhood), while `tests/test_binary_roundtrip.sh` re-covers `strip_index_lines` alongside `tests/test_diff_helpers.sh`'s four units for the same filter. The same drift reaches the export suites: `tests/test_diff_export.sh` holds five `_write_export_status` units whose subject is `export_status.sh`, and `tests/test_diff_dispatch.sh` holds five `test_session_path_*` units that are integration tests over `export_path` and `diff_export` together. (c) `tests/test_session.sh`'s header names `libs/session.sh`, which does not exist, and lists `session_state_write` as covered when it has no unit (finding 22). (d) Near-duplicate units accumulate where a file is extended in a second pass: `tests/test_routing.sh` carries six units for three behaviours under two naming conventions (finding 52). `tests/test_session_env.sh` carries nine more units for other libs: seven drive `env_load` through phase 1 (finding 73) and two exercise `ENV_REL`. `resume_list.sh` is worse still: it has no unit anywhere, not even indirect (finding 79). | `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh`, `tests/test_session.sh`, `tests/test_common_lib.sh`, `tests/test_interface_contract.sh` | test organisation | candidate: one dedicated file per lib, split the mixed files, and correct the stale Covers list |
| 4 | **Dead code.** `_self_env_dir` in `env.sh` is assigned and never used (ShellCheck does not flag it because the file is sourced, so a top-level variable may be external). `env_resolve_one`'s `explicit` parameter and its `if [[ -n "$explicit" ]]` branch are dead: every caller passes `""`, and explicit values are handled in `env_resolve_identity`; disabling the branch leaves the suite green. `parse_base_flags` in `common.sh` has no caller: the leaves parse identity through `cli.sh`'s `parse_args` spec and the dispatcher uses `parse_args_collect`, yet the header still lists the function and two units in `test_common_lib.sh` cover it. `_resume_state_cell`'s second parameter `CREATION_TS` is never read either (probe-verified: the caller passes it and the body ignores it). | `env.sh`, `env_resolve.sh`, `common.sh`, `resume_list.sh` | dead code | candidate: remove all four with their stale header text and units, or document each as a kept public API |
| 5 | No unit asserts that a comment line is warning-free; the comment-guard mutation survived. The comment skip is redundant with the invalid-key warning for `# c`. | `env.sh` | coverage gap | note; a "no stderr" assertion would pin it |
| 6 | No unit asserts that a loaded value is exported to a child process; the `export`-to-assigning-builtin mutation survived (same-shell reads only). | `env.sh` | coverage gap | note; a child-process read would pin it |
| 7 | **Env and identity resolution policy.** One coherent piece with three parts. (a) Precedence: `env_resolve_identity` re-reads the `.env` once per resolved identity field (up to three loads per invocation, each a subshell fork), and the session path then loads the same file again in full; a single ascending-precedence overlay (load the `.env` once, apply `AGENT_SANDBOX_*`, then the explicit flags, later writer wins) reconstitutes the same state in constant reads, and the `.env` reader joins the KV family (finding 22). (b) Load scope: the dispatcher exports the identity triple and `ENV_FILE` but never calls `env_load`, so the rest of the `.env` (`MAKEFILE_VERSION`, `INSTALL_DIR`, `SERVE_PORT`, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL`, provider stubs) loads only on the session path; loading the full `.env` at the dispatcher closes the layer split, at the cost of exporting every key to every subcommand. (c) Namespace and double delivery: override inputs are namespaced while the exported values are generic and unprefixed, which clashes with other tools and grows riskier under (b); identity is also delivered twice on the dispatcher path, by export and by forwarded `--name=`/`--project=`/`--sandbox=` flags, with `check_base_flags` re-validating what the dispatcher already resolved. | `env_resolve.sh`, `agent-sandbox.sh`, `common.sh`, cross-cutting | design (precedence, scope, namespace) | one handover with a propagation checklist; must move the Makefile template, compose files, entrypoints, scripts, and tests together |
| 8 | **Test units weaker than their stated intent.** `test_changes_dir_name_is_leaf` documents the subpath doubling rather than preventing it: its name and comment claim leaf enforcement the code does not implement, and adding a slash rejection breaks only that test. `test_verify_passes_record_within_container` is vacuous against its comment: it writes an all-PASS record and never exercises the "layer FAIL with status PASS" mixing case the comment names. `test_session_state_read_malformed` duplicates `test_session_state_read_missing_key`: both assert empty output for a file with no matching key, and no mutation distinguishes them. Two more units in the package-branch suite share the pattern: `test_preflight_bypass_returns_before_any_git` claims a nonexistent directory proves the short-circuit, but removing the bypass leaves it green because a git command against a missing directory also produces silence (bite B4); and `test_preflight_skips_deleted_files_without_warning` stays green when the skip is removed, because both checks come out quiet for a deleted path anyway (bite B6). | `tests/test_dirs.sh`, `tests/test_dry_run_record.sh`, `tests/test_session.sh` | test looseness | candidate: rename or enforce the leaf case, rewrite the mixed-record unit, and give the malformed case an assertion the reader can fail |
| 9 | `_source_lib` has no unit; its missing-file path and its source path are unexercised. | `dirs.sh` | coverage gap | candidate follow-up: a `_source_lib` unit |
| 10 | **`lib_preflight` and directory-name defaults.** `lib_preflight` compares severity to exactly `CRITICAL`, so any other value including a misspelling is treated as WARN and a typo'd severity silently downgrades a critical check; no test covers an unknown severity. The baked-library inventory and its severities are hand-written literals in two entrypoints (`src/capability/entrypoint.sh`, `src/reasoning/entrypoint.sh`) with no manifest: the Dockerfile bakes 21 files, the two lists classify 11 of them, they already differ, and a new container-reachable lib must be added by hand to both or a missing file yields a raw source error instead of the stale-image message. The same single-source-of-truth gap affects `SANDBOX_DIR_NAME`'s default `sandbox`, re-defaulted with `:-sandbox` in the entrypoints, dry-run scripts, compose, and knowledge tests. | `dirs.sh`, `entrypoint.sh` (both), cross-cutting | robustness / single-source-of-truth gap | candidate: reject an unknown severity, derive the inventory from the baked set against one manifest with per-layer severity, and route the directory-name default through one helper |

Because the value is unescaped, a field value executes as shell code. Rows 38 and 242 are that finding seen from the two reader families - 38 from the `draft_state.sh` chain probe (`_write_export_status` through `exported-at`), 242 from a hand-built record run through `confirm_run` - and the two reader call sites are `confirm.sh:64` and `reject.sh:32`, with `tests/test_draft_state.sh:197` evaling the output as well. Both are one fix away from closed and neither waits on the pair. Observed consequences. (a) Correctness: an append-only write plus a first-match read makes a re-write a silent no-op, so the capability entrypoint's `session_id`/`session_ts`/`host_head_sha` upgrade path and its `changes_dir`/`input_dir`/`output_dir` refresh cannot change what `package_branch.sh` and `routing.sh` read. (b) Writer robustness: the `.export-status` writer's `mktemp` fallback and atomic temp-plus-rename are unasserted, though `test_diff_export.sh`'s header claims atomicity. (c) Redundancy: `session_state_read`'s missing-file guard only silences stderr; it does not change the result. (d) Coverage: `session_state_write` and `session_state_write_set` have no unit at all. Cost is not the motive - an eight-line record read three times per invocation costs about 60 microseconds per read. (b) `add -N` and `add` produce the same `git diff HEAD` bytes for a new file, so the distinction the naming carries is unobservable through the diff. | 63 | **Untracked inclusion is a hand-rolled substitute for a git-native facility, and it mutates the sandbox's contended index.** The module stages untracked files (`git add -N`), writes the diff, then restores the index. Git reaches the same result without touching the shared index: point `GIT_INDEX_FILE` at a scratch file, run `git add -A` against it, and diff the scratch index against the baseline. A probe on a fixture with a deletion, a modification, an untracked file, a CRLF file, trailing-whitespace content, and a gitignored file produced byte-identical output (415 bytes both ways), excluded the ignored file natively (`add -A` honours `.gitignore`, which also retires the `--exclude-standard` listing), left the real index untouched, and created no `.git/index.lock`. `wait_git_lockfile` exists in `diff_export.sh` and `capability/entrypoint.sh` because the sandbox index is contended - and `add -N` mutates exactly that index while the agent may be running. The scratch index takes no lock, which removes the interference and the destructive failure mode of finding 57, and deletes two functions, the `_DIFF_STAGED_UNTRACKED` global, and the per-file add loop. Timing on 300 untracked files: 407 ms current against 64 ms scratch index. One caveat: `add -A` applies clean filters, so a repository carrying `.gitattributes` text conversion could produce different bytes than a worktree read; this repository has no `.gitattributes`. Measured lock taxonomy on a probe repository: with `.git/index.lock` held, every reader we use returns 0 with the correct verdict (`status --porcelain`, `diff --stat`, `ls-files -m`, `ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, `rev-parse`, `rev-list`, `for-each-ref`, `merge-base`, `cat-file`, `log`, `show-ref`), while every writer fails with rc 128 (`add -A`, `commit`, `checkout -b`, `apply --index`, each printing "a git process may have crashed in this repository earlier: remove the file manually to continue"). The scratch index staged the full change set with rc 0 while `.git/index.lock` was held and left that file untouched, and its artifact was byte-identical to the `add -N` form (252 bytes both ways). Two read-side measurements complete the picture: `git status --porcelain` writes the index when its stat cache is stale (the index mtime moves after a touch), and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` leaves the index untouched with the verdict unchanged. So our own guards create the file while merely reading, which is the mechanism behind the false-lock class. | `diff.sh` | unification opportunity | design candidate - see [`20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md`](20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md) |
| 64 | **Two implementations of the verbatim-diff contract.** `_write_git_diff` calls itself the single source of truth for it, and inside `diff.sh` that holds. `package_branch.sh:148` writes per-commit patches with its own copy of the same two post-processing steps: `strip_index_lines` followed by `awk '{print} END{print ""}'`, against `_write_git_diff`'s `sed -e '$a\'`, and with no empty fast path. The two trailing-newline mechanisms also disagree on empty input, where `awk` prints a newline and `sed` prints nothing, so an empty commit's patch is a one-byte file while the writers' empty case is a zero-byte file. `diff_is_empty` accepts both. | `diff.sh`, `package_branch.sh` | duplication / unification opportunity | design candidate - give `_write_git_diff` an options parameter and delete the copy, see the design note |
| 65 | **The two artifact writers cannot carry binary content while the per-commit patches can.** `--binary` appears only in `package_branch`'s `GIT_DIFF_OPTS` (l.141-143). So `all-changes.diff`, documented as the net delta since the baseline, and `uncommitted.diff` silently omit a binary change that `patches/` carries - an operator who applies `all-changes.diff` on the host misses it. The current behaviour is frozen by a test that constructs its own diffs with `git diff` and asserts the default one cannot apply (`test_default_diff_has_no_applyable_binary_payload`), so no writer change invalidates it, but the test also records the gap as expected rather than deciding it. | `diff.sh`, `package_branch.sh`, `tests/test_binary_roundtrip.sh` | capability gap / open decision | decide together with finding 64; the design note recommends `--binary` on both artifact writers |
| 66 | **Two dependencies the module does not need.** `diff.sh` sources `routing.sh` and references nothing from it - no `resolve_*`, no `export_path`, no channel symbol appears in the file. It sources `session_state.sh` for one call: `write_all_changes_diff`'s `init_sha` default, used when the third argument is omitted. In production that default is dead: the only production caller, `package_branch.sh:347`, always passes the baseline, because `package_branch.sh:292` resolved it once and threads it to all four writers (`package_commits`, both diff writers, `write_changed_files`). The two-argument form and its `init_sha not found` branch are exercised only by `tests/test_diff_helpers.sh`. Removing both makes `diff.sh` a standalone patch library over git and coreutils, and deletes a duplicate of the baseline check that `package_branch.sh:294` already performs with a better diagnostic. Removing the argument at the call site confirms the default is load-bearing there: passing an empty baseline to `write_all_changes_diff` leaves all three package-branch suites green (bite B17), because the writer's own `init_sha` fallback silently supplies it. | `diff.sh` | dependency / unification opportunity | design candidate - part of the same pass, see the design note |
| 67 | **The module holds four layers, and two of them are policy for other modules.** Patch plumbing (`strip_index_lines`, `diff_is_empty`, `_apply_patch_file`) and artifact generation (the three writers) belong here. `write_changed_files` decides what a bundle contains and has one caller, `package_branch`; `apply_and_commit` encodes the empty-member commit rule plus the author and message contract, and belongs with the apply drivers. Neither needs to sit beside the byte-level filter. The counter-argument: 319 lines is not large, and the same evaluation's other changes touch every function already. | `diff.sh`, `package_branch.sh`, `scripts/workflows/apply.sh`, `scripts/workflows/draft.sh` | module boundary | candidate: move `write_changed_files` to `package_branch.sh` and `apply_and_commit` to the apply side; deferred, not part of the design note's scope |
| 68 | **The export reports SUCCESS for a package_branch that failed internally.** `diff_export` calls `package_branch ... \|\| { ... }`, and bash suspends `set -e` for the whole body of a function invoked in a `\|\|` list. Failure detection inside `package_branch` is therefore explicit-guard-only, and its guards cover one condition: the preflight refuses an unreadable repository. Any other failing command is invisible. Probed with a truncated blob object, under the production shell (`set -euo pipefail`): `git diff` fails per-commit with `error: object file ... is empty`, the per-commit patch lands as a one-byte file, `changed-files/` still receives its copy, `.export-status` reads `STATUS=SUCCESS`, the call returns 0, and no error log is written. The two lines of git error on the console are the only trace, and the operator's `make draft` would land a message-bearing empty commit. The pipeline form compounds it: without `pipefail` the diff pipeline's exit status is `awk`'s, so the same failure is masked even outside the `\|\|` context. | `diff_export.sh`, `package_branch.sh` | robustness gap | design candidate - merged into the pipeline note (option area 5): a per-step status check inside `package_branch`, plus a call form that does not suppress `set -e`, and a unit that forces a mid-package git failure and asserts a FAIL status |
| 69 | **The stderr capture file is written into the directory the callee wipes.** `diff_export` creates its capture with `mktemp "${OUTPUT_DIR}/.package_branch.stderr.XXXXXXXXXX"` before calling `package_branch`, which then runs `rm -rf "$OUTPUT_DIR"` and `mkdir -p "$OUTPUT_DIR"` (`package_branch.sh:337`). The file is unlinked while `tee` still holds the descriptor, so the capture is written into nothing and the failure-path check `[[ -f "$_pb_stderr" ]]` finds no file. Any failure after the wipe produces an error log with no STDERR section - exactly the case where the diagnostic matters. The existing failure units pass because the preflight fails before the wipe. The capture's location (E1) and the success-path cleanup (E2) are both unpinned, so a stray capture file could also ship inside a bundle. | `diff_export.sh`, `package_branch.sh` | latent defect / coverage gap | design candidate - merged into the pipeline note (option area 5): keep the packager's overwrite, move the capture out of `OUTPUT_DIR`, and assert both that a post-wipe failure keeps its stderr and that no capture file survives in a bundle |
| 70 | **`wait_git_lockfile`'s wait semantics are unpinned, and the header presents it as a feature of `diff_export`.** Cutting the poll budget to one fifth (`_timeout * 5` to `_timeout * 1`) leaves the suite green (E10), so a caller requesting three seconds may wait 0.6 s and receive nothing but the timeout message; only the outcome is pinned (rc 1 plus the message). The early return for an absent lockfile is result-redundant, because the loop body already cannot run (E9). The header lists lockfile polling among the module's reliability features, but `diff_export` never calls it: it is a helper the caller must invoke, and `scripts/dry_run_capability.sh` calls `diff_export` without it, so the protection is opt-in per call site. | `diff_export.sh`, `capability/entrypoint.sh`, `scripts/dry_run_capability.sh` | docstring drift / coverage gap | design candidate - merged into the pipeline note (option area 5): retire the wait with finding 63, since the scratch index removes the writer contention it guards; document the caller obligation until then, and pin the budget with a unit that counts polls |
| 71 | **Minor unpinned items in the export path.** The `SUMMARY=` line of the error log can be dropped with the suite green (E14). The `\|\| true` on the `init_sha` read is result-redundant, because `session_state_read` already returns empty on a missing record (E8). The `.export-status` write on success is the function's last statement, so it is the return value of `diff_export`; that is load-bearing but unasserted as such. | `diff_export.sh`, `tests/test_diff_export.sh` | coverage gap | notes; fold the `SUMMARY` line into the existing error-detail unit |

| 80 | **The docker verb vocabulary mismatch drops the age for every running session.** `_resume_state_map` stores `running` or `stopped`; `_resume_state_cell` compares that value against `last_verb`, which is `started` or `stopped`. `running` never equals `started`, so when docker responds the override always fires and the cell prints a bare verb with no timestamp: probe 8 reads `log=started docker=running -> running`, while the same session with docker absent reads `started 5m ago`. The docstring's promise that the time stays from the log when it matches the verb is unreachable for a live container, which is the common case in the interactive list. Prefer that helper over repeating a case at every call site." `docs/adr/lint_gate_exit_codes.md` applies the same verdict principle to the gates. So a help request is a success, the process must exit 0, and six call sites currently repeat the case rule 3.2 warns against - with every instance dead. | 135 | **A liveness-gate abort was observed once and did not reproduce.** During the S4 mutation the suite aborted with `ERROR: test liveness gate failed` instead of running the units; the gate scans only `tests/test_*.sh` and `tests/stubs/libs/*.sh`, so the mutation in `scripts/stop.sh` cannot have caused it. Repeated runs of the same mutation fail a unit normally (721 passed, 1 failed), and `check_test_liveness.sh` reports 0 findings standalone, so the observation is unreproduced. The gate's own findings were lost because the capture took only the summary line. The gate is blocking, so one unreproduced abort is worth watching rather than acting on. Five further passes reproduced it independently - the `lint.sh` pass, the `dry_run_capability.sh` pass, the `dry_run_reasoning.sh` pass, the `check_*.sh` gates pass, and the `run_tests.sh` pass - on byte-identical input, producing false `UNREGISTERED` and `DANGLING` findings at random and aborting the whole suite with zero test results. The Makefile pass saw the false-positive form first: the `start` `INTERACTIVE_FLAG` bite (M11) exited non-zero through the gate and reported PROVEN, and two re-runs gave 722/722. A bite script that reads the exit code alone cannot tell a non-assertion abort from a caught mutant; the failing file's name is the evidence. On an idle tree the gate is clean: five standalone runs on the shared checkout gave `0 finding(s)` each. Every reproduction happened while eight suites ran concurrently, so the trigger is concurrent load, and the bite method must run one suite at a time; part of that load was leaked spin-loop processes from earlier suite runs, recorded as row 285. The `lint.sh` pass reports a mechanism and a measurement: `printf '%s\n' "${REGISTERED[@]:-}" \| grep -qxF "$fn"` under `set -o pipefail` yields a non-zero pipeline status when `grep -q` exits early on the match and `printf` then takes SIGPIPE, which the `if !` reads as not-found; it captured 36 distinct false findings across 30 runs, 13 of them with at least one, under eight-way load. I could not reproduce either half on an idle host: 0 false negatives in 20,000 iterations of that construct with a three-element array, and 0 findings in 12 standalone runs of the gate. The mechanism is plausible and load-sensitive but unconfirmed, and the arrays the gate scans hold hundreds of entries rather than three, which multiplies the write and exit interleaving. The `dry_run_capability.sh` pass adds ten runs on a pristine copy under the same eight-way load: five produced no findings and five produced one to four, naming a different unrelated test file each time. The `run_tests.sh` pass supplies the captured output row 135 asked for: in two loops of 40 gate runs it emitted a phantom finding in 26 and 31 runs, each on a randomly chosen file whose registration is present, for example `UNREGISTERED: tests/test_package_branch.sh: test_dispatcher_export_status_contents() is defined but never registered via run_test` while that function is defined at line 306 and registered at line 520. It isolated the same `pipefail` sensitivity as the `lint.sh` pass (`{ printf 'foo\n'; sleep 0.2; printf 'bar\n'; } \| grep -qxF foo` returns 141) while its own 1444 replicated membership checks passed three rounds clean, so the trigger remains unconfirmed. It also records a second harm: when `RUN_TESTS_DIR` is set the gate is skipped entirely, so a subset run loses the check. | `scripts/run_tests.sh`, `scripts/check_test_liveness.sh` | harness reliability | fix (code): make the gate deterministic or stop it blocking the run; until then every bite verdict is unreliable |

When the flag occupies the subcommand slot the scan never sees it, so `agent-sandbox --help` falls through to the dispatch case's `*)` branch, prints `Unknown subcommand: --help` to stdout with the subcommand list, and exits 1. The comment above the scan states the intent that `--help` works uniformly for every subcommand. Under the shipped layout those resolve to the same directory (`SANDBOX_DIR=/home/agentuser/sandbox` and `CHANGES_DIR=/home/agentuser/workspace/session-diffs`), so the check passes by construction rather than by contract and breaks silently if either path moves. ## Findings triage

Each finding gets one sector and one action. The action vocabulary has three buckets: **note** (the row is an input to a design decision, and the note lands before anyone implements it), **docs** (a documentation edit alone closes it), and **fix** (a code change or a pinning unit, with no design decision outstanding). A sector's note collects its rows; the row stays the evidence.

| Sector | Rows | Artifact it needs |
|---|---|---|
| A - configuration, state and argument surface | 58 | design note (the largest surface: 165 env vars, 13 CLI call sites, 20 Make translators, 5 declaration surfaces) |
| B - persisted record formats and their semantics | 17 | design note (4+ shapes, 9 readers, one host-eval consequence) |
| C - failure signalling and verdicts | 54 | design note (small, and A and B both need its principle) |
| D - diff pipeline | 15 | the existing note, [`20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md`](20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md) |
| F - build-layer contracts | 42 | design note (labels, readiness, digest, compose, the prune partition, and the onboarded tree) |
| H - operator guidance text | 23 | fold into A, or a small note |
| I - test-suite organisation and coverage conventions | 75 | policy doc; this report is its evidence base |
| J - propagation, naming and module boundaries | 16 | policy line for the sweep gate; the module boundary goes to the CLI-to-workflow note |
| G - host portability and the bash floor | 4 | docs edit plus the requirement probes |
| no sector | 8 | plain docs edit, or accepted as is |
