### Proposed order

Counts in this section are read from the findings data file, [`20260924-design-active-test_suite_readthrough.jsonl`](20260924-design-active-test_suite_readthrough.jsonl), and never restated by hand.

1. **Note C, failure signalling.** Sector C holds 54 rows, of which 36 are unresolved, and both larger notes need its principle settled: what a three-valued verdict means, whether unknown fails closed, and whether a primitive may alias "the tool could not run" to "there is nothing to report". Finding 84 (the state guard's narrow scope) sits here and in the pipeline note's area 5, and the prune group (rows 139, 143, 144, and 150) is the newest instance.
2. **The CLI-to-workflow boundary note.** Already drafted as [`20260924-design-draft-cli_workflow_interface.md`](20260924-design-draft-cli_workflow_interface.md). It consolidates rows 48 (the dispatcher arm), 130, and 136, and records four boundary issues found while writing it. It needs only a confirmation pass, and row 130's fix is decided already by rule 3.2 of `bash-coding-conventions.md`.
3. **Note A, configuration, state and argument surface.** Sector A holds 58 rows and is the largest surface, with 45 unresolved. Its resolution half is opened as [`20260924-design-draft-resolver_contract.md`](20260924-design-draft-resolver_contract.md); its declaration and transport half (rows 41 through 48) has no note yet, and the Makefile arm of row 48 stays with the parser doc. The five-surface table and the 19-row probe table in this report are its evidence base.
4. **Note B, persisted record formats.** Sector B holds 17 rows, with 12 unresolved, and is the only sector with a security consequence (findings 38 and 242, one finding from two sides). The eval removal does not wait on the decision - the parsers can assign a whitelisted set of names directly - so it can land as a fix at any time. The structured-format decision (finding 20) rides here.
5. **Note F, build-layer contracts.** Sector F holds 42 rows, with 36 unresolved, and is no longer the small sector it was: phases 3 and 4 added the image, compose, hook, entrypoint, seeder, snapshot, stop, build, prune, and onboarded-tree contracts. It is now the second-largest sector and a candidate for splitting along the build-layer and lifecycle lines.
6. **Note I, test-suite organisation.** Sector I holds 75 rows, with 61 unresolved. A policy doc produced when this read-through closes, with the report as its evidence.
7. **The fixes.** The 244 rows marked fix need no decision. They are cheap, and each has a unit attached, so they can be picked up in file order at any time. Three do not fit that pattern. Row 137 is a safety fix and should not wait for the batch: a typo on `--dry-run` prunes for real. The path-guard unification (see the Consolidation map) spans several files: rows 152, 153, and 165 are its three visible faces, and it is worth one change rather than three. The help-surface item is the same shape at a larger scale: rows 130, 136, and 174 are its three faces across twelve call sites, and one library-side conversion closes eleven of the twelve dead copies.
8. **The sweep gate.** One line in the writing rules: record the convention before a repo-wide sweep. The history holds three ASCII sweeps for one convention, two moves of the same directory, and two renames of one variable.

What this triage does not do is reopen anything decided. Rows 14, 15, and 62 stay accepted as is, and finding 67 stays deferred. It also does not schedule the fixes; they need a handover of their own when someone starts them.

## Consolidation map

Rows that record the same finding stay separate rows, because a row number is a stable identifier: the design notes, the sections above, and this report's own cross-references cite rows by number, and 221 such references now span these three documents. Consolidation happens by grouping, and the group is the unit of work. A row joins a group only when the finding recurs, not when the class recurs - ordinary coverage-gap rows whose subject differs stay individual.

| Cluster | Rows | The one finding | Where it is worked |
|---|---|---|---|
| Leaf-layer coverage gaps | 1, 2, 5, 6, 9, 215, 216, 217, 220, 224, 225, 227, 230, 231, 232, 241, 245, 248, 250, 252 | behaviours in `env.sh`, `env_resolve.sh`, `dirs.sh`, and `common.sh` that have no unit at all | fix (test), one unit each; sector A |
| Parser units in a consumer's suite | 73, 147, 164, 172, 281 | a shared module's units, or a family's units, live with a file that is not their subject | note I; sector I |
| `session_env.sh` unpinned items | 74, 75, 76 | phase-1 behaviours and exports that no unit reads | fix (test); sector A |
| `package_branch.sh` unpinned items | 82, 85 | the `.msg` artefacts and the minor unpinned behaviours in the bundle dispatcher | fix (test); the `.msg` choice is the pipeline note's area 6 |
| Artifact contract and stage boundaries | 57-61, 63-66, 68-71, 83, 84 | the contract is duplicated or absent, and the stage boundaries leave shared state unowned | the pipeline note. Rows 58, 59, 62, and 67 stay in the findings table: two are docstring-and-unit items, one is a benign set, and one is deferred |
| Resolution and declaration | 7, 22, 38-48, 72, 78, 206 | the ladder is bypassed where it exists and improvised where it does not | the resolver note, with declaration named as its sibling |
| Post-move and post-removal reference drift | 12, 92, 116, 258 | header paths, file names, and directory descriptions that a move or a removal did not update | fix (docs); sector J |
| CLI to workflow boundary | 48, 200 | the dispatcher and the scripts it execs disagree about how a subcommand is found and how it is called | the CLI-to-workflow interface note; the Makefile arm of row 48 stays with the parser doc; its help-surface rows 130, 136, and 174 moved to the help item in this table |
| The `--help` path across the CLI | 130, 136, 174, 199, 201, 204 | help is not a success path at any layer: the leaf parser prints the usage and returns 2, every caller's conversion of that status is dead under `set -e`, the dispatcher reads its own `--help` as a subcommand, and one leaf prints another command's usage because it defines no `usage` of its own | fix work: the conversion belongs in `cli.sh`, so one change closes eleven of the twelve dead copies - `stop.sh:53`, `prune.sh:240`, `resume_agent.sh:81`, `run_agent.sh:94`, `package_branch.sh:380`, `build.sh:296`, `onboard.sh:416`, `apply.sh:192`, `confirm.sh:179`, `draft.sh:573`, `reject.sh:84` - and `start_agent.sh:274` keeps its dead copy even though its help already exits 0 through `parse_help_flag` (the working pattern at row 173); `run_agent.sh` additionally needs a local `usage` (174) and the dispatcher an arm for the flag in the subcommand slot (136); rule 3.2 of `docs/development/bash-coding-conventions.md` decides that one helper owns the conversion, the boundary shape is area 5 of the CLI-to-workflow note, and the entry goes into `docs/adr/command_flag_parsing.md` |
| Fail-open argument and failure handling | 137, 139, 140, 143, 144, 150, 168, 191 | a mistyped argument or a failed step silently removes more than the operator asked for, or removes it while reporting success | fix rows; 137 is decided by `cli.sh`'s own drop-mode note, and 139's declaration side is already in `install.sh` |
| **Path guard unification across the host leaves** | 152, 153, 165 | the path-shape guard is written twice, with two spellings (`^[A-Za-z]:[/\\]` in `onboard.sh:103` and `^[A-Za-z]:\\` in `start_agent.sh:96`), the first resolving with `realpath` before it tests the value and the second never resolving, and no unit pins either absolute-path branch; one helper covers both spellings, the resolve order, the absolute-result requirement, and the existence policy | unify work: one helper in `src/libs/common.sh` beside `sandbox_dir_canon`, or a new `src/libs/paths.sh`, replacing `onboard.sh`'s `resolve_and_validate` and `start_agent.sh`'s `validate_wsl_path` and absorbing the five canonicalisation call sites at `stop.sh:60`, `prune.sh:246`, `resume_agent.sh:89`, `start_agent.sh:343`, and `session_env.sh:128`; the seam already exists, because `scripts/guards.sh` holds the git guards and `common.sh` holds the sandbox primitive, and `validate_project_dir`'s existence, git, and commit steps are the same policy a fourth time |
| The prune partition invariant and its unpinned legs | 141, 142, 146 | the invariant is stated in the file header and three of its legs have no unit: the preview's disclosure, the unnameable resource, and the volume filter's scope | fix (test) for 141 and 142; note F for 146 |
| The onboarded tree's guarantees | 155, 156, 157, 158, 159 | onboarding states guarantees about the tree it produces - mode bits, a promoted env file, a provider hook - and delivers or documents none of them reliably | note F; fix rows attached |
| The confirmation gate | 160, 161 | the last gate before files are created has no unit on any of its branches, and the partial-state warning beside it has none either | fix (test) |
| Stated guarantees and live-observed workarounds with no unit | 176, 177, 178, 179, 180, 182, 183, 184, 185, 234, 253, 263, 265, 275 | the file's comments state a guarantee, a rationale, or a workaround that no unit observes, and in two cases (180, 184) the stated mechanism is not the one the code uses | fix and note rows; 185 joins the harness limitation list |
| Vacuous assertions | 171, 181, 188, 192, 213 | a unit's assertion is satisfied by a code path other than the one the unit names, so removing the named guard leaves the suite green | fix (test) rows |
| Dead conditions and dead parameters | 149, 175, 222, 226, 259, 312 | a condition or a `case` arm that the code above it makes unreachable, or a parameter that no caller populates | fix (code) rows: delete the arm or the parameter, or wire it |
| Overlapping and unobservable guards in apply_run | 209, 210, 211, 212, 214, 277 | the guards overlap, so no unit attributes a refusal to one of them: the required-argument check, the not-found check, and the project check refuse the same inputs, the count's default is unreachable, and the stale-lock clear has no observable effect on this path | one fixture per refusal, plus the entry-point unit of row 213 |
| Overlapping and unobservable guards in draft_run | 228, 229, 235, 236, 237, 238 | the same shape in the draft path: the two metadata refusals mask each other, the source, patches, and required-count refusals share one input, and the presence check is masked by the fixtures | one fixture per refusal; retire the replica oracle of row 223 |
| The test-side replica for draft orchestration | 223, 233 | the suite's orchestrator is a reimplementation whose behaviour differs from production, so the production guards are masked and the uncommitted-only path is unasserted | `tests/libs/draft_fixtures.sh`: delegate to `_run_draft_workflow`, or call it directly |
| Overlapping and unobservable guards in confirm_run | 243, 244, 246, 247 | the pre-work guards, the target check, the delete policy, and the merge flag are each unobservable because the units use valid repositories and reachable states | one fixture per refusal; decide the deletion policy once, in both paths |
| Overlapping and unobservable guards in reject_run | 251 | the fourth workflow repeats the shape: two pre-work guard verdicts and the already-absent branch arm have no unit | one fixture per pre-work guard |
| Workflow incoming-state invariants | 241, 253, 255, 272 | the diff workflows declare no incoming-state contract, so enforcement is improvised per file: `apply` and `draft` refuse a dirty tree (force tolerates the first, never the second), `reject` discards by accident of the checkout outcome, and `confirm` destroys tracked WIP with `reset --hard` while reporting the tree unchanged | the diff-invariants design note, [`20260925-design-draft-diff_workflow_invariants.md`](20260925-design-draft-diff_workflow_invariants.md), whose scope is the whole diff branch; rows 216 and 231 are the plumbing coverage of the same `--force` flags across `scripts/workflows/{apply,draft,confirm,reject}.sh`, `scripts/guards.sh`, `scripts/templates/Makefile.template` |
| The simple-record read/write family (KV) | 22, 38, 242 | the `KEY=VALUE` records have four readers and two writers with divergent duplicate-key rules, and the family's colon-delimited member (`.draft-state`) emits unescaped `KEY="VALUE"` for the caller to `eval`, so a field value is executed in the operator's shell | one `kv_read`/`kv_write` pair with a whitelisted, non-eval load; the eval removal is independent of the pair: `src/libs/session_state.sh`, `src/libs/export_status.sh`, `src/libs/dry_run_record.sh`, `src/libs/session_inventory.sh`, `src/libs/draft_state.sh`, `scripts/workflows/confirm.sh:64`, `scripts/workflows/reject.sh:32`, `tests/test_draft_state.sh:197` |
| The interactive picker's unasserted display and autosave surface | 260, 261 | the bundle table's column width, uncommitted marker, and AGE prefix, and the autosave channel's mtime order and newest-saved cell, all survive mutation | fix (test), one unit each; sector I |
| The interactive helper's interface and its five call sites | 148, 256, 257, 258, 259, 260, 261, 263, 264, 265, 266, 271 | a helper that returns one token per answer, or confirms a plan the leaf already built, where the operator's model is collect the answers and hand back a fully specified command; the EOF loop, the leaf vocabulary the helper owns, the drifted self-description, the unpinned display, the unasserted equivalence, and the printed command that is not the run | the interactive-helper design note, [`20260925-design-draft-interactive_command_contract.md`](20260925-design-draft-interactive_command_contract.md); rows 256-266 |
| The Makefile template's argument surface | 267, 268, 269, 270, 274 | the accepted-variable set is undeclared against the template's own convention, the misuse guards cover three names, the recipes are unpinned apart from two thin-CLI assertions, `.PHONY` is incomplete, and the draft channel carries two defaults | fix (code) and fix (test) rows; sectors A, F, and I |
| The test runner's contract as the instrument of the suite | 304, 305, 306, 307, 309, 310, 311 | the deadline cannot kill a test that ignores SIGTERM, the zero-unit guard is the only thing failing a file that asserts nothing, the report trust boundary is undeclared, the output modes have no unit, the skip arithmetic is unpinned, the record validation is unreachable, and no unit checks which stream carries a diagnostic | fix (code) and fix (test) rows; sectors C and I |
| The verification harness's gate set, runner contract, and their output | 286, 287, 288, 289, 308 | the umbrella's membership is hardcoded and half-pinned, its forwarding and summary are unread, one leaf's finding count and run directory are unasserted, the ADR that governs the exit codes is one gate behind, and the runner has no subset selection while the only selector silently disables the gate | fix (test) rows plus the docs fix at 290; the harness has no design note by policy, so this is a policy-doc item |
| The dry-run probes' check mechanics and their shared preamble | 292, 293, 296, 297, 300, 303 | checks that cannot do the job they claim: a warn check that can never pass outside the harness, two precondition guards that continue past their own failure, an existence test standing in for a readability test, a record write whose failure leaves the verdict alone and becomes an orchestration timeout, plus a preamble and a helper implemented twice and two overlapping criticals | fix (code) and fix (test) rows; sectors A, C, and J |
| The dry-run cross-container marker's contract and lifetime | 294, 295, 299 | the handshake file is left behind on the success path, its name and value are unpinned against the process that reads it, and the reader derives its path from `SANDBOX_DIR` instead of using `CHANGES_DIR` while both branches that would catch a mismatch only warn | note, fix (test), and fix (code) rows |

No row is merged by relabelling. A group that later needs a single entry gets one in the document that works it - a design-note section, or a policy doc - not here.

## Design notes

| Note | Status | Scope | Open decisions |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md`](20260924-design-draft-diff_pipeline_unification.md) | draft | the export pipeline: the artifact contract, the scratch index, the artifact set, the stage boundaries, and the per-commit artifact names | the `.gitattributes` caveat; whether a silent binary omission is acceptable; the state guard's scope (area 5); whether the per-commit writer moves (area 6, Option C) |
| [`20260924-design-draft-resolver_contract.md`](20260924-design-draft-resolver_contract.md) | draft | setting resolution: the ladder, the leaf contract, the `.env` dual role, the levels, the failure contract, and the output surface | whether Option B across areas 2, 4, 5, and 6 becomes the recommendation; the `.env` dual role (area 3) stays deliberately open |
| [`20260924-design-draft-cli_workflow_interface.md`](20260924-design-draft-cli_workflow_interface.md) | draft | the boundary between the dispatcher and the scripts it execs: name-to-implementation resolution, the handoff form, workflow-side helper resolution, who resolves identity, and the verdict across the boundary | all five areas carry a recommendation; the table, the uniform handoff, the entry helper, and the boundary verdict await confirmation, and the router-versus-parser split with `command_flag_parsing.md` is stated in the scope. The `agent-sandbox.sh` pass added rows 199-206 and 208 to the note as evidence and corrected its `--env` claim: the forward set follows each leaf's parse spec, so the interface hands `--env` to the two leaves that accept it and to no workflow. Rows 199, 200, 201, 203, 204, and 206 are the note's own evidence, not separate work. |
| [`20260925-design-draft-diff_workflow_invariants.md`](20260925-design-draft-diff_workflow_invariants.md) | draft | the state contract and operator-facing guarantees of the whole diff branch: what `apply`, `draft`, `confirm`, and `reject` accept and leave behind, what is recoverable, the bundle's guarantees, and the per-command interface promises | seven option areas, each with a recommendation: the header `INVARIANT` form, the per-workflow enforcement policy, the acknowledgement spelling (`FORCE=1` with its two meanings stated), the warning's recovery sentence, the forced discard's breadth, `confirm`'s rescue, and the document series' shape. The first write covers the whole branch, so later passes add to it rather than opening a sibling. |
| [`20260925-design-draft-interactive_command_contract.md`](20260925-design-draft-interactive_command_contract.md) | draft | the contract between a leaf and the interactive helper: the return type (an argv for the leaf's own parser), the helper's layer, vocabulary ownership, convergence before any side effect, the non-tty and EOF policy, the derived equivalent-command display, and the uniform `--interactive` surface | eight option areas, each with a recommendation: an argv return, a move to `src/libs/`, the vocabulary with the leaf, a converged path, fail closed on a non-tty, a derived display line, one convergence unit per leaf, and the flag on all five leaves. It answers the operator's framing of 2026-09-25: the model is the target, and the tree realises three other shapes. |
| [`20260925-design-draft-git_boundary.md`](20260925-design-draft-git_boundary.md) | draft | where repository knowledge lives: the two parts allowed to run git, the interface between them, the lock rule, and recorded identity in place of the read-side git calls | all three option areas carry a recommendation; the six violator conversions and the retirement of the wait, the probe, and the delete await the operator's decision |

All six are draft, and none is an ADR. Each settles into an ADR when the operator decides: `docs/adr/diff_packaging.md`, `docs/adr/env_resolution.md`, and `docs/adr/command_flag_parsing.md` respectively, each of which takes a dated entry rather than a rewrite, and the diff-invariants note into a new `docs/adr/diff_workflow_state_contract.md` (name to confirm at settlement), and the interactive-contract note into a new `docs/adr/interactive_command_contract.md` (name to confirm at settlement), with its flag-parse half in `docs/adr/command_flag_parsing.md`; and the git-boundary note into a new `docs/adr/git_boundary.md` (name to confirm at settlement), whose lock rule is the pipeline note's option area 5 made authoritative. The CLI-to-workflow note settles there only while its principles govern the parser's surface; a router principle with wider reach would need its own ADR. The invariant note is the first of a series: one note per capability branch, each settling into its own ADR, indexed from a line in `system_overview.md`'s Core Invariants.

The version marker's value is unasserted (M7 survives) and the marker has no consumer. M11 exposed the flaky liveness gate (row 135) as a false-positive source, now recorded there.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/guards.sh` (88 lines, 5 functions, 4 units in `tests/test_guards.sh`, 14 bites):** findings 275-283. The three-valued verdict is pinned hard (M1 fails 33 units, M2 three, M14 32) while its required silence is unpinned (M3); the hint wording is unpinned apart from one line (M4, M5); `validate_project_dir` runs three refusals where the middle one can never be the sole refusal and two of the three leave the suite green (M11, M12); `draft_clear_stale_lock` fails open when `lsof` is absent (probe P1) and its absent-probe unit never reaches that branch because the stub directory still holds the failing `lsof` (M10); the no-lock unit asserts the return code only (M9); `tests/test_guards.sh` covers one of the five functions while the guard's own unit sits in the save-guard suite with a Covers list that omits it; that unit's BDD block names the wrong function; and the header names a non-consumer and omits two real ones.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/lint.sh` (49) and `scripts/check_markdown.sh` (64):** findings 286-291. The umbrella's gate set is hardcoded and restated in three places, one of them a stale ADR, and membership is half-pinned (L1 survives, L2 and L3 fail); the forwarding of gate output and the summary line have no unit (L5 survives); the Markdown gate's `COUNT` disjunct, its finding-count pattern, and its run directory are all unasserted (M1, M6, M5 survive); and its header claims tracked files while the glob lints the working tree. The pass also reported a mechanism for row 135's liveness flake (`grep -q` closing the pipe on a match, `printf` taking SIGPIPE under `pipefail`) with 36 false findings across 30 runs under load; I could not reproduce it on an idle host (0 false negatives in 20,000 isolated iterations, 0 findings in 12 standalone runs), so row 135 records it as plausible but unconfirmed.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/dry_run_capability.sh` (215 lines, 11 units in `tests/test_dry_run_probe.sh`, 14 bites):** findings 292-298. Four checks cannot do what they claim - a warn check that needs `BASH_ENV` to pass at all, two `mktemp` guards that continue past their own failure, `test -d` standing in for a readability test, and a record write whose failure returns 0 and becomes an orchestration timeout. The cross-container marker is left behind on the success path and neither its name nor its value is pinned against the reading probe. The record's layer list carries a seventh name the header does not mention, which is row 17's finding in its third instance. Eight of fourteen bites prove; the six survivors are the layer list, the two marker literals, the input check, the writability stub, and the summary call.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/dry_run_reasoning.sh` (189 lines, 5 reasoning units, 11 bites):** findings 299-303. Its cross-container marker check passes by a coincidence of two paths (`$SANDBOX_DIR/../workspace/session-diffs` happens to equal `CHANGES_DIR` under the shipped layout) and both mismatch branches only warn, so a real link-up failure leaves `status=PASS`; the brief's claim that it reads a path nobody writes is disproved by the compose values. The probe preamble is duplicated from the capability probe and `_is_readonly` is a verbatim copy of the harness's; the provider map duplicates the provider registry and fails open on an unknown id; `_running_as_bash_script` is a tautology the suite cannot distinguish from a stub; and the two `session_state` criticals overlap so neither is observable. Its record asserts only that `init_sha` is readable, so a forty-zero value yields `layer.session_state=PASS` here while the capability probe fails on it - row 17's fourth instance.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/run_tests.sh` (271 lines, 18 `run_test` lines in the self-test, 14 bite verdicts):** findings 304-312. This is the instrument every other verdict in the read-through passed through, and its own accuracy is the widest-radius result: a file whose report says `fail=0` but whose exit status is non-zero is summarised as a pass (probe P2: `5 tests ... 0 failed` at rc 1, reproduced here), and a rc-124 file with a valid report is discarded (probe P4: `0 tests ...`). `run_with_deadline` cannot enforce its deadline against a test that ignores SIGTERM (probe P1 hung the runner until an external kill), which is the mechanism behind row 285's leaked spinner. The zero-unit guard, the output modes, the skip arithmetic, the unreachable record validation, and the diagnostic stream are all unpinned; the report trust boundary is undeclared; and there is no subset selection while the only selector silently disables the gate (probe P5). Rows 135 and 262 gained this pass's captured evidence rather than new numbers. The shell read-through is now complete.
- **Sixth note, the git boundary:** `devlog/discussions/20260925-design-draft-git_boundary.md` draws the seam. Two parts may run git: the diff pipeline (container and host sides under one contract) and project delivery (mount and copy). Delivery records the replica's identity and every other module reads that record; only the pipeline may leave a deliberate index lock, and only through `patch_apply` and `draft_branch_create`; every read sets `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so the harness stops manufacturing the lock it cannot attribute. The note carries the measured taxonomy, the six violator modules, and the reason the wait, the `lsof` probe, and the automatic delete retire together.
- **Phase 4, `scripts/workflows/interactive.sh` (476 lines, 4 functions, 32 units, 14 bites):** the shared picker library, sourced by five callers. The `-printf` misreport consequence is added to row 51 and the reachability fact to row 148. The picker's core pagination is well pinned (I3, I9 prove). BDD blocks written back into all 32 units.

Two results stand out: the subcommand list's content is unasserted, so the declared set can drift from the dispatch case silently (200), and the per-branch identity subsets are unpinned in three directions at once (206), which is the evidence the note's area 4 needed. - **Phase 4, tenth file:** `scripts/workflows/apply.sh` read, 30 units read (22 in `tests/test_diff_workflow.sh`, 8 in `tests/test_apply_count.sh`), 26 bites run (8 proven, 18 survived), five probes, findings 209-220 recorded. The pass's structural result is 213: every unit calls `apply_run` inside a command substitution whose failure is handled by `|| RC=$?`, which suppresses errexit in the subshell, so the `|| true` that protects the count report is invisible to the suite - probe P6 shows the entry point aborting with rc 1 and a truncated report once it is removed. The guards overlap (209, 210, 211, 214), so each refusal is individually unattributable; and the whole `--interactive` branch is unpinned (215). BDD blocks written into both test files. The pass also recorded a recurrence on the 2026-09-25 Bash feedback entry: the first probe sweep's mutations no-op'd because perl interpolates shell variables inside `\Q...\E`, so probe scripts now pass literals through the environment and keep the backup comparison.
- **Boundary-note evidence:** the same pass produced row 219 for the CLI-to-workflow note's area 4: the interface forwards `--sandbox` to a leaf that requires it and never reads it, so the per-branch flag sets are not only unobservable (206) but can carry hollow requirements.
- **Phase 4, eleventh file:** `scripts/workflows/draft.sh` read, 30 units read (all in `tests/test_draft_workflow.sh`, plus one draft unit in `tests/test_apply_count.sh`), 40 bites run (15 proven, 25 survived), five probes, findings 221-241 recorded. Four structural results: the suite's oracle for the orchestration is a test-side replica (`_test_draft_run`) that diverges from `_run_draft_workflow` in three ways, so the production guards behind it are masked (223); no unit drives `draft.sh` as a script, so `main` is unexercised (224); the usage advertises `--strict`, a flag the parser rejects and the conventions doc records as deliberately removed (221); and the pre-collected patch list is read from argument 9 while both callers pass it at 8, so it is always empty (222, probe P2). The savepoint reset is asserted by nothing, and probe P4 shows the rollback abandoning the branch switch when the tag is missing - the state its docstring forbids (234). BDD blocks written into `tests/test_draft_workflow.sh` (30 of 30).
- **Phase 4, twelfth file:** `scripts/workflows/confirm.sh` read, 12 units read (all in `tests/test_confirm_workflow.sh`), 25 bites run (12 proven, 13 survived), two probes, findings 242-250 recorded. The pass re-demonstrated the read-through's most serious finding, the phase-2 row 38, from the consumer side and gave it its own row, 242: probe P1 got a command substitution in a `.draft-state` field to execute in the operator's shell. Row 242 joins row 38 and the KV family item (row 22) across four files. The pass also found the lesson draft.sh has not learned - confirm.sh's comment names both hazards of the fixed-name savepoint tag and holds its target in a local variable instead (supporting row 234) - and two confirm units that still pin the removal of the `confirm-savepoint` tag design (248). BDD blocks written into `tests/test_confirm_workflow.sh` (12 of 12).
- **Phase 4, thirteenth file:** `scripts/workflows/reject.sh` read, 4 units read (all in `tests/test_reject_workflow.sh`), 14 bites run (4 proven, 10 survived), five probes, findings 251-254 recorded. The pass's sharpest result is 253: the residue discard the comment describes is not the one the code performs, so an untracked residue file survives a normal reject, and the unit that claims to pin the discard supplies tracked residue only. Three observations are recurrences recorded on their canonical rows instead of as new rows: the unread `--sandbox` (219, third instance, probe P1), the verdict the caller can drop (243, bite R3), and the unasserted operator messages (249, bites R5/R10/R11). The `eval` at `reject.sh:32` is the KV item's second reader call site (probe P4), and the help arm is row 130's dead conversion, confirmed here with an instrumented mutant (probes P2/P5/P6). BDD blocks written into `tests/test_reject_workflow.sh` (4 of 4). Follow-on from finding 253: the direction is set as an `INVARIANT` preamble per diff workflow, in `prune.sh`'s form, with a fail-closed acknowledgement gate where a dirty tree is a legitimate input; probing that direction showed `confirm` is the worse half, since its `reset --hard` destroys tracked WIP on both failure paths while reporting the tree unchanged (row 255, probes P9/P10).
- Phase 4 continues with the remaining workflows (confirm, reject, interactive) and the Makefile template.
